## CHAPTER XLIII.

->*Of the flaming fire.*<-

Since there is a need for large and small fires in mine-works, which must be learned and applied according to the various sorts of metals—and not merely according to the manner of their various meltings and refinings—the condition of such necessary fires must also be known. To set these down in detail would require great effort, and the writing of it would grow into a large volume; it is the duty of knowledgeable melters and refiners to order and regulate their fires according to what each metallic condition requires, to further and not to hinder their work. Therefore, I leave these to their further and serious thoughts, so they may take these matters into fuller consideration.

## CHAPTER XLIV.

->*Of <term>Ignis torrens</term>, or of the roasting fire.*<-

Things tending toward ashes, soot, and the waste of metals, and the <term>exuviums</term> <note>the outer coverings or husks</note> or hulls of bodies, melters believe may be removed safely in a roasting or calcining fire. They build a large wood fire beneath them to roast or calcine the metal, so that, as they suppose, they retain nothing

<summary>The author emphasizes that practical experience is essential in mining and metallurgy because written instructions cannot cover every detail. He introduces the necessity of regulating different types of fires, such as flaming and roasting fires, according to the specific requirements of various metals and the removal of impurities.</summary>
<keywords>metallurgy, mine-works, experimental knowledge, fire management, Ignis torrens, roasting fire, calcining, metal refining, Valentinus Basilius</keywords>

--- Page 103 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the roasting and calcination of metals, contrasting the use of intense fire with more gentle annealing processes.</meta>

nothing of it, or of such waste you heard of just now; they yield their <term>exuvium</term>, and copper yields cinders and flakes. But if it is suddenly quenched, it then rubs and consumes iron; therefore, annealing is more commendable, as they do at *Mansfield*: a great heap of ore is laid together, which they kindle and let stand in a gentle glowing heat to burn away that which should be removed in 그at glowing. Metals in *Sweden* are annealed in this way by the heat of the sun in summer; there the metal runs together finely and purges itself so neatly that it would be refined if it stayed its full time in that heat. I approve of this annealing more than of calcining in a fire's flame. There is a twofold glowing fire, and metals require a twofold glowing or annealing: one sort is used at *Mansfield*, where they kindle heaps of ore with bundles of straw, letting them glow of their own accord like a heap of coals, and the ore is annealed, which is the purpose of the process. Secondly, annealing is also good for stony bodies, reducing them into <term>calxes</term> <note>powdery oxides remaining after heating</note>; but those who made metallic calxes in an enclosed heat or glowing fire obtained only the calxes of the bodies' <term>exuviums</term> <note>outer husks or waste</note>. Therefore, neither they nor others have any cause to wonder if they achieve no success in that way.

---

### CHAPTER XLV.
### *Of the corroding fire.*

This fire should be categorized among the coal fires, being of a consuming nature, and its corrosiveness exists in the cold fire. It has the same qualities as a burning fire: it shines and burns. Its burning is corrosive, and in that regard it is better than the other because it does not burn the substance to ashes, but instead reduces the bodies to a dust or sand—which would be laborious if it had to be done by filing—it should

<meta>catchword: should</meta>

<summary>The author advocates for gentle annealing over harsh calcining for purifying ores, citing examples from Mansfield and Sweden. He also introduces the "corroding fire," a chemical process that reduces metals to dust more efficiently than mechanical filing.</summary>

<keywords>metallurgy, annealing, calcining, Mansfield, Sweden, ore, calx, exuvium, corroding fire, chemical reduction</keywords>

--- Page 104 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the transformation of metals through fire, moving from the corroding fire to the glowing fire and the lamp-fire.</meta>

should be brought to stars. The next neighbor to this fire is the glowing fire, of which I will give only a hint.

## CHAPTER XLVI.

### *Of <term>Ignis candens</term>, or of the glowing fire.*

This fire is purposely applied to metallic bodies; it consumes them, since their matter is naturally inclined toward it. This fire is of great importance, making their bodies very malleable; their <term>exuviums</term> <note>outer waste or husks</note> stay on the float, and it is the best quality they have, that in that glowing heat they cast off the part that must be gone, and the good part remains. Things nowadays are slighted; the world supposes it has skill enough and wants no further knowledge. *Quot capita tot sensus* <note>As many opinions as there are heads</note>; everyone thinks his own wit is best, though some have scarcely begun to know any of these things, which is the reason why men are still kept in their ignorance. Men may suppose that by this corrosive water I mean an <term>Aqua Fortis</term>; it is no such matter. How many tons of precious <term>Aqua Fortis</term> are used in vain at *Goslar* in the *Harz*, which would serve for better use, and the expenses laid out for wood might have been saved.

## CHAPTER XLVII.

### *Of <term>Ignis incubans</term>, or of the lamp-fire.*

This fire serves when metals are worked openly and not <term>luted</term> <note>sealed with airtight clay or paste</note> in; then the metal does not fly away as dust, nor does its best part run away; for you heard that a flaming

<summary>The author describes the "glowing fire" used to refine metals by removing waste while maintaining malleability, criticizing the wasteful use of Aqua Fortis at Goslar. He also introduces the "lamp-fire" used for open metalwork to prevent the loss of material as dust.</summary>

<keywords>Ignis candens, glowing fire, metallurgy, exuviums, Aqua Fortis, Goslar, Harz, Ignis incubans, lamp-fire, luting</keywords>

*his last Will and Testament* 75

flaming fire is hurtful for working metals. Lamp-dishes are commonly made of glass, set in an earthen pan filled with ashes or sand, and kept in a state of sweating; in that sweat, many suppose the metal receives its body, or that one changes into the other. I leave this transmutation to its own merit and cannot approve of it. Concerning this warmth, I cannot disprove it nor find fault with it, and indeed all metals should be dealt with in this manner.

These two fires—of ores and the lamp-fire—if they were used in medicinal ways, would do better than the calcining or flaming fire can do; in cases where those are of no use, the long fire must be kept consistently at an equal heat if any good is to be done. Some kept the lamp-fire in a stove-furnace, where everything was spoiled during the work; it was either too hot or too cold. It lacked a consistent heat, as the final product showed, because it was not well controlled.

---

## CHAPTER XLVIII.

->*Of the cold fire.*<-

This is a strange fire; little can be said of it to those who cannot conceive of it. Whether it was not noticed, or whether they despaired of it, I do not know. This is what is elsewhere called coagulating; it cannot consume the other fire. It can melt the work, but to consume it is impossible. It works in the air as well as in the fire, where it shows its efficacy and is the sole proof of its fusibility. Metallic mercury is of a cold fusion; all other fusions are hot. If you do not believe it, feel it. The fixation of the warm flux is called coagulation; there, the one opposes the other; one congeals, while the other remains in a liquid state. This difference must be known

<meta>catchword: by</meta>

<summary>The author discusses the use of lamp-fires for transmutation and medicine, warning against the inconsistent heat of stove-furnaces, and introduces "cold fire" as the process of coagulation seen in metallic mercury.</summary>

<keywords>lamp-fire, transmutation, ores, stove-furnace, cold fire, coagulation, metallic mercury, cold fusion, metallurgy</keywords>

--- Page 106 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the cold fire, specifically its role in the melting and fluxing of metals, completing the sentence from the previous page.</meta>

by those who are employed in the melting of metals and their fluxes.

It is of importance to control this fire well, or how stones are to be weighed; things that are excessively cold are a death to a tempered body. As for what animals live in either too cold or too hot a fire—to speak precisely of life is as impossible as to speak truly of God. Therefore, do not stare at definitions of what human reason is able to conceive. Philosophy is strangely conditioned, and it appears by this fire also that a thing which is very cold may contain a life nonetheless.

When it is in its highest degree of ascension, it then comes down again and turns to silver, then to copper. If the lower hot fire does not do it, then surely the cold fire must, for it dissolves again into its mercury, which is the flux of the cold fire. If it lays hold of it, it must then run to all bodies; in its running, it puts off not only the lowest but the highest body also. Take this into further consideration.

## CHAP. XLIX.
### *Of the warm fire.*

I have spoken of this already; it can be made and controlled in several ways. Coals, wood, pitch, oil, and other combustible things are fit for it. Here I would only speak of a heat that is good for the flux of metals, by which they are purged, as you heard above; it is needless to repeat it here.

This concludes this first part, where I have informed you about the lower work, or preparatory work, governed and observed by nature, by which she presents metals and minerals to us—

## CHAP. II.

### *Of the general operations of various metals.*

Almighty God, for his eternal honor and glory, has presented to mankind innumerable wondrous works, which he, as the sole Mediator and Creator, has set forth in natural things. He has shown the same also in his omnipotence underground, in metals and minerals. From them we may learn, as the twelve Sibyls prophesied of the bright, true, and only Son of Righteousness and Truth, in whom rest—corresponding to the twelve ports and gates of Heaven, and to the twelve months, movable and immovable—visible and invisible bodies: the seven Archangels standing before the Throne of God; after these the seven planets—Sun, Moon, Mars, Jupiter, Venus, Mercury, and Saturn—and the rest of the stars; and the seven metallic ores in their properties, such as gold, silver, copper, iron, tin, lead, and mercury; then vitriol, antimony, sulfur, bismuth, cobalt (or brass ore), alum, salt, and other mineral growths.

So that the true center may be understood and conceived, God has made the first separation according to his word: The Spirit of the Lord moved upon the water. The whole elemental body of the earth has been water, but the Spirit of the Lord <term>Sabaoth</term> <note>Zebaoth</note> has divided it and fashioned

the earth from the muddiness of the water, and in it all metallic fruits that were ever created and generated underground; all these were first water and may be reduced again into water. All other creatures, whether they are animals, vegetables, or minerals, are produced from the first water: the various kinds of beasts, fishes, and sea monsters, following the Lord's spirit and following the first eternal breathing Essence, which brought forth and shaped things colored and uncolored, soft and hard, small and great creatures, according to the twelve stones in the breastplate of *Aaron*. He created man after his own image; the Holy Spirit was infused into *Adam*, who had a fullness of eternal wisdom, and that according to the order of *Melchizedek*. Almighty God, who is the first and the last, the first principle and end of all things, has set his gifts into times and hours, days and years, which according to his eternal decree have their revolutions. He has blessed through his most holy means *Abraham*, *Isaac*, *Jacob*, *Aaron*, *Melchizedek*, and others he has infinitely blessed, according to his good will and pleasure from eternity, putting various periods to them. And in his unsearchable decree and will, he has also laid the foundations for minerals and metals as a help for supporting men in their necessities in this miserable life. Thus has he improved and exalted the earth in her goodness; men have reason to return hearty thanks to the Creator for it.

God in his gracious providence, next to the knowledge of himself and of his holy Word, can bestow no better gift to man than to endow him with the true knowledge of metals and minerals. Jews thought themselves wise men in this; but just as some miners know little of minerals and metals, so the Jews knew little of their Messiah and God's word in its true sense. Therefore, from that blessed and promised country, the knowledge of precious stones, minerals, and metals has come to us, as if by an inheritance; being the last, we have become the first, and they the last. But in the end, Heaven's gate will be opened to them again; internal and external gifts and means will be bestowed on

### CHAP. III.

->*Of the Stones, rocks, and flints of Gold, its operation, condition, and striking courses.*<-

Gold is wrought in its proper rocks and marbles, and in the purest <term>matrix</term> <note>womb or environment of formation</note> of the firmest earth, from a most perfect salt, Sulphur, and Mercury, purged of all dregs and impure spirits, with the conjunction of a natural, highly clarified Heaven, of white, yellow, and red sulfurous earth, after the fiery nature of <term>Sol</term> <note>the Sun</note>, in a deep fixation; so much so that no other metal has a higher, more compact, and more heavy body of a golden matter, in which there is no humidity. All the elements are equally bound up within it, which in their unity have wrought such a fixed body, coloring the same throughout with an everlasting citrine color, with the deepest tie and uniting of its pure earth, Sulphur, and Mercury; and with its <term>Vitriol essence</term> it does everything that the Sun performs among the stars. Naturally, everything is gold that clings to it on all sides; and it is found in the best and closest stones and passages, and the power of <term>Sol</term> works purely upon that

Gold ores are naturally formed thus, so that the gold stands in them close, compact, firm, and good, which is found sometimes in the cross passages. Its fixedness is found in the depth underground, where it has its greatest power, and it is also found sometimes in a speckled <term>jaspis</term> <note>jasper</note>, full of eyes, and mixed with flints in its passages, where many times <term>vitriol flint</term> is found abundantly, which vitriol is the best among all other sorts of vitriols. ## CHAP. IV.

->*Of silver ore, of its mine, operation, condition, and striking passages.*<-

Silver ore is wrought in its own stone, of a perfect nature and most noble earth, and of a fixed clear sulfur, salt, and mercury, which with a mixture does join in a fixed and firm uniting, and appears of a degree lower than the gold is, and is the best metal next to gold. In the refining of it it loses very little, and is separated *per se* <note>by itself</note>, or with other metals joined in the fire. Its natural fitted stone causes the silver ore, following its heavenly influence and the nocturnal influences of the Moon. In Northern parts the most silver passages are found; for as *Luna* borrows her light from *Sol*, even so the silver passages and silver stones have at their right side gold passages, and with that noble queen *Lunaria* is compared a root, whereby the gold passages acquire strength, and get the more power in that mixture, and

Ancient philosophers wrote strangely of her virtues, being a fertile yoke-fellow of *Sol* <note>the sun or gold</note>, which may be applied to the upper and lower metallic work, because nothing is as fixed, next to gold, as silver is in its perfection; this is the reason why silver passages are accompanied with white fluxes and mineral veins. Next to those are such passages in which are generated red mineral sulfur and red-yellow juices of the noble gold.

Silver metallic ore is often wrought in a red-goldenness and comes forth better than the other; a proof of this may be had, if it is well ordered. White gold ore is naturally tinged this way by white copper glass, which causes such ores and passages because of the nourishment of their perfect minerals. With the glass ore, black fumes are exhaled and feed upon <term>Wismuth</term> <note>bismuth</note>, lead, and tin ore, wherein minerals that strike near the <term>Lunar passages</term> <note>veins of silver</note> are greedily refreshed. Thus grows the firmest and most compact silver ore from its pure, proper, and unmixed stone, improving the bad places and instruments. Silver ore has many remarkable virtues next to gold from the heavenly influences, changing several sorts of silver stones and descending from the origins of their highest finished unity.

They also carry and produce not only mixed chambers and mine chests, but also several hard and sturdy mixed ores in whole flint-works, and other copper flowers, yellow and black ore. They are found to be different in their nature, form, and color, so that one is harder, sturdier, flatter, broader, narrower, whiter, or bluer in its color—thus qualified and natural in its end, middle, and beginning. This is the reason why these silver-fruits and ores are found differing in their colors and forms, one being more compact, fairer, and of a better glass than the other. Sometimes firm and compact gold, silver, and copper are found in such a vein or passage; it is found this way sometimes at *Krenach*.

union and juncture. And in one mine is found copper ore, in another is found silver ore, and in another mine there is found an iron stone; why should not such remarkable distinctions be noticed, which Nature, from God’s imagination, has held forth so gloriously to miners, and set these before them to be discerned by them? Some silver passages are found also in their natural *Zachstones* <note>a historical term for a specific type of rock or gangue mineral found in veins</note>, which are either in the hanging or lying ones <note>referring to the hanging wall or footwall of a mineral vein</note>. Silver passages show themselves also with blue gritty flowers, in hollowed fluxes, in sprinkled marbles, and carry flint-works of several colors; and these passages and fissures are full of pleasant silver colors—of yellow and green, and of the color of goslings <note>a yellowish-green color</note>. The more they are mingled with such colors, the more they have been worked by nature.

There are some silver passages and veins which carry three distinct colors after the manner of a rainbow, where one color works in nature either more intensely or more mildly than the other, in a curious order; and one may be discerned before the other in their passing strokes and shootings, together with their chamber-colors and floats, as they fell separately and apart in each mine-ore.

## CHAP. V.

### *Of copper ore: its stone, operation, and striking passages.*

Copper ore is produced in its own proper stone, from good pure salt and very hot burning sulfur, through a heavenly impression into all its parts. It is tinged red throughout and not quite freed from a superfluous humidity. It has an affinity with iron, because copper and iron are near kin to one another and their dwellings and houses are set side by side; this is the reason why one may easily be transmuted into the other.

<summary>The author describes the diverse colors and geological signatures of silver veins before introducing the properties of copper ore, noting its alchemical relationship and proximity to iron.</summary>
<keywords>silver ore, copper ore, iron, Zachstones, mineral veins, sulfur, transmutation, mining geology</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the description of copper ore, focusing on its physical appearance in various geological formations—such as floats and flat works—and listing its primary geographical locations.</meta>

<page-num>89</page-num>
<header>his last Will and Testament</header>

This metallic ore is often worked in flat float-works, which are green and flinty; many times it appears in a red or brown form, and is also seen like limestone in black and yellow flat works, or like coals in green flinty passages, in a twofold manner: either as a current or in the manner of a float. Sometimes it is red and brown, mixed with a green color; some are of a lapis lazuli color, some of a copper glass <note>a vitreous copper ore, likely chalcocite</note>, flinty and iron-shot, or of a white "food" <note>likely referring to a matrix mineral or 'nurturing' substance</note>. The copper ore in its vein is sometimes rich in gold and silver, especially when it is accompanied by curious *Zach stones* <note>a historical term for a specific type of rock or gangue mineral found in veins</note> and enclosed within permeable stones—provided that other metals and minerals do not encroach upon them, which would corrode and consume them.

And copper ore is also a flat work mixed with foliated earth; the mercurial copper is difficult to extract from it during an ordinary melting, as it yields a large amount of iron and unripe copper-food <note>immature or developing ore</note>, which significantly interferes with the copper during roasting and makes it unmalleable. The richest copper ores are found in *Hungary*, *Bohemia*, *Silesia*, *Thuringia*, *Hesse*, and *Vogtland*; similar ore is also found around *Trautenau* <note>modern Trutnov</note>, where it breaks everywhere in the manner of a float mixed with sand ore. Where it breaks forcefully in the flat work, they call that a "flat of cliffs" <note>likely meaning fissures or clefts</note>; these are poor in silver and must be roasted or calcined.

In some places, it breaks in a beautiful blue and brown color, or it looks reddish, like copper glass and green ochre; sometimes it is a goldish-white, which is called white copper ore. It turns white during an effective mixture because, during its formation, it takes in a great deal of silver and lead. It also breaks in a yellowish and lapis lazuli-like color, green-flinted upon floats and moving passages, in limestone and spongy stones. It also breaks in a blue color like blue ochre; it is copper-like, glassy, and flinty, occurring in great and huge rocky and marble veins, being mixed with white marble. These are rich in silver, found in green flint stones which are clear and brittle. It lies dry and green in fissures, open caves, and passages, like green frogs sprinkled one into another in a strange manner,

### CHAPTER VI.

->Of iron ore, its mine, operation, stocks, floats, and passages.<-

Ironstone and iron ore is wrought in its mine-stone according to the heavenly influence of *Mars*; for he is *Trinus magnus*, the great lord of war, and an instrument by which others are forced and compelled. It is composed of a hard, earthly, impure sulfur of putrefied salt and gross Mercury; these three principal pieces in their joining mix with much earthiness. Therefore, it is a difficult labor to soften iron with or in the fire, as it carries much impurity by reason of its sulfur. Above other metals it has a deep red quick spirit, which if it be taken from *Mars*, then the iron is gone also, leaving behind a putrid earthiness. Iron is not easily mixed or joined with other metals, or united in the casting. Iron has a threefold partition and several parts in its earthly ore: namely, a magnet, a quick metallic ore which has its quality from quick Mercury and must hold communion and affinity with iron. It must be quickened and renewed with iron filings, in which it lies like a hedgehog, and is endowed by *Sol* in nature with glorious gifts and adamantine virtues; at one side it attracts, and at the other side it repels, which virtues may be augmented and increased in it. A true type of just judgment, it shows, after the Sun, the true hour in the body of the compass, by water and by land.

Secondly, steel, the hardest and purest, most malleable

Thirdly, there comes the common iron ore, ordered together by its earthly sulfur, which three provided good thoughts to the first expert *Naturalists*, that Master of Mine-works *Tubal Cain*, who made his three principles in all things, and made his dimensions in the mines in three distinct parts. In such metallic ore, he first found the ironstone formed in several ways, namely, upon standing passages and floats, fallings and proper pieces singed, after the four elements and colors of the rainbow. Then he considered exactly its flowers, according to the condition of each stonework, how and out of what the ironstone may most conveniently be melted, and what manner of instruments may be used for it, where it may best and most firmly be wrought; for its ore affords a threefold ferocity and wildness, which are useful, namely *Glass heads*, which are like a sharp bloodstone, breaking in the manner of a skull, are scaly, and brown *spiffy* <note>Spiffy (or *spieße*) likely refers to a needle-like or spear-like crystalline structure.</note>, some of which are white thorns, like the wood upon which *Abraham* purposed to offer his son *Isaac*. By this exchange, Tubal Cain, the great and first mine-master, perceived that the stones have their own activity. By observing and finding that the limestones which contain iron ore consist of such mixtures that they may be burned into lime or calx to build walls with them, and how other sorts of *Tapff-stones* <note>Likely "topfstein" or pot-stone/steatite.</note> as well as lime stones are fit to be burned, he found them to be helpful for his smelting. Its ores have decreased in many countries, and all other metallic ores are decaying; only gold, silver, copper, and lead keep their multiplying condition all over the world.

## CHAP. VII.

->*Of Lead-ore, its Mine, condition, and striking*<-
->*passages.*<-

The lead ore is formed under the heavenly influence of the black and cold <term>Saturn</term>, by an unrefined, watery sulfur, impure metal, and salt. First, generally, there is formed a brittle, glittering lead-color in that ore which is called <term>Glasse</term> <note>Likely referring to galena or "glance" ore.</note>; occurring in many rocks, it contains gold and silver and yields large and lasting mine-works. Some lead stones are very broad because glassy ores are mixed with them, along with flints or <term>marcasites</term>; they are partly glassy, reddish-gold, white-gold, silvery, copper-glassy, and made of copper. Some lead ore turns to a blue color, mixed with a white transparency, like a shot <term>Bolus</term> <note>A type of fine, medicinal, or pigment-grade clay.</note>; some is like stone-salt and alum; some are of a dark green, like green

Lead ore is worked in several ways, and its color changes according to the type of ore, especially in the sorts of glassy ores. True lead glasses and ores afford a half or a third part of lead mixed with some other metal; and if one of the other metals is found in the glass and holds the predominance, then the lead passages are simply good, and lead is united with gold. These are mixed stones, for the stones of mine ores are more wonderful in their unique properties.

It is subject to other metals and is the supreme refiner in the essential fruits of others. And *Saturn* <note>Lead.</note> in his degree and power is the highest in a singular division of all his works, in which he shows himself in a clarified, transparent soul; he runs into <term>Antimony</term> with its sweetness, which should completely embrace the gold. This is done for a reason: for in its weightiness, it yields the lightest remedy for all melancholy and heavy blood. Just as heavenly stars are various and the clouds beneath them are of all sorts of colors, so one lead is purer and more malleable than others, as is seen in the lead-stones in *England* and at *Villach*.

For lead ores which are mixed with other stones—especially those containing silver, iron, or copper—yield many light stones and lead-work, which are picked out for separating, especially if they are rich in gold. There are such worthy metals in *Hungary*, and less effort is required for their refining. But if you get a brittle, mixed flint, then the glass is half iron; those that are most pliable provide a melting glass for refining such stubborn, wild ore that will not melt. Artists may prepare such *Saturnal* glass, mingling with it a small quantity of metallic flower, which will look as beautiful as if it were natural. An effective medicine for human health may be extracted from lead.

# CHAP. VIII.

->Of Tin, its ore, operation, mist, stocks, floats, fallings, and striking passages.<-

Tin ore is formed in a sandstone, having its influence from Jupiter above, composed of a dark brown, purple-colored, grayish, black shining mercurial salt, and some sulphur mixed with it, interlined with an unnatural, coarse sulphurous fume; all these incorporate together, making up the body of Tin. This unnatural fume is the cause of the brittleness of Tin, and makes all other metals that are melted with it unnatural and brittle. This Tin or <term>Quitta</term> grows or breaks in a threefold manner, namely: it slides, it is full of fumes, and it grows in pieces. It has a threefold wildness also, such as <term>Shoel</term>, flint, and <term>iron-mould</term>, which causes lead-work; their colors are black, slate, brown,

Some grow in a <term>glimmer</term> or <term>cat-silver</term> and are <term>iron-molded</term>; others also strike into a fire stone or flint, so that fire must be applied to them; others are in a soft stone and, as it were, swim along.

Some are richer than others. That which grows pure and in small black stones, and heaps together that natural work, gives the greatest gain. And because <term>Jupiter</term> is its potent Lord, it has a mighty throne and seat—that is, a mighty, huge <term>mine-ore</term> from which <term>tin</term> is made in heaps. It is of such a nature and property that it presses outward and blossoms to the light of day, thrusting off <term>soap-work</term>, from which comes the wash-work of <term>tin-soap</term>.

For <term>Qwitter</term> does not grow in the sand of the earth; besides, in its body it is removed further from the seat of its throne to its footstool, making for itself a twofold dominion. In one, it borders and reaches to slates and other stones that lie about it, so much so that its dominion increases—containing not a little, but much—on the blue stones, fallings, floats, passages, <term>shools</term>, and cliffs, which incline upon one another and join. Many times a mighty <term>tin</term>-stone is wrought which sinks down among its own cinder and slate, and as it sinks, it purges itself. Then other fumes come like clouds, which shoot into it from all sides, and then it breaks again as good as ever it did before. It is of such a good condition that it despises no lodging, nor passes any by; but however poor and despicable the stone is in that place—be it red, brown, fresh, or stale, broad or small—it will press into and mingle itself with it, and will not be forced out of it. It makes itself great, small, coarse, mild, tame, subtle, and pliable, even as the rest will have it, and all this in a natural way; it loves to border upon silver and iron,

Tin ore in this place is better and more malleable if found far away from flint passages, and is less mingled with iron mold, especially of copper stones, which can hardly be separated during calcining; this results in lead-work without any fair glass.

Some of it is so mild and soft that when they are cleared and calcined, they still lose something; for flints and sulfurous matters, which are volatile and cannot endure any great heat, corrode some of the metallic tin. This appears by the white, thick fume during the calcining. They are calcined so hard by reason of *Bake-iron* <note>possibly a term for roasted iron or a specific iron-rich mineral</note>, otherwise they might yield twice as much; for they lose extremely in calcining. It is strange to some why they shrink together to such a small quantity, seeing they get a greater quantity of lead with good <term>Qwitter</term> <note>a mining term for certain tin ores or residue</note> when first brought out of the mine.

# CHAP. IX.
## *Of Mercurial ore and its passages.*

Mercurial ore is produced in its proper mine-stones by the quality of its salt earth and its nimble volatile earth, in a moist, greasy, slimy, watery oiliness, which is mixed with a most subtle, red, sulfurous digested earth, with a very weak flow-binding, like an unripe, pleasing fruit of all particular metals.

Mercury shows its virtue in many things admirably, and works effectually upon minerals and metallic sulfur, and upon those which border upon antimonial stones or ores; it loves to be in such places where the tin ores lie higher than silver passages. It requires many iterating, effectual operations upon other ores, and is multiplied upon other strange stones, and is drawn through the juices of minerals and metals, which have an affinity for one another,

<summary>This page describes the refining of tin ore and how impurities like flint and sulfur can cause significant material loss during calcination. It also introduces Chapter 9, detailing the volatile, "oily" nature of mercury and its proximity to tin and silver deposits in the mine.</summary>

<sig>H 3</sig>

and produce many strange miscreants; this is the reason why it is so pleasant to metals; goldsmiths amalgamate and gild with it.

It is used also for metallic colors, and is prepared into an oil and water for man's health, and is sublimed to corrode the worst of poisons. It is a true robber, taking along whatever costs have been bestowed on him; but if he can be caught in his nature, then he is in subjection and obedience to both the quick and the dead. He is very effectual in *Medicina* <note>medicine</note>, especially for outward sores; he is bad to the bad, and good to the good, and is not everyone's friend, though he is willing to do whatever you put him to. His metallic stones are of the same nature as pure white slate earth, inclined toward a water-blue, in freshly intermingled white marbles, in a glassy, grayish, and porous *Glimmer* or cat-silver <note>mica</note> which lie beneath between the slates, in a float-way. These are mingled in their metallic passages with unfled <note>non-volatile or unrefined</note> marcasites, and with the subtlest small-streaked white talc, and are thoroughly grown through with two sorts of passages—standing and float-striking—in which is wrought a curious, red, shining quicksilver ore, not unlike red mine-sulfur. Sometimes it flows purely out of the cliffs and caves of the passages and stands in a sink or puddle together like water, which its natural liquid substance sufficiently proves.

## CHAP. X.

### Of Bismuth, Antimony, Sulfur, Salt, Saltpeter and Talc.

Bismuth is formed in its own mine-stone, not quite freed from protruding silver or tin-stone, consisting of an imperfectly pure quicksilver with tin-salt and fluid silver-sulfur of a brittle, immiscible earth, partly of a

<summary>This page concludes the description of mercury's versatile yet volatile nature in alchemy and medicine, followed by its geological occurrence in slate and marble. It then begins Chapter 10, defining bismuth as a mineral composed of mercury, tin-salt, and silver-sulfur.</summary>

<page-num>102</page-num>
<header>VALENTINUS BASILIUS</header>

crude fluid sulfur, partly of a mixed, very dried sulfur, according to the matrix it has obtained after it was conceived. Then it becomes a bastard of a brittle nature, easily uniting with mercury, and is formed naturally in a twofold form: the one is fluid and metallic, is melted with dry wood, and when mixed with clay, yields much white arsenic. The other is small-streaked, or *dense* <note>spissic</note>; it remains an unripe substance and yields a fixed sulfur instead of arsenic. Both of these are silver *bismuth*.

*Antimony* comes from perfect mercury, made of a little salt and a watery fluid sulfur. Though it shines black naturally and its outside is of an antimonial form, yet it graduates and purifies the noble nature of gold and does much good for man when prepared artificially in its several ways. Notwithstanding its color, it keeps its high and mighty praise and virtue. For when it meets with a master who can clarify it, extract its natural gold from it, and draw a blood-red oil from it that serves against many chronic diseases, it must be reduced to a transparent glass. This black, evaporated, unripe metal represents to us God's majestic glory, who is no respecter of persons, bestowing rarities of virtues and knowledge upon poor, despised men!

The red mine-sulfur, which is found in *Tyrol*, *Tonawitz*, and *Engadin*, and grows in a black-blue flat stone, has singular, innumerable good virtues. Within it lies hidden a mighty purifying quality; its color lies close to that of the red gold-silver ore, or cinnabar ore, and it looks almost like it, with a redness that shines forth most pleasantly.

Salt has its special virtues to penetrate and to preserve from putrefaction; it contains a noble spirit. It would be very necessary that men would not be so careless and neglectful in their seasoning with salt, allowing matters to stink and corrupt, considering it too lightly and taking so little notice of the noble gift placed into good mineral works: saltpeter lying on their hoary old walls.

Talc is an ingrown sulfur; it shines incombustibly like gold and silver, closes and bows, and is transparent like glass. It is called Sulfur, *Lutum* <note>Alchemical clay or sealant</note>; it remains incombustible in the fire, like *Alumen plumosum* <note>feather alum or asbestos</note>, lies in rocks and stoneworks, and serves for the graduating of metals. Every metal, mineral, and salt in particular is good to be used, and each is distinguished by its particular name: even as those who make glasses put their several names upon them and put their several forms upon them, making them into drinking glasses, flagons, bodies, bolt-heads, helmets, receivers, pelicans, jar-glasses, wine-glasses, and funnels; all these he frames after his own fancy, either into small, great, long, or round forms, even as he pleases.

## CHAP. XI.

### A comparison between God's word and the Minerals.

Just as the heavenly glorious God in a spiritual way, in His most dear Son our Lord JESUS CHRIST, at His redeeming of mankind for the good of man, appeared as a Sun of righteousness—which glory the Prophet *Esaias* <note>Isaiah</note> prophesied of in the Lord's spirit many years ago: how two *Cherubim* and *Seraphim* having six wings, moved and sang before the Lord: "Holy, holy, holy is the Lord Sabaoth, of whose glory all the world is full." This Prophet saw the most omnipotent Lord of Lords, knowing Him as a God in a triple essence, and that out of that noble chaos of Jesus Christ should flow the fountain of life, of mercy, and righteousness, which the Lord God made apparent on the Tree of the holy Cross, where out of the side of His dearest Son ran blood and water, to which the Lord in the Revelation of St. *John* adds fire, smoke, and

## CHAP. XI.

### A comparison between God's word and the Minerals.

Just as the heavenly, glorious God, in a spiritual way—through His most dear Son our Lord **JESUS CHRIST**, at His redeeming of mankind for the good of man—appeared as a Sun of righteousness, which glory the Prophet *Isaiah* <note>Esaias</note> prophesied by the Lord’s spirit many years ago: How two *Cherubim* and *Seraphim* having six wings moved and sang before the Lord: "Holy, holy, holy is the Lord **Sabaoth** <note>Zebaoth</note>, of whose glory all the world is full." This Prophet saw the most omnipotent Lord of Lords, knowing Him as a God in a Triple essence, and that out of that noble *chaos* of *Jesus Christ* should flow the fountain of life, of mercy, and of righteousness. This the Lord God made apparent on the Tree of the holy Cross, where out of the side of His dearest Son ran blood and water, to which the Lord—in the *Revelation* of *St. John*—adds fire, smoke, and

No man is saved unless he be first born again—that is, through water and blood, which thoroughly purges not only men and the sons of men, but also the whole *Limbus* <note>the border or spiritual threshold</note> upon earth; for it is not metallic blood and water, nor is it Mercury and Sulphur that does it, nor in the body underground is any golden silver wrought into any blood-red ore. The blood shed from Christ's side for the good of man is that great evidence; for thus all Mineral stones that are in the plain element of earth, and the spirit of all ores and marbles and stones, come from the divine essence, just as the heavenly spirits before the throne of God, with the heavenly Angels and Spirits, are furnished for the praise of God: thus the earth also is created in her stones, ores, veins, and passages for the honor of God and the welfare of man, which imitates God's wisdom, filled with the infinite and incessant bringing forth of fruits.

Where should the decay of metals come from? Surely just as the eyes of the holy Apostles and Disciples were held, so that they could not know the Lord in his glorified spiritual body and essence, no more can men see these things in metals.

Why does *Saint John* in his Revelation speak of smoke and of fume? Surely he did not mean the fire, smoke, and fume of bakers' ovens or kitchen chimneys, but there was revealed unto him the heavenly fire, the mist, vapor, and fume,

Now if there were not a fire and vaporous fume in the earth, how could they produce their fruits, which are the minerals and metals underground?

As the fiery element is covered with the airy, and the heaven with clouds, and the earth is filled by them, and together with the fire was enclosed as one element with the other two. In like manner, at the first Creation, the subterranean passages and veins were laden with ores, as trees were with fruit, which the Lord God in Paradise had implanted into them. This effective fire, vapor, and fume is likened unto Mercury, Sulphur, Salt, and Sea-water, wherein earth lies enclosed and hidden, even as the supreme throne of God is encompassed by other thrones and heavenly habitations.

As the four Evangelists are witnesses of the New Testament and Covenant; so they are a type and sure testimony of the four elements, that the earth is created after the holy Heaven; thus are we taught in the Lord's prayer, "as it is in heaven, so on the earth," in which, and beneath, and under, God is everywhere. This is in action still; King *David* could confess that he could not hide himself from the Lord anywhere.

Seeing the holy and blessed God has laid the creatures in the earth with the four elemental qualities, therefore let rational Miners open their eyes and learn judiciously to know the passages and clefts of ores, metals, and minerals; then they will get a lasting name with great praise, and will be like the noble gold, which appears in a glory and beauty when it comes from the *quartz* <note>original "Quart," likely referring to the mineral quartz or the assaying process</note>, and can be then reduced into an oil, which preserves man in lasting health, beyond any balsam, and has become a vegetable <note>in alchemy, "vegetable" refers to the "growing" or "living" state of a substance</note>, which is potable <note>drinkable; a reference to Aurum Potabile, or liquid gold, a legendary alchemical medicine</note>.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the divine and natural properties of metals, moving from the philosophical origins of minerals to the specific medicinal potential of gold and the spiritual parallels between minerals and biblical history.</meta>

# VALENTINUS BASILIUS.

<margin>How</margin>
It is feasible that a singular medicine for the good of mankind may be prepared from gold, because man is created by God from *Limus terrae* (the clay of the earth), and the whole earth is a *Limus*. All the doctors are not able to produce another such medicine, which has a curious, sweet fragrance and stands distinct in two lights. It must necessarily be in *rerum natura* (the nature of things), because it was brought to God’s altar as an offering prepared by man’s art, and He allowed it to be extinguished. No one knows what it is, nor do we educated doctors know its preparation; when their confections, syrups, herbs, and potions do no good and they are in despair, then they might willingly turn to metals, which they formerly had scruples about using in their ointments and plasters. I mention this in reverent remembrance for true, rational miners. From gold and silver are joined not only gold and silver money and other plates for man's use, but they serve man’s use in many other things; and after the first metal’s virtue, there come others also, more and more effective, even to the last of the metals.

There are such virtues in minerals also, such as in vitriol, antimony, alum, salt, and the like. All these are a nourishment to metals, even as Manna was to the *Israelites* in the desert. As they are easily withdrawn and taken from metals, so it happened to them also: Heathens and Christians received that Manna, together with mines and kingdoms; they are set and aimed at the heap of rubbish, where they still worship the [golden] calf. I have spoken more of this in that book where I treated *Fossilia*.

## CHAPTER XII.

### *How precious stones and jewels are formed, and how God has bestowed blessings upon those who work the mines.*

Jewels are formed from the substance of the most perfect, transparent, and noblest earthiness, with a mixture of the noblest mercury, sulfur, and salt, without any vapor or moist matter. They are of a dry coagulation and are commonly generated in a round form in their dwellings, lodgings, veins, and passages, firmly bound together. Some are of a transparent luster, others are darker; and they have their various colors.

Not many ores are found in which these noble, generated bodies are brought to any perfection, nor are they found along a continuous path; here and there they have their centers, to which are joined delicate and miraculous growths, where they are petrified *guttatim* <note>drop by drop</note>, falling into the cavities of the hardest, purest stones, growing in several layers, as we see animal stones grow. The more precious the jewels are, the fewer of them there are; and the coarser their mixture is, the more of them are found, which is apparent in garnets. Who has yet searched into the living spirits of such noble creatures that the Lord has created for man’s benefit?

Pygmies, or *homunculi*, who in former times lived in the hollow ores of mines, could not have lacked skill in such paths, having traversed and traveled up and down all these slippery corners and ways. Since the location and situation of such jewels lie somewhat nearer to Heaven, in the Eastern countries bordering on Paradise, they must necessarily abound in gold and jewels.

<meta>catchword: and</meta>

<summary>This chapter explains the alchemical formation of precious stones from mercury, sulfur, and salt, noting their rarity and their geographical concentration in Eastern lands near Paradise. The author also mentions the legendary presence of pygmies or spirits in mines who possess knowledge of these hidden treasures.</summary>

and such precious vegetables, which our thoughts can hardly reach. God requires no more of man, whom he entrusts with these things, than to be faithful and just; and this is an argument for us to think that for the same cause pious Kings and Princes, and the wise Patriarchs of old, were gifted from above to have a love for searching into the works of the mine, and did so with uprightness and judgment. Let honest, godly Christian miners choose the better part, and learn to know the pearl—the spirit of the Lord proceeding out of God's own mouth—and let them consider well their eternal fixation, to return their love again to him who loved them first. Bringing all things into subjection for them, he imparts everything to them abundantly in grace and mercy, and by the innocence and merit of his only son, bestows on them temporal and eternal blessings, and puts more glorious ornaments on them, and better ones than gold, silver, jewels, and pearls were ever adorned with.

## CHAP. XIII.

->*Of the essence of Gold, which is abundantly found, not*<-

This chapter is a summary of all mineral colors and forms, and how, through a heavenly operation, they are daily clad in the metallic prime matrix <note>the original substance or "mother" from which metals are formed</note> and set forth in their various works. In these, there shines forth to us the eternal light of the lustrous Sun, the deity, the day of joys, and the eternal, most fixed and fairest *Sol*, as also a most yellow, pure, red, and fixed citrine <note>a yellow or lemon color</note> color of heaven's eternal lightning, and the most glorious paradise of all the stars—a natural created light for all creatures, besides the beauti-

full *Aurora* of mineral earths, and of their subtlest, compact, and best binding enclosed, speaking to all other white untinged metals; I, *Sol*, of an essential being, am Lord of Lords in power, might, and perfection; I overcome all, and I overcome and bring them into subjection, and none of them can master me, but I do conquer them all. They are subject to me, and to my being, for my kingdom is established with infinite and invincible power and dignity; by me all metals, minerals, animals, and vegetables are strengthened and rectified; for I give to every one that knows me in my green, blue, and red nature, all what I have, and all what he desires. I cause to drop down, according to the four cardinal streams of *Pison* and *Gihon*, the noblest substance of Mercury, in the form of a most pure, transparent, crystalline water, and the most noble substance of Sulphur, of *Hidekel* and *Phrath*, the clearest, fairest astral salt from a vitriol salt, which through all mines flew upward very fruitfully and penetrates all the mineral stones. I alone graduate and exalt the silver; unto *Lune* <note>the Moon, or silver</note> I give light and luster in all righteousness. Of my virtue all *Magi*, naturalists, and scribes speak all the world over, from the East to the West. I am the Lord over the heavenly clarified garments and colors; I adorn the firmament and the weather; I clothe the rainbow according to God's will; I exalt all jewels, all such growths and creatures; and what I cannot inwardly walk through and reach in my course, I leave to be perfected with my friend and lover the *Lune*. She receives the best part of me, and of the subtlest an abundance; the *Indies*, *Hungary*, and *Carinthia* testify to the same. For all what is to live, and is to receive a life, rejoices in me, and next to God, in none else; for to Him honor and glory belongs solely; after Him, I find no higher Lord and Commander. But for my part I do not rest, neither do I desire any rest; I do my office readily, into which my Creator has placed me; I let my pliability be found gloriously, like a wax in stones, which have, by reason of hardness, fire enough, if need be.

<summary>In this passage, Gold (Sol) is personified as a supreme ruler over all metals and minerals, describing its divine role in strengthening nature and its relationship with Silver (Lune). Sol explains its alchemical origins through the four biblical rivers and its influence over the heavens, colors, and life itself.</summary>
<keywords>Sol, Gold, Alchemy, Lune, Silver, Pison, Gihon, Hidekel, Phrath, Mercury, Sulphur, Astral salt, Vitriol, Mineralogy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the personified speech of an alchemical spirit (likely the spirit of Vitriol or the Mercury of the Philosophers), following the speech of Sol (Gold) from the previous page. # VALENTINUS BASILIUS

I am hidden from unwise men and am ready to be discerned by men of understanding. I am abundantly predominant in a well-known mineral, as also in *Mars* <note>Iron</note> and *Venus* <note>Copper</note> which are of low degrees; in them I lie hidden also. All these have a double spirit, well-known to *Lune* <note>Silver or the Moon</note>, pleasant to her, and next to her. Hence God allowed *Moses* to erect a brazen serpent in the desert after my color, in hearkening to the people at Mount *Sinai*. My best and fairest color appears in transparent juices, such as vitriol, which according to my condition in due time penetrates ores, whereby they grow rich in luster and are trained up in a pleasant form, corroded into a greenness like sealing wax, green like goose dung, blue like sapphire, and so forth, sometimes of the color of a water flint. My red and white colors are the best, which are heartily wished for. I love to be kindled in vitriol and further it after descension in its green food, into a deep red spirit, after whose laxative purging comes that expected *aqua Saturni* <note>Water of Saturn</note>, the true acid-well: from which I myself and all other metals, animals, and vegetables have my offspring and life. For metals and minerals rise only from there, having their beginning and origin from it, for it is that quickening water which ordinary miners do not know of, but is known only to philosophers. It works minerals and metals in several ways, in the form of traps which did <unclear>skept</unclear>, pure, white, compact, found like purified sugar in a blue slate-work. An extraordinary pleasant mineral for all colors. Salt ores are at a further distance, which by my attractive changing are found in floats, blocks, and passages, which in many places bring the water to the daylight, so that it often is found as a pure and dry salt above ground, of glassy light flames, or in a great frost like onto flocks of snow; there shoots a brittle, glassy light stone, wrought in great pieces. In the same order are all other jewels according to my enlightened heavenly stone, distributed among their operations, worths, and virtues, and clarified in a most fixed transparency, and endowed with an everlasting spirit, distinct in

<summary>The speaker identifies as a hidden spirit present in vitriol and base metals, describing itself as the "quickening water" and "acid-well" from which all metals and minerals originate. It details the various colors and physical forms it takes within the earth, including salts and crystalline structures known to philosophers but not to common miners.</summary>

# HIS LAST WILL AND TESTAMENT.

several colors, such as diamonds, emeralds, carbuncles, sapphires, rubies, crystals, chalcedony, jasper, beryl, chrysolite, onyx, cornelian <note>Carmel</note>, turquoise, lapis lazuli <note>Lazur-stone</note>, pearls <note>Margarits</note>, corals, *Terra Lemnia* <note>Lemnian Earth, a medicinal clay</note>, turpentine stones, and garnets of deeper low colors; each in its heavenly color’s order is transparent, and is naturally created and preserved in its own *officina* <note>workshop or laboratory</note>. Hence it may be argued that all these, together with good fruits, serve for man’s good, both for his body and spirit; for nothing is hidden from my transparent power. My splendor and luster overshadow all these, and they are held to their growth until maturity. Let no creatures marvel at these several distinctions and from where they all should come, for all have their principle from me and from my spirit, which is hidden in me—which none can dive into, except the sole Creator of all things, from whom it proceeded as if out of His Divine mouth. Thus I close my speech, and I myself startle at so great a mystery, and attest in truth as a farewell that I am not only the Gold and present *Sol*, but also give strength and power to all the inferior earthly spirits; for *Aristeus* and *Onizon* are in subjection to me, for I am $\alpha$ and $\omega$ <note>Alpha and Omega</note>, God be praised forever.

Thus I conclude the second part of my Mineral book, wherein I have faithfully shown as much as I know and could apprehend by my industry. Let others also do their endeavors; let them also produce their knowledge, so that the light of noble nature may still be supplied in her fullness and may not go out, which would give cause to the enemy and envious men to be outrageous against such truths. Let God still and incessantly be importuned with prayers and thanksgiving. For these ends I have written these two Treatises of mine and annexed the manuals at the beginning (which otherwise did not need to be done), so that by earnest prayer and thanksgiving, and continued earnest worship of God, everyone might carefully exercise himself therein and be convinced in his reason how gloriously Almighty God has created, ordained, and held forth nature to perform her operations.

## A Declaration of the
## XII. Keys.

<image-desc>A decorative initial 'H' set within a square woodcut frame featuring elaborate foliate and scrollwork designs.</image-desc>
Here follows the third part of my intended writings, wherein is truly shown the original and prime matter of our Philosophic stone, which is a perfect instruction for the practical part, showing the direct way to the inexhaustible fountain of health and the abundance of riches to provide for man's necessities. This is a Declaration of my former writings, which is left as a love-token to all those who are lovers of Philosophy.

My friend, you must note that by this intended work touching the Minerals, I had reason to prefix the two parts of Minerals, and Metals, and their ores, holding it a necessity to hold forth a light to the ignorant, showing how one spirit from above frames all such ores, metals, and minerals, taking their origin underground to generate there. For earth is always ready and covetous to attract and to retain that spirit which proceeds from Heaven’s powers, which it presents in process of time in a formality and perfection. The manner of it has been spoken of sufficiently in my former writings, which is the reason why I give only hints of them in this place.

<meta>This page continues from the introduction to the third part of the work, providing a detailed breakdown of the three kingdoms of nature and their primary components—Mercury, Sulphur, and Salt—formed from the four elements.</meta>
<warning>Ink stains and bleed-through are present on the left side of the page, partially obscuring some text.</warning>

Note that all things proceed from a heavenly influence, elemental operation, and earthly substance; from this mixture arise the four elements—water, air, and earth—which engender by the help of fire hidden therein, in a warm digestion, producing a Soul, Spirit, and Body. These are the three prime principles which, in a coagulation, become Mercury, Sulphur, and Salt. These three, being in conjunction according to the nature of the seed, produce a perfect body, whether it be in the kingdom of Minerals, Animals, or Vegetables. All things in the world that are visible and palpable are divided into these three kingdoms: the Animal, which contains those that have a living breath and are composed of flesh and blood, such as men, beasts, worms, fishes, and birds; the Vegetable, which contains trees, herbs, seeds, roots, fruits, and all such things that are of a growing quality; and the Mineral, which contains all manner of ores, metals, minerals, marcasites, calxes, zincs, lobols <note>Likely referring to cobalt</note>, all sorts of flints, pebbles, bismuths, and stones, both precious and others.

*Animals* have their special seed, a spermatic substance, which after copulation generates flesh and blood; this seed is their *prima materia* <note>First matter</note>, derived from a heavenly influence, created by God from the four elements and wrought by nature, which were formerly mentioned in my writings.

*Vegetables* also have their proper seed which God bestowed on them, according to their several qualities and forms, by a heavenly and sidereal influence; they receive their elemental, fruitful growth from the earth, with an order thereby to generate and increase.

*Minerals and Metals* also have their original seed from God, by the heavens' influence in a liquid aerial substance, formed by a mineral spirit, sulphurous soul, and earthly salt joined into one body; of these I have spoken in my former writings.

Note further, if any of these *Metallia* and mineral kinds shall be brought to a further propagation and augmentation, it must first be reduced to its first seed and *prima materia* <note>First matter</note>.

<meta>This page continues the "Elucidation of the XII. Keys," moving from the general classification of the three kingdoms of nature to the specific alchemical process of separating and recombining the three principles—Mercury, Sulphur, and Salt—to create the Philosophical stone.</meta>

# An Elucidation of the XII. Keys.

If you wish to transmute metals, augment them, or bring them into a tincture or Philosophical stone, you must first understand how you may destroy the metallic and mineral form by a spagyric art, and separate it into a Mercury, Sulphur, and Salt. These three must be purely separated and brought to their first principles.

This separation is done in and by a Mercurial spirit, a sulphurous Soul, and a white Salt. These three, in a due ordering of a true manual <note>Referring to practical laboratory work or a handbook of practice</note>, must be joined again so that they may be brought to the highest and most perfect purity.

In this conjunction, the quantity must be exactly observed. After this conjunction, the whole substance is merely a liquid substance and philosophical water, in which all the elements—first the heavenly, then the elemental, and lastly the earthly qualities—are shut up and lie hidden within.

For the Mercurial spirit is cold and moist, the sulphurous Soul is warm and dry, and this liquor is the true *prima materia*, and the first seed of metals and minerals, which by Vulcan’s art <note>The use of fire in alchemy</note> is brought to a *plusquam* perfection, into a transcendent fixed Medicine, out of which the true Philosophical stone is generated, and it must be produced in that way.

Therefore, observe and take notice that all metals and minerals have only *one root* from which their descent generally is. He who knows that rightly does not need to destroy metals to extract the spirit from one, the Sulphur from a second, and the Salt from a third. For there is yet a nearer place in which these three—Spirit, Soul, and Body—lie hidden in one thing; it is well known and may be obtained with great praise. It shall be named afterward in several terms.

He who learns to know exactly this golden seed, or this *Magnet*, and searches thoroughly into its properties, then has the true root of life and may attain that which his heart longs for. In my former writings, as also in the XII. Keys from the first to the last, I ordered my style of writing in this way, wherein I held forth to posterity the *practice*, how the great stone of the philosophers, or the best purified

# An Elucidation of the XII. Keys.

...purified gold may be made out of Sulphur and Salt, with the help of the spirit of Mercury, which must be drawn from a crude, unmelted *Minera* <note>Mineral ore</note>, according to the tenor of my fifth Key, set down in a parabolic manner.

The reason why I based the work of the Philosophic stone upon the metal gold is so that simple laborators <note>Laboratory workers or practitioners of alchemy</note>, to whom the other body or *subjectum* (which contains all three principles) is unknown—though it is a well-known thing, it is yet a stranger to their minds—may learn more wisdom and knowledge by this means. Many of the ancient Philosophers, who lived long before me, have in the same manner as I obtained the true universal stone of all mysteries and health, as their extant books provide evidence. 

The first time, I took great pains, went to great expense, and consumed much time regarding the purified gold mentioned in the first Key; I prepared this heavenly stone in the cloister where I lived and successfully obtained it. The Highest in heaven bestowed his further grace and blessing upon me, so that I took into further consideration the tinging, animated spirits placed and planted into their several bodies. Let no man be ashamed to learn, to add more to his learning, and to dive further into that which was hidden from him, despite his knowledgeable ways. Nature reserves many things in her secrecy which men's dull understanding and shortness of life cannot reach.

Since God in his great goodness has bestowed this great gift upon me, as an improvement of that talent, I have imparted the same to my fellow Christians in the said XII Keys.

Those who are endowed with deeper wits and knowledge, and who in their hearty and careful endeavors strive further to dive into the Art, will meet in the same place with an easier and better-known matter (which was almost named and set down) of an effectual quality. From this matter, in the same way as the ancients before me in their exact speculation and practice, they have in the end better known the

# An Elucidation of the XII. Keyes.

...only scope and purpose, which has been practiced several times by me also; in a shorter time and with less effort, both they and I have obtained health and riches. In this known and lowly matter and mineral substance is found a sulphur and tincture more effectual and more worthy than the best gold can afford, which is fluid and open; its mercurial spirit and its mystical salt are also free and open, whose virtues may, with less effort, be drawn from it in a visible manner.

He who has considered my XII Keys exactly, frequently perusing them, must understand that the whole preparation of our stone is presented there from beginning to end without any defect—yet so that it should only be prepared from gold fitted for it. But we, according to God’s ordinance in nature, have pointed at a gold which is much better and requires deep consideration. Since this is unknown and strange to novices, I forbear for some reasons to give them any direction regarding it, with a resolution to write and point toward such matters as they themselves are inclined to seek their seed in.

At the beginning of my XII Keys, according to the manner of philosophers, I related in a parabolic way the property and work of our stone and balsam, and how it was made by artists—which has come to me as an inheritance as well. In this, I spoke as much as was appropriate concerning the government of the fire, the changing appearances, the principal planetary colors, and the final end of it. After the accomplishment of these, study the XII Keys well, for each contains a particular work.

The first key informs you that if you seek the seed in a metallic body, such as gold, then before all things it must be most exactly purged of all its impure leprosy <note>A term for the impurities or dross found in metals</note>, and nothing must be mixed with our fountain except that which is of a pure spermatic quality. This purifying is performed with antimony, which stands in a near relation and affinity to gold, which is the reason,

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the first key, specifically discussing the purification of gold using antimony and lead (Saturn) to prepare it for the alchemical process.</meta>

...why antimonial sulphur purges the Soul of Gold, graduating it to a very high degree. On the other hand, Gold can improve the Soul of Antimony in a short time and can bring it to a firm fixation, exalting Antimony and Gold to an equal dignity and virtue; it can be brought not only into a white metal of *Lune* <note>Silver</note>, but also into a transcendent medicine for human health, about which you shall have further instruction hereafter when I treat specifically of Antimony. Although Antimony has promised a sociable brotherhood to *Saturn* <note>Lead</note>, because Antimony's qualification rests in some way on the quality of *Saturn* in an equal concordance, yet after the fixation of the exalted Sulphur of Antimony, his next friend *Saturn* cannot take any prey from him, because the King has received him into his golden palace and made him a partaker of his triumphing kingdom. This is the reason why he can now endure both heat and frost, and overcomes them, and stands with the King as a conqueror in great and transcendent glory.

The purifying of the gold is performed thus: laminate the gold thinly in the proper manner and cast it three times through antimony. Afterward, the *Regulus* <note>the metallic heart of the mineral</note> which is produced by the casting must be melted before the blast in a strong fire and driven off with *Saturn*; then you will find the purest, fairest, and most lustrous gold, as pleasant to behold as the luster of *Sol* <note>the Sun or Gold</note> is. This gold is now fit to surrender its innermost being—first being brought from its fixed state into a destructive form, and passing through the salt-sea of its corruptibility, it is drowned therein, then escapes again and appears visibly.

My friend, note and take this into serious consideration, because the chief point lies here: have a *Balneum* <note>a bath or water-bath</note> made, and let nothing come into it which should

not be there, so that the noble seed of the Gold does not fall into a destructive and irrecoverable opposition after its destruction. Take an exact and careful view of such things as my second Key informs you of—namely, what matter you ought to take for the *King's Balneum* <note>Bath</note>, by which the King is destroyed, his external form is broken, and his undefiled Soul may come forth. For this purpose, the *Dragon* and the *Eagle* will serve, which are *Niter* <note>Saltpeter</note> and *Sal ammoniac*. After their union, both of these are made into an *Aqua Fortis* <note>Nitric acid/solvent water</note>, as you shall be further informed in my Manuals, where I shall treat specifically of Gold and of other metals and minerals. Into this *Balneum* the King is thrown, having first been brought—as you shall hear in the quoted passage—into an *amalgam* of *Mercury* and of *Sulphur*, which immediately seizes him, corrodes all his members, and is dissolved; he is then immediately mortified by this salt water into a most splendid, transparent oil. You must note that this dissolution is not sufficient, and the King is not yet minded to let go of his Soul from his fixed body; this you can see when you separate the water from the dissolved body of the King, where you shall find a fixed powder of gold, from which you will hardly extract the soul that is within. Therefore, follow my counsel and bear the yoke which I bore before you, and learn to know exactly through diligent effort, further as I shall inform you. Having dissolved your gold entirely in the said water and brought it into a pleasant yellow oil, then let it stand well-sealed for a day and night in a very gentle *Balneum Mariae* <note>Water bath</note>. The feces <note>residue</note> which have settled must be separated from it. Then take this pure dissolution and put it into a well-coated body or retort; apply a helmet <note>alembic head</note> to it, with a receiver sealed to it in the best manner. Set it into a sand capel <note>sand bath</note> and drive the gold with the water over the helmet. Repeat this a third time, then draw off the water in a *Balneum Mariae*; you will find a fair gold powder. Keep this in a glass for an hour in the fire, and let the remaining humidity be drawn from it.

### The third Key.

Then take one part of good spirit of salt-nitre <note>nitric acid</note>, and three parts of dephlegmated <note>concentrated</note> spirit of ordinary salt <note>hydrochloric acid</note>. Pour these spirits together, having warmed them a little, into a vessel upon the previously described gold powder. Seal a helm and receiver to it, and distill the gold over as before in sand several times with repeated distillation—the more often the better. Let the gold become more and more volatile, and at last let it all come over. By this repeated distillation, its fixed body is divided; all its members are torn asunder and opened, and it willingly leaves its soul to a special judge, about which my third Key will give sufficient information.

Note further, that after this work those salt spirits must be removed from the gold which was distilled over, very gently in a *Balneo Mariae* <note>water bath</note>. Let none of the tincture of the gold come over, so that the body suffers no loss. Then take that gold, or rather these crystals of gold from which you have separated the water, and put it in a reverberating pan. Set it under a muffle; let its first fire be gentle for an hour, so that all its corrosiveness may be taken away. Then your powder will be of a fair scarlet color, as subtle as ever was seen. Put it in a clean vial and pour on it fresh spirit of ordinary salt that has first been brought to a sweetness <note>dulcified or neutralized</note>. Let it stand in a gentle digestion; let that spirit be deeply tinged and transparent, red like a ruby. Decant it, pour on fresh spirit, and extract again. Repeat the work of decanting and pouring until no more of its tincture appears. Put all these extractions together and separate them gently in a *Balneo* <note>water bath</note> from the Sulphur of *Sol* <note>the soul or essential principle of gold</note>. Then that powder is subtle and tender, and of great worth. This matter is such that, in a short process, it transmutes *Luna* <note>silver</note> in its tincture to the highest perfection, according to the direction of my XII. Keys.

He who has some knowledge in this may make this

Query: whether this extracted dry Soul and Sulphur of the King <note>gold</note> be exactly that Soul of which Philosophers have this saying: the philosophic work for the preparation of the most precious stone requires three things: namely, a wet volatile Mercury, or a mercurial spirit; a wet volatile sulphurous Soul; and a dry astral Salt; which, after their dissolution together with the first two, must be apparent and known in a watery form. How does that come about, because in this process nothing is spoken of any mercurial spirit and volatile Soul, but the Soul of the King appeared in a subtle form of powder? The answer to this I delay until the querist learns better to understand the distinctions in this book, and then I will perform my promise and set his anxious and entangled mind at liberty, which is so much troubled by this doubt; I will deal with him as a good father may deal with his son. In this pursuit, wherein our Mastery lies, most of the wits have been fooled, leading them captive in their erroneous ways, being led about in a desert of misleading paths, because in their supposed deep intellects, they had not conceived how all things of the world are generated: that every spirit must have a Soul, and every Soul a fit spirit, and that both spirit and Soul are spirits and spiritual, which must have a body in which they may have a dwelling.

Gold and silver, but chiefly gold, is brought to the highest fixedness by such degrees as nature afforded, insomuch that its nature is found very hot and fiery, freed from all phlegmatic humidity <note>cold moisture</note>, of which Luna <note>silver</note> is not so wholly freed, though she has obtained a degree of fixed sulphur and waits for the King to warm her cold body with his hot seed; this concerns the particulars and belongs to them, which in that place shall be plainly demonstrated. In gold there is no watery humidity at all, unless it were reduced again into Vitriol, which would be but a useless and unprofitable work and would require huge expenses, in case the Philosophers' stone should be made of Vitriol of Gold, of which there must be a great store; indeed in that Vitriol

there would be found a convenient spirit, which nature would desire, of a white quality, as also a Soul and Salt of a glorious essence.

But what countries, goods, and lands have been ruined in this way, I refrain from discussing; I only give this warning to my disciples: nature has left a more direct way to follow and imitate, so that they might also take heed not to fall into such extreme and inescapable poverty.

The solar Mercury *Sol*, never being brought so far toward destruction—nor did the ancient Philosophers ever use that method, as it is a thing entirely contrary to nature—contains indeed a humidity, but it is a mere elemental, watery humidity after its dissolution and is good for nothing. Water and other principles do not exist in the elements, but the elements rest in the principles and seeds of metals, of which I have spoken previously. Therefore, let no one be so over-clever as to make our stone only from dry and fully digested gold: because its phlegmatic humidity has entered into a dry fixedness and fixed coagulation, which is not found to be so in other metals. Although they are also subject to a hard coagulation and have passed through the fire, they are still not wholly digested, nor brought to a full maturity from the natural, original root; this ought to be noted. And do not be offended by my former writings if they seem to run contrary to this.

Although I have shown that the spirit, Soul, and body all come from one metallic essence and must be prepared in this way—among which I held gold to be the best—nevertheless, I acted in this as it seemed fit for a philosopher, just as the ancient Philosophers did before me. But I hope you noticed my declaration: that I provided a specific reason for your speculation to better observe nature and her principle, and to consider the origin. It was not appropriate for me to inform all men how the doors are bolted within, especially at that time when I never intended to write so plainly about these things, which are hidden even from the best of men; but when my heavenly Prince
