# An Elucidation of the XII. Keys.

I was commanded, upon the changing of my mind, not to bury the talent given to me, but to do the same for those whom God thinks worthy, and to leave it to them. I must remind you of one more rule regarding the points I have previously set down and mentioned just now, so that you may blame me less, as if I were now rejecting those things I formerly wrote about.

Read all those who have written about metals since the beginning of the world, and you will find that they were all of one mind, and that I make use of their sayings: that the first and the last metal is a metal, because the first metal has already obtained and acquired the proceeding seed of metals in a metallic quality. This quality does nothing else but continue incessantly in metallic generation, as I spoke of in the first and second parts of *Minerals and Metals*, and I have spoken of it in several places in this part as well.

Many have called gold lead, and lead they called gold, because lead was found not only to have the same weight, but because three deep, glittering stones have solely obtained their transcendent perfection from this planet, along with many other reasons which would be too long and unnecessary to relate here. This is what requires wisdom: to distinguish between these and other things, and to search exactly into God's mysteries and the nature laid before us. But because man was brought to a deep blindness through Adam's fall, human understanding is so eclipsed that people can hardly conceive of this or other mystical matters in nature.

The stubbornness is so great among the covetous—for they mostly search and dive into such mysteries out of mere greed, pride, and ambition—that it made the ancient philosophers, upon the command and inspiration of the Most High, aim to place a certain restraint on their hands. They wrote of such mysteries in such a way that unworthy men should not understand them, and only worthy men might perceive them through their illumination. And writing

often mentioned one thing, and have mingled others among them, still understanding the one and the same. In several places they showed that the <term>Philosophers stone</term> is, and comes from, an animal seed; others from a vegetable seed; and a third sort says it comes from a mineral seed. Others write that the stone is made of an animal, vegetable, and mineral seed together. All this is only understood as the mineral and metallic seed, and does not consist of any plurality of seeds. Hence this Art became obscured, so much so that hardly one among many thousands attains the knowledge of it. For that reason, it is held as an Art, so that not every fool should grasp it in his dull head—and why should they? For if this Art were as common as brewing beer and baking bread, then anyone could judge what good could be expected; would not all manner of vices be practiced publicly without any control?

Therefore such men, who in their lofty minds are only for pomp and pride, must have their wings clipped, and these things ought not to be put into their mouths; things are clear enough for those on whom God intends to bestow them.

I return now to what I intended, which is to teach a willing student how to proceed further with the extracted <term>Soul of Gold</term>. Truly, it is a great thing to discover such mysteries; I warn everyone to make good use of them. Furthermore, if you have the purple cloak of the king, known as the <term>sulphur of Sol</term>, then be thankful to God for it. Bear no evil mind against your neighbor. Unlock your golden seed according to the tenor of the <term>Key</term>, and turn it to water; for in our Art there must be body, soul, and spirit, which run together in the innermost root. One lays hold of the other and improves its whole quality throughout, so much so that there is a new created world and earth, which afterward is illuminated by the soul and is exalted into a transcendent efficacy.

To which end I took these pains to disclose that which all the world was silent about and concealed until their final end, and buried in silence in their very graves.

The scope I aimed at in doing so was not to seek after any vainglory, but rather that God’s gracious, provident goodness might be shown to posterity, so that future ages might see clearly, and some of posterity's eyes might be kept open and be helpful to their needy fellow artist, and make them partakers of God's graces and gifts. Though my mind is greatly troubled when I think on what I have done—because I write so plainly—not knowing into whose hands these my writings will fall after my departure.

Regarding further the *Vitriol*, I should make mention of it in my manuals, where I treat and write generally of minerals. It is that *philosophic gold*, except for one thing: its dissolution during the fire. It is not subject to any corruptibility; otherwise, it would fly away with body and soul. Neither water nor earth can do it any harm, because it received its first birth and beginning from a heavenly water, which in due time is poured down upon the earth.

In these condensed golden waters lies hidden that true bird and *Eagle*, the King with his heavenly splendor, together with his clarified *salt*. You will find these three shut up in this one thing and golden property, and from there you will obtain all that you need for your intention.

Therefore, set that golden body you have obtained—which in dignity and virtue is exalted beyond all other gold—into its proper and lawful dissolution at its due time. Then the Angel of the Highest will appear to you and tell you that it is the *Resolver* of all the mysteries in the world. Receive it with joy and keep it safe, for its quality is more heavenly than earthly; therefore, it heartily inclines to strive after that which is above, from where it had its origin.

If you have separated this prophet from the matter which remained, then you do not need to undertake any further process; you were taught parabolically in my *XII Keys*. For even in his remaining formal substance, you may find and expect a pure immortal *soul*, together with the glory of the *salt*. Both of these are obtained by means of the spirit and must be had from there, and no impure or contrary thing must be added to it. It is done in the same manner as I told you in my *Keys*, with the soul and salt of gold by the Saturnine water, in whose place this spiritual mercurial spirit might be used with better advantage.

This distinction must be exactly observed, being of great concernment, because the *Salt* of *Vitriol* is not so strongly guarded and is not in as fixed a body as the *Gold* is, but is still an open body which has seen no coagulation as yet, nor has it passed through any melting fire. Therefore, that body has not yet come to any compactness; there is room left for its own spirit to enter into, which can embrace and unite with its like, and a snow-white extraction of *Salt* may be obtained. On the other hand, a sharper matter must penetrate *Gold*, as you shall hear when I speak more of it in its proper place.

Consider it well: how sincerely and faithfully I disclose to you all the locks and bonds by which the whole *philosophical* wisdom is shut up, which hitherto never entered any more thoughts, much less was it ever practiced or discovered. Nothing caused me to do it but only *God’s* infinite mercy, and my goodwill and love toward my *neighbor*, which my *predecessors* have not done so completely, and it was left to me to do it.

Keep each in its own and proper quantity; otherwise, in your work, you cannot come to a happy end.

...acknowledge that I have written no untruth, but have disclosed such a truth very plainly. And note that the ancient philosophers endeavored to describe the preparation of the stone under the concept of distilling wine and its spirit, which in their work are almost like one another. For 1. they taught how to make a spirit out of the best wine, without any foreign phlegm <note>watery residue or impurity</note>, which to this day among common artists must be, and is, called the right and true mystical spirit of wine; whereas it may soon be proved that this supposed spirit of wine contains much invisible humidity, or phlegm, in an imperceptible way, which is nothing other than its <term>vegetable Mercury</term>. For the fiery spirit of wine is the true fire and soul of the wine. Every <term>Sulphur</term> secretly contains its original and principal <term>Mercury</term>: vegetables in their kind, animals in their kind, and the minerals also after their kind. 2. They taught how this spirit of wine must be separated into two distinct parts: namely, that this spirit of wine be poured upon white <term>calcined tartar</term> and be drawn over in a gentle distillation. In this distillation, the secret and true spirit of wine is separated from its <term>vegetable Mercury</term>, as I faithfully informed you in my manuals. From the remaining earth they taught that a salt be drawn, to be added to the rectified spirit, whereby it is fortified and strengthened in its substance, and at last the <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> should be generated! It is completely against God's ordinance that a vegetable should produce an animal, or an animal produce a mineral. The practical part is presented by way of a parable under the concept of this preparation. Now, just as they taught concerning wine, so in a similar manner and through a short way, our Gold can also be prepared (not the usual and common gold), and may be dissolved, divided, separated, and brought back to its first principle.

But you must note that this dissolution and separation was never described plainly by any of the ancient philosophers who lived before me and knew the <term>Magisterium</term>. The reason why I do so is that love for my neighbor has moved me

Those who are sincere in their desire to attain art and wisdom, and intend to propagate the fame without sophistication, and desire in reality to glory in that honorable truth, you may show a real proof of it in this manner.

I tell you truly for the highest truth, that you may dissolve our *Gold*, naturally driven together, in a short way to bring it to its *prima materia* <note>first matter</note>, and it is done thus: take the known Mineral Spirit, in which our *Mercury*, *Sulphur*, and *Salt* are shut up, containing that Philosophical mystical *Gold*; pour that *guttatim* <note>drop by drop</note> upon white calcined tartar. These two contrarily qualified matters will contend; let them stay together until their contention and strife be ended, and our *Gold* hides itself invisibly in the vegetable *salt acre* <note>acrid or sharp salt</note>, or in the belly of tartar. Lute a helmet to it and distill it at first gently in a *balneo* <note>water bath</note>; then increase your fire. Then *Hermes’s* volatile bird will fly away from our *Gold* in that sublimation and sit on the highest pinnacles of the Temple, looking about which way to go, but it is soon caught in the *receiver*, which must be pure and very dry. When you see that its flight is only slow, then take the glass out of the *balneo*, set it in ashes, and increase your fire; then it will fly more nimbly. Keep that fire going until everything has come over; and its brother, the *Red Dragon*, hiding its redness under a red color in a whitish fume, will begin to follow after its flying brother. Then stop the fire; once the drops have all fallen from the helmet, take it off. That which you find in the *receiver* you are to keep as a treasure of mysteries. In this manner, you have gained wisdom, understanding, and skill, as well as the fundamentals and desires of philosophers. By this short, clever proof, you learn and obtain the knowledge of how this water may be sought after, found, and encountered; and it is not to be considered a common water, but is that real, infallible heavenly water of which I have written at the beginning and have frequently repeated. In a spiritual manner, it is poured down upon the earth from heaven's power, and it begins and accomplishes the generation of all metals. For that reason, the ancient philosophers called this water *Mercury*, but I call it the *Spirit of Mercury*.

Now if you proceed correctly in this work, and you know what food and what drink to give to this bird—namely, *Sulphur*

joined, the one in their mixture embraces the other, even as a Magnet draws Iron, but in an improved essence, better than they had before their dissolution. This is the success, beginning, middle, and end of the total Philosophical wisdom, affording riches and health, and a long life; it may rather be said, and really proved, that this spirit is the essence of Vitriol, because this Spirit and Oil differ so much, and were never united radically, because the Oil comes after the spirit, and each can be received apart; This fiery spirit may rather and more fitly be called an essence, sulphur, and substance of Gold, and it is so, though it lies lurking in Vitriol as a spirit.

This golden water, or spirit drawn from Vitriol, contains again a sulphur and Magnet; its sulphur is the *anima*, an incombustible fire, the Magnet is its own Salt, which in the conjunction attracts its Sulphur and Mercury, unites with the same, and they are inseparable Companions. First, in a gentle heat, the undigested Mercurial spirit is dissolved; by this is further extracted, according to a Magnetic quality, the sulphurous *anima*; in that earth sticks the Salt, which is also extracted in a Magnetic way by the Mercurial spirit, so still the one is a Magnet to the other, bearing a Magnetic love for one another, as such things where the last, together with the *medium*, is drawn forth by the first, and are thereby generated, and thus take their beginning. In this separation and dissolution the spirit, or Mercury, is the first Magnet, showing its Magnetic virtue toward the sulphur and Soul, which it *quasi Magnes* (as a magnet) attracts; this spirit *per modum distillationis* (by way of distillation) being absolved and freed, shows again its Magnetic power toward the salt, which it attracts from the dead earth; after the spirit is separated from it, then the Salt appears in its purity: if that process be further followed, and after a true order and measure the conjunction be undertaken, and the Spirit and Salt be set together into the Philosophical furnace, then it appears again, how the heavenly spirit strives in a Magnetic way to attract its own Salt; it dissolves the same within 40 days,

But in the exchange of it, a bright glittering whiteness appearing, then the case is altered, and the dissolved fluid watery Salt turns into a Magnet; for in that dissolution it lays hold on its own spirit, which is the spirit of *Mercury*, attracts it powerfully like a Magnet, hiding it under a form of a dry clear body, bringing the same by way of uniting into a deep coagulation and firm fixedness by means of a continued fire, and the certain degrees of it.

The King with the white Crown being thus generated, and by the drying up of all moisture being brought to a fixed state, it is then nothing else but earth and water, though the other Elements are hidden within it imperceptibly; however, both of these maintain the predominance, though the spirit turns to earth and can never be seen in a watery form, and this double newborn body still abides in its Magnetic quality; for as soon as his departed Soul is restored after its white fixation, then like a Magnet he attracts it again and unites with it, and then they are exalted to their highest tincture and *rubedo*, with a bright transparency and clarity. Thus in brief you have a short account of Vitriols, Sulphur, and Magnet. Pray to God for grace, that you may conceive of it correctly; put it then to good use, and be mindful of the poor and needy.

At the closing I annex this briefly, to present to you a natural proof, so that you may immediately cast and throw down the *Sophister*, and take his *Scepter* from him. *Note*, that from all Metals, especially from *Mars* and *Venus*, which are very hard and almost fixed Metals, a *Vitriol* can be made from each separately; this is the reduction of a Metal into a Mineral: for Minerals grow into Metals, and Metals were at first Minerals, and so Minerals are the *proxima materia* (proximate matter) of Metals, but not the *prima* (prime matter): from these vitriol may be made, and other reduc-

This spirit being distilled, then there is again a reduction of a mineral into its spiritual essence, and each spirit in its reduction keeps a metallic property; but this spirit is not the *prima materia*. Who is now so crude and absurd that they should not be able to conceive further and believe that by these reductions from one to another there is a way to the *prima materia*, and at last to the seed itself, of both metals and minerals? Though there is no necessity to destroy metals, because their seed in the minerals is found openly fixed.

O good God, what do these ignorant men think! Is this not a very easy and childlike labor? One begets the other, and one comes from the other—is not bread baked from corn through distinct processes? But the world is blind and will be so until the end of it. This is all for now, and I commit you to the protection of the Highest.

# THE FOURTH PART,

## On Particulars of the Seven Metals, and how they may be prepared profitably.

### First, regarding the Sulphur of *Sol*, by which *Lune* is tinged into good Gold.

<image-desc>Ornate woodcut initial 'T' decorated with floral and foliate scrollwork.</image-desc>Take pure gold that has been cast three times through *antimony*, and six parts of well-purified quicksilver (*mercury* vive) that has been pressed through leather; make an *amalgam* from them. To the amount of this amalgam, grind twice as much common sulphur. Let it evaporate on a broad pan over a gentle heat under a muffle, constantly stirring it with an iron hook. Keep the fire moderate so that the matter does not melt together. This gold calx must be brought to the color of a marigold flower; then it is correct. Then take one part of saltpeter, one part of sal ammoniac, and half a part of ground pebbles, and distill a water from them. Note: this water must be distilled cautiously and precisely. Distilling it in the common way will not suffice; he who is accustomed to chemical preparations knows what he must do. And note, you must have a strong stone retort, which must be coated to keep the spirits tightly contained. Its upper part must have a pipe more than half a span long and two fingers wide. It must first be placed in a distilling furnace that is open at the top so that the upper pipe may stand out directly. Attach a large receiver and seal it well with lute. Let your first fire be gentle, then increase it until the retort appears glowing hot:

### OF SULPHUR OF SOL.

Put a spoonful of this ground matter in through the pipe, close the pipe suddenly with a wet cloth, and the spirits come rushing into the receiver. After these spirits have settled, then add another spoonful; in this manner you proceed until you have distilled everything. At last, allow time for the spirits to settle and turn into water. This water is a hellish, strong dissolving one, which instantly dissolves prepared gold calx and laminated gold into a thick solution, of which I made mention above in the third part. This is that water which I mentioned in my second Key, which dissolves not only gold, but brings it to a volatility, carrying it over the helmet, whose *anima* may afterward be drawn from its torn body.

Note, the spirit of common salt effects the same, if drawn in that manner which I shall speak of afterward. If three parts of this salt-spirit be taken, and one part of *spiritus nitri*, it is stronger than sal ammoniac water; and it is better, because it is not so corrosive, dissolves gold sooner, carries it over the helmet, makes it volatile and fit to part with its soul. You have your choice to use which you think best and which may be more easily prepared, as follows: Take one part of the prepared gold calx and three parts of the water which you choose; put it into a body, lute a helmet to it, set it in warm ashes, and let it dissolve. Upon that which is not dissolved, pour three times as much water, so that all dissolves. Let it cool, separate the feces, put the solution into a body, lute a helmet to it, and let it stand in a gentle heat day and night in a *Balneo Mariæ*. If more feces have settled, separate them; digest them again in the bath for nine days and nights, then abstract the water gently to a spissitude, like an oil in the bottom. This abstracted water must be poured back onto that spissitude; this must be repeated often, so that it becomes weary and weak. Remember to lute well at all times. To the oleity at the bottom, pour fresh water which has not yet been used; digest day and night firmly closed, then set it in a sand Capel and distill the water from it to a thickness. Make the abstracted water warm, put it into a body, and lute it,

# Of Sulphur of Salt.

Take the white body of *Sol* <note>Gold</note>, from which you have drawn its *anima* <note>soul or tincture</note>, reverberate <note>heat in a reverberatory furnace</note> it gently for half an hour, and let it become solid <note>corporeal</note>. Then pour well-rectified honey-water, which is corrosive, onto it. Extract its Salt in a gentle heat; this is accomplished in ten days' time. Once the Salt is all extracted, distill <note>abstract</note> the water from it in a water bath <note>Balneo</note>. Wash <note>edulcorate</note> the Salt with repeated distillations using common distilled water, and clarify it with spirit of wine. Then you have the Salt of gold <note>Sal auri</note>, of which you shall hear more in its proper place regarding the good medicinal qualities it has for mankind. On the remaining matter, pour spirit of *Tartar* (of which I will speak in another place because it belongs to medicines). Digest these for a month's time, then distill <note>drive</note> it through a glass retort into cold water; then you have the living <note>quick</note> Mercury of *Sol*, which many strive to obtain, but in vain.

There is one mystery more in Nature: that the white *Solar* body, having once lost its *anima*, may be tinged again and brought back to being pure Gold, which mystery is revealed to very few. I shall give a hint of it, so that you may not grumble at me for having concealed any part of the work.

I hope you have considered and taken to heart what I have entrusted to you regarding the universal stone of Philosophers in my third part—namely, how it rests merely upon the white spirit of *Vitriol*, and how all three principles are found only in this spirit, and how you are to proceed in bringing each into its certain state and order.

Take the Philosophic *Sulphur*, which in order is the second principle and is extracted with the spirit of *Mercury* <term>☿</term>. Pour it onto the white body of the *King* <note>Gold</note>, digest it for a month in a gentle water bath <note>Balmy</note>, then fix it in ashes, and finally in sand, until the brown powder appears. Then melt it with a fluxing powder made of *Saturn* <note>Lead</note>; then it will be malleable and beautiful Gold, just as it was before, lacking nothing in color and virtue <note>potency</note>.

But note: the Salt must not be taken from the *Solar* body, which I mentioned previously in a repetition of the

# Of Sulphur of Sol.

...the twelfth Key, where you may read about it. A transparent Vitriol may also be prepared from gold in another manner, as follows:

Take one pound of good Aqua Regis made with Sal ammoniac; *that is*, dissolve four ounces of Sal ammoniac in Aqua fortis—then you have a strong Aqua Regis. Distill and rectify it often through the helm <note>the top part of a still</note>; let no sediment <note>feces</note> remain behind, and let everything that ascends be transparent. Then take thinly beaten gold rolls, previously purified through antimony; put them into a vessel <note>body</note> and pour Aqua Regis over them. Let it dissolve as much as it will, or as much as you can dissolve in it. When all the gold is dissolved, pour in some Oil of Tartar, or Salt of Tartar dissolved in spring water. It will begin to hiss <note>tisse/effervesce</note>; when it has finished hissing, pour in more of the Oil. Do this until all the dissolved gold has fallen to the bottom, nothing more precipitates, and the Aqua Regis clears up. 

This being done, then pour off <note>cant off</note> the Aqua Regis from the gold calx and wash <note>edulcorate</note> it with common water eight, ten, or twelve times. When the gold calx has well settled, pour off that water and dry the gold calx in the air where the sun does not shine. Do not do it over a fire, for as soon as it feels the least heat it kindles, and great damage is done; for it would explode <note>fly away</note> so forcibly that no man could stop it. 

When this powder is ready, take strong vinegar and pour it over it. Boil it continually over the fire in a large amount of vinegar, stirring it constantly so that it does not stick to the bottom, for twenty-four hours together; then the explosive <note>fulminating</note> quality is removed from it. Be careful that you do not endanger yourself. Pour off that vinegar, sweeten <note>dulcify/wash</note> the powder, and dry it. This powder may be distilled <note>driven</note> through an alembic <note>per alembicum</note> without any corrosive; it will be blood-red, transparent, and beautiful, which is remarkable. It unites willingly with the spirit of wine and, by means of coagulation, may be brought back to a *Solar* body <note>Gold</note>.

Do not speak much of this to the common people. If you receive any benefit from my plain and open instruction, be resolved to keep these mysteries secret until your dying day and do not make a show of it; otherwise, you are naked and

Anneal <note>heat and then cool</note> the remaining powder in a strong heat, and put it into spirit of wine. Repeat this process six times; at last, this gold powder will be as soft and porous as firm butter. Dry it gently, because it melts easily. 

Then take a <term>coated body</term> <note>a distillation flask reinforced with a protective coating</note> which has a pipe in its back part. Then take the softened, well-dried gold powder and, after it has been warmed, swiftly cast it into the hollow pipe at the back. Red drops will instantly enter the helmet. Keep the fire at this level as long as anything continues to ascend and no more drops fall into the receiver. Note: there must be some of the best spirit of wine in the receiver, into which the drops of gold are to fall.

Then take this spirit of wine into which the gold drops fell, put it in a <term>pellican</term> <note>a circulatory distillation vessel</note>, and seal it *Hermetice* <note>hermetically, or air-tight</note>. [quanti]ty of the Lunar calx is not tinged, the other is as good as it was to be used. If you succeed in this rightly, then be thankful to God; if not, do not blame me; I could not make it plainer to you.

Now if you want to make this vitriol, take the powder previously made and boiled in vinegar. Pour onto it good spirit of common salt mixed with saltpeter water and the spirit of salt of niter. This saltpeter water is made in the same way that *aqua Tartari* is made with saltpeter. Gold is dissolved in this water. Once this is done, boil away the water until it thickens, then place it in a cellar. There, a pure vitriol of *Sol* <note>gold</note> will crystallize. The water which remains with the vitriol must be poured off. Distill it again to a thick consistency <note>spissitude</note> and place it in the cellar; more of the vitriol will crystallize. Repeat this work as long as any vitriol continues to form. If you intend to make the Philosopher's Stone from solar vitriol, as some eccentric men attempt to do, then first consult your wallet and prepare ten or twelve pounds of this vitriol. Then you may perform the work very well, and the Hungarian vitriol or others dug out of mines will allow you to do so. You may also extract the sulfur and salt from this vitriol with spirit of wine; since this is easy work, it is unnecessary to describe it.

Repeat this three times; then the salt is prepared.

When the water grows white and the Salt is entirely out of it, then extract the honey water, wash away <note>edulcorate</note> the corrosiveness by distilling, and clarify the Salt with spirit of wine. The said Salt of Silver <note>Lune</note> has excellent virtues for the human body, of which I shall speak in another place. Thus you have the white tincture, which you may animate with the volatile dissolved soul <note>anima</note> of Gold <note>Sol</note>, fix, bring to the deepest redness, and at last ferment and increase <note>augment</note> the same infinitely <note>in infinitum</note>, the spirit of Mercury being added thereunto. And note, that a process is to be performed upon Gold with its own Sulphur and Salt.

If you understood how their first mover <note>primum mobile</note> is to be known, then it would be unnecessary to destroy metals in this manner and for that purpose; instead, you might prepare everything from their first essence and bring them to their full perfection.

### *Of the Particular of Mars, together with the extraction of its Soul <note>Anima</note> and Salt.*

<margin>Using water is best.</margin>
Take one part of red *Oil of Vitriol*, or *Oil of Sulphur*, and two parts of ordinary well-water; put those together and dissolve filings of steel in them. This solution must be filtered while warm; let a third part of it gently evaporate, then set the glass in a cool place. Crystals as sweet as sugar will form <note>shoot</note>, which is the true *Vitriol of Mars*. Decant <note>cant</note> off that water, let it evaporate more, and set it again in a cold place, where more crystals will form. Anneal <note>neal</note> them gently under a muffle furnace, stirring constantly with an iron wire; then you will get a beautiful purple-colored powder. On this powder, pour distilled vinegar and extract the soul <note>anima</note> of *Mars* in a gentle water bath <note>Balmy/Balneum</note>. Remove <note>abstract</note> the vinegar again and sweeten <note>dulcorate</note> the soul. This is the soul <note>anima</note> of *Mars*, which, when added to the spirit of *Mercury* and united with the soul <note>anima</note> of *Sol*, tints *Lune* into *Sol*, as you heard regarding the Gold.

# Of the Particular of Saturn.

...and spirit is united together, and fit to transmute precipitated <term>Mercury</term> into <term>Sol</term> <note>gold</note>.

The precipitation of <term>Mercury</term> is done thus: take one part of the spirit of <term>Salt of Niter</term> <note>potassium nitrate</note>, and three parts of <term>Oil of Vitriol</term> <note>sulfuric acid</note>; put these together and cast into it half a part of quick <term>Mercury</term> that has been very well purged. Set it in sand and apply a reasonably strong fire to it, so that the spirits may not fly away. Let it stand a whole day and night, then abstract <note>distill off</note> all the spirits; you will then find in the bottom a precipitated <term>Mercury</term>, somewhat red. Pour the spirits on it again and let it stand day and night; abstract it again, and your precipitate will be redder than at first. Pour it a third time upon it, then abstract strongly; then your precipitate is at the highest <term>rubedo</term> <note>redness</note>. Dulcify <note>purify by washing</note> it with distilled water and let it be strongly exiccated <note>dried</note>. Then take two parts of this precipitated <term>Mercury</term> and one part of the dissolved *Saturnal Oil*; put these together and set it in the ashes. Let all be fixed; not one drop must stick anywhere to the glass. Then it must be melted with the appropriate additions of lead; they close together and afford Gold, which afterward, by casting through <term>Antimony</term>, can be exalted <note>purified or refined</note>.

I have informed you of this where I treated of *Mercury vive* <note>quicksilver</note>. But note, that <term>Mercury</term> must not be precipitated unless with pure <term>Oil of Vitriol</term>, or <term>Oil of Venus</term> <note>oil of copper</note>, with the addition of the spirit of <term>Salt of Niter</term>. Although such <term>Mercury</term> cannot be brought to its highest <term>fixation</term> by way of precipitating, its fixed <term>coagulation</term> is found in *Saturn*, as you heard.

Beat the aforementioned <term>Mercury</term> small, grind it on a stone, and put it in a vial. Pour the dissolved *Saturnal Oil* onto it; it enters instantly, provided you proceeded correctly in the precipitation. Seal the vial *Hermetically*, fix it in ashes, and finally in sand to its highest <term>fixation</term>. Then you have bound <term>Mercury</term> with a true knot and brought him into a fixed <term>coagulation</term>, which brought its form and substance into an improvement with an abundance of superfluous riches. If you carry it on a white precipitate, then you get only silver, which contains but little Gold.

# Of the Particular of Saturn.

One thing more I must tell you about this process: there is yet a better way to work with <term>Saturn</term> <note>lead</note> for more profit. You, *seeker*, so that you may not have any cause to complain that I have not declared it, take it as follows: take two parts of the aforementioned dissolved oil, or of the *Saturnal Soul*; one part of *Astrum Solis*; and two parts of *Antimonial Sulphur* (whose preparation follows afterward). Add half as much *Salt of Mars* <note>iron salt</note> as the total weight of all these ingredients. Weigh them together, put them all into a glass vial (leaving a third of it empty), and set it in together to be fixed. Then the *Salt of Mars* opens within this compound and is fermented by it, and the matter begins to turn toward blackness. For ten or twelve days it is eclipsed; then the salt returns to its coagulation, seizing the whole compound in its operation. Coagulate it first into a deep brown mass; let it stand thus unstirred in a continued heat until it turns into a blood-red body. Increase the fire so that you may see *Astrum Solis* become predominant, which appears in a greenish color like a rainbow. Keep this fire burning continuously and let all these colors vanish. It turns into a transparent red stone, ve<unclear>r</unclear>y heavy. It is unnecessary to project this on <term>Mercury</term> <note>quicksilver</note>, but after its perfection and fixation, it tinges all white metals into the purest Gold. 

Then take one part of the prepared fixed red stone (or of the powder) and four parts of any white metal. First, let the metal melt for half an hour and let it be well clarified. Then project the powder upon it and let it drive <note>circulate or flow</note> well. See that it has entered into the metal and that the metal begins to congeal; then it is transmuted into Gold. Break the crucible in pieces and take it out. If it has any dross <note>slag or impurities</note>, refine it with <term>Saturn</term>; then it will be pure and malleable. If you apply it to <term>Lune</term> <note>silver</note>, then add more of the powder than you would to <term>Jupiter</term> <note>tin</note> and <term>Saturn</term>; for example, half an ounce of the powder tinges five ounces of <term>Lune</term> into <term>Sol</term> <note>gold</note>. Let this be a miracle; do not fool your soul by imparting this mystery to others who are unworthy of it. Proceed with <term>Salt of Saturn</term> as you were informed regarding <term>Mars</term> <note>iron</note> and <term>Venus</term> <note>copper</note>, except that distilled vinegar performs that which honey

### *Of the Particular of Jupiter, together with the extraction of its Soul and Salt.*

Take Pumice-stones, sold in shops, heat them until glowing, quench them in good old Wine, heat them again, and quench them as you did before; let this heating be repeated a third time. The stronger the Wine is that you quench with, the better it is; after that, dry them gently. Thus are they prepared for that purpose. Pulverize these Pumice-stones finely, then take good *Tin*, laminate it, and stratify it in a cementing way in a reverberating Furnace. Reverberate this matter for five days and nights in a flaming fire; it draws the tincture of the Metal. Then grind it small, first scraping the *Tin* laminates; put it in a glass body and pour good distilled *Vinegar* over it. Set it in digestion; the *Vinegar* draws the tincture, which is reddish-yellow. Remove this *Vinegar* in a *water bath* <note>Balneo</note>, wash <note>edulcorate</note> the *Soul* <note>Anima</note> of *Jupiter* with distilled water, and dry it gently. Proceed with the rest as you did with the *Soul* of *Saturn*, namely: dissolve it radically in, or with, the *spirit of Mercury*, and distill them. Pour that upon two parts of red *Mercury* precipitate, being precipitated with this *Venerean* <note>copper-like</note> sanguine quality, then coagulate and fix it. If done successfully, you may acknowledge *Jupiter's* bounty, which allowed this precipitate to be transmuted into *Gold*, which will be apparent at their melting. It performs this also: it transmutes ten parts of *Silver* <note>Lune</note> into *Gold*, if other *Sulphurs* be added to it. Demand no more from *Jupiter*; it is all he is able to do, being of a peaceful disposition; he has told all that he could do. The process regarding this *Salt* is to extract it with distilled *Rain-water*, clarified with *spirit of Wine.*

# Of the Particular of *Mercury vive*, and of its *Sulphur* and *Salt*.

Take half a pound <note>lib.</note> of quick *Mercury*, sublimed seven times; grind it very small and pour a good quantity of sharp vinegar over it. Boil it on the fire for an hour or more, stirring the material with a wooden spatula. Take it from the fire and let it cool. The *Mercury* settles to the bottom, and the vinegar clears up. If it is slow in clearing, let some drops of spirit of Vitriol fall into the vinegar; it precipitates the other, for *Vitriol* precipitates quicksilver (*Mercury vive*), Salt (*Sal*) of Tartar precipitates Gold (*Sol*), Copper (*Venus*) and common salt precipitate Silver (*Lune*), and Iron (*Mars*) does the same to Copper (*Venus*). A lye <note>lixivium</note> of beech-ashes does it to Vitriol, and vinegar is for common Sulphur in that way, and Iron (*Mars*) for Tartar, and saltpeter <note>Sal-peter</note> for *Antimony*. Decant the vinegar from the precipitate; you will find the Mercury like pure washed sand. Pour vinegar on it and repeat this work a third time; then wash <note>edulcorate</note> the material and let it dry gently.

Take two ounces of the Soul <note>Anima</note> of Iron (*Mars*), one ounce of the Soul <note>Anima</note> of Lead (*Saturn*), and one ounce of the Soul <note>Anima</note> of Tin (*Jupiter*). Dissolve these in six ounces of *mercurial spirit*. Let all be dissolved, then distill it over; leave nothing behind. It will be a golden water, like a transparent dissolution of Gold (*Sol*). Your prepared and washed <note>edulcorated</note> *Mercury* must be warmed in a strong vial; pour this warmed water gently over it. A fizzing <note>tiffing</note> will occur; stop the vial until the fizzing is gone. Then seal it hermetically <note>Hermeticè</note> and set it in a gentle water bath <note>Balmy</note>. In ten days, the *Mercury* is dissolved into a grass-green oil. Set the vial in ashes for a day and a night, controlling your fire gently. This green color turns into a yellow oil; in this color is hidden the redness <note>Rubedo</note>. Keep it in this fire and let the material turn to a yellow powder, like orpiment. When no more distills over, then set the glass in sand for a day and a night, and give a strong fire to it;

### *An Oil made of Mercury, and its Salt.*

Take quicksilver (*Mercury*), having been often sublimed and rectified with quicklime (*Calx vive*). Put it in a vessel <note>body</note> and dissolve it with heat in strong *Nitrous* water. Distill away <note>abstract</note> the water from it. The corrosiveness that remains there must be extracted with good vinegar, well-boiled in it. At last, distill away <note>abstract</note> this vinegar; the remainder must be purified <note>dulcified</note> with distilled water and then dried. Afterward, one pound <note>lib. 1.</note> of the best spirit of Wine must be poured onto each pound of the material. Let it stand sealed <note>luted</note> in putrefaction; then distill <note>drive over</note> what may be distilled, first gently, then more strongly. From that which has come over, distill away <note>abstract</note> the spirit of Wine by a water bath <note>per Balneum</note>. A fragrant oil remains behind, which is the *Astrum Mercurii* <note>Star of Mercury</note>, an excellent remedy against venereal diseases.

Seeing that the Salt and the *Astrum* of Mercury have the same medicinal operation, I hold it needless to write of each in particular and will join their operation into one, and declare it in the last part concerning the Salt of Mercury, because they have the same effect in medicinal operations. Take the prepared oil, or *Astrum Mercurii*, which by reason of its great heat keeps its own body in a perpetual running, casting it on the next standing earth <note>the solid remains</note> from which you formerly drew the oil. Set it in heat; the oil draws out its own Salt. That being done, add a reasonable quantity of spirit of Wine and distill it away <note>abstract it</note> again. The Salt remains behind, dissolved in the fresh spirit of Wine, being purified <note>dulcified</note> by repeated distillation <note>cohobation</note>.

## Of the Particular of Antimony, *together with the extraction of its Sulphur and Salt.*

**T**ake good *Hungarian Antimony*, pulverize it finely to a meal, calcine it over a gentle heat, stirring it constantly with an iron wire, and let it be whitened <note>albified</note>, so that at last it may be able to endure a strong fire. Then put it into a melting pot, melt it, pour it out, and turn it to a transparent glass; beat that glass, grind it finely, and put it in a glass vessel <note>body</note> with a broad flat bottom. Pour on it distilled vinegar, and let it stand sealed <note>luted</note> in a gentle heat for a good while; the vinegar extracts the antimonial tincture, which is of a deep redness. Distill off <note>abstract</note> the vinegar; there remains a sweet yellow fine powder, which must be washed <note>edulcorated</note> with distilled water until all acidity is taken off; then dry it <note>exiccate</note>. Pour on it the best graduated *spirit of wine* and set it in a gentle heat; you will have a new extraction, which is fair and yellow; decant <note>cant</note> it off, pour on other spirit, and let it extract as long as it can. Then distill off <note>abstract</note> the spirit of wine and dry it <note>exiccate</note>; you will find a tender, deep yellow, fine powder of an admirable medicinal operation, which is nothing inferior to *potable Gold* <note>potable Sol</note>.

# Sulphur and Salt of Antimony.

...receiver to it, distill it, as aqua fortis is usually distilled. Take one pound each of the distilled liquor and common salt; rectify them once more. Let no muddiness come over with it; everything must come over clear. Then take one pound of pulverized antimonial glass, pour this spirit onto it, seal it well with lute, digest it, and let it all be dissolved. Then draw off the water in a *Balneo Mariae* <note>water bath</note>; there remains at the bottom a black, thick, fluid matter, but somewhat dry. Lay it on a glass table and set it in a cellar; a red oil flows from it, leaving some dregs <note>feces</note> behind. Coagulate this red oil gently upon ashes and let it be dried <note>exiccated</note> there. Then pour the best spirit of wine on it; it extracts a tincture which is blood-red. Decant <note>cant off</note> the liquid that is colored, and pour more spirit of wine on the remainder until all the redness is extracted. Thus you have the tincture, or *Antimonial Sulphur*, which is of a wonderful medicinal efficacy and is equivalent to potable gold, as you heard in the previous process. And its preparation now serves to proceed with it *Particulariter* <note>specifically</note>, as I showed in the previous section. This black matter, which remained behind after the extraction of the sulfur, must be well dried. Extract its snow-white salt with distilled vinegar, wash it <note>edulcorate</note>, and clarify it with spirit of wine. Observe its virtues in medicine <note>Medicina</note>, of which more will be said in the last part.

Thus I also conclude my fourth part. Other mysteries in nature and some augmentations might be added here, but I waive them, mentioning only the chief of them. These are such as may be performed easily and in a short time, and by which a great store of riches may be obtained. The rest, which are not of such importance and may easily lead novices into error while bringing no immediate profit, may in due time be found and obtained through careful practice.

If you only know those by which health and wealth are obtained, then these metallic sulfurs in their compounds may bring great profit to you. To write of all these in detail <note>circumstantially</note> is impossible for one man; it is a task of infinite labor. Call upon God for grace and mercy.

# In this book are contained
## these following heads.

<image-desc>An ornamental woodcut initial 'T' inside a square frame decorated with floral or vine patterns.</image-desc>

1. The nature, condition, and property of mine-works, in which minerals and metals grow; of their first seed, birth, quality, and property, and of their exhalation and inhalation, and how they are supported in their growth and increase.

2. The origin of ores, stones, rocks, and marbles; of their passages and clefts, their liquids, juices, energies, and operations; *namely*, of gold, silver, copper, iron, tin, lead, quicksilver, and of minerals.

3. A plain literal expression of that Universal Elixir, by which a healthy life is prolonged, all diseases are expelled, and an abundance of riches is obtained: with a perfect declaration of the XII Keys.

4. A particular description of metals, by which their several endowments are declared, showing how they serve to keep man in perfect health, with many manuals for the working of them to serve man so he may live more comfortably in outward means; and how metals and minerals in particular are brought to their highest preparation, by which it is ensured that men may live in this world without lacking necessary things.

Human *fear coming upon me, I began to con-*

*Having spent some time in my* Order *after I had done*

### THE
## PREFACE.

*In our monastery, there was one of my fellows who was greatly tormented with the stone. He was often bedridden and sought out many physicians, but none was able to cure him; he was left hopeless, taking his refuge in God’s omnipotence. Then I began to anatomize vegetables and extracted their salt and quintessences, but none of all these would or could help my sick fellow. I made trials of many of them, but all were too weak to dissolve the stone. I took his case into further consideration and intended to know fundamentally what efficacy the great Creator had put into minerals and metals; the more I searched into them, the more I found, with one secret still issuing forth from another. God blessed me in this and opened my eyes, so that I saw marvelous virtues in the nature of minerals and metals which the great Creator had implanted in them—so much so, that it is a difficult matter to believe it.*

*Among these, I happened to obtain one mineral, composed of many colors, which had many and rare virtues in medicine. I drew its spiritual essence from it, by which in a few days I cured my diseased colleague. For this mineral spirit was very strong and strengthened the weak spirit of my brother, and he lived a long time after that cure. He prayed daily and hourly for me as long as he lived, even to his dying moment; his and other men’s prayers availed so much with the great Creator that, by His blessing and my endeavors, many great matters were revealed to me which He did not reveal to worldly wise men.*

<summary>The author describes how he turned to mineral and metal alchemy after botanical remedies failed to cure a fellow monk’s kidney stones. After successfully curing his colleague with a mineral essence, the author’s continued research and the prayers of others led to the revelation of further natural secrets.</summary>
<keywords>Basilius Valentinus, alchemy, mineral medicine, the stone, quintessence, vegetable salts, medical trial, divine revelation, monastery</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the author's preface, transitioning from the description of his specific medical discovery to a broader statement of intent to share his alchemical knowledge—his "Philosophical stone"—with future generations for the benefit of human health.</meta>

## The Preface.

*This <term>Philosophical stone</term> for man's health and his sustenance in this valley of misery I reveal to posterity, as much as is proper for me to do, following in this the steps of my predecessors. These philosophical instructions are enigmatic and short, but are a rock on which Truth may firmly be built. I wish good success and blessings from above to those who undertake this work. Amen.*

# The Contents of this Book,

->are<-

I. Of the great stone of the Ancient Philosophers.
II. The XII Keys, by which the doors to the <term>Philosophers' stone</term> are opened, and the deep fountain of health and wealth flows from there.
III. A short repetition of his writings about the Philosophical stone, in which the true Philosophical light is plainly presented; to which is added information about Quicksilver, Antimony, Vitriol-water, common Sulphur, <term>Calx vive</term>, Arsenic, Saltpeter, Salmiac, Tartar, Vinegar, and Wine.
IV. Of the <term>Microcosm</term>, or Man's body: what it contains, of what it is composed, its entire contents, and of its outcome and end.
V. Of the great mystery of the world, and its medicines belonging to man.
VI. Of the *Magisterium* of the *VII Planets: their essence, properties, virtues, operation, and revolution, and their admirable hidden mystical qualities.*

# Of the Great Stone of the Ancient Philosophers, writ-
### ten by
# BASILIUS VALENTINUS

<image-desc>An ornate woodcut initial 'D' set within a square frame decorated with stylized foliage and scrollwork.</image-desc>
Dear friend and well-wisher to the <term>Art</term>, in my Preface I promised to those who are desirous to learn and to dive into Nature's condition, to show and to speak of that <term>cornerstone</term>, as much as I am permitted from above to do, regarding what the ancient philosophers prepared their <term>stone</term> from, by which they prolonged their lives in continued health, and by which they also obtained their riches to live comfortably in this miserable world. To fulfill my promise, not leading you into any tedious <term>sophistic labyrinths</term> but disclosing the very <term>headspring</term> of all goodness, you are to note and to take into serious consideration my following expressions, if your intent is to learn anything concerning this <term>Art</term>; I do not intend to use any prolixity in words, for that would be to no purpose: I love few words which are full of pith.

Note, it is given to only a few men to attain the mastery of this <term>Art</term>; though many strive and endeavor to work upon that structure, yet the true knowledge and the attainment of it the great Creator has made common,

<summary>Basilius Valentinus introduces his work on the Philosophers' Stone, promising to explain how the ancient philosophers achieved health and wealth through its preparation without using unnecessary or confusing language.</summary>

but bestows it only on those who hate lies and love

and chiefly those men are fit for it who love God unfeign-

Therefore I tell you for an absolute truth, that in case you
intend to go about making this <term>stone</term>, you should be a fol-

to the great Creator to bestow His blessing upon you

a full resolution never to do evil again, but lead a godly
life, so that your heart may be enlightened in all good
things. And remember, whenever you are promoted to any
honor, to be helpful to the poor and needy, to deliver
them out of their misery, making them glad with your help-
ing hand, so that the Lord may bestow greater blessings
upon you, and you may thereby be confirmed in faith that
there is a throne in Heaven prepared for such a person,
hereafter to live in eternal bliss.

My friend, do not despise reading the good and real writings
of those men who had the <term>Philosophic stone</term> before us,
for from them I had it, next to God's revelation: the
reading of their books must be frequently repeated; then the
fundamentals of it will better stick to the memory,
and truth, like a burning candle, will not be extinguished.

Be industrious in your careful working, search into <term>Scri-
ptures</term> continually, do not be prepossessed with opinions,
and follow the unanimous consensus of <term>Philosophers</term>:
a wavering man is easily led into wrong ways, and
those men who have wavering minds seldom build firm
houses.

through; therefore do not trouble yourself to seek for it in such
things where <term>Nature</term> would not have you seek for it.

# Ancient Philosophers.

not: For if our stone were of a condition such as other vegetables are, it would easily be consumed in fire, and nothing would remain but only its salt. Although there have been men who have written great volumes on the vegetable stone, yet, my friend, you must note that it will be very difficult for you to conceive of it; for they call our stone a vegetable one because growth and augmenting belong to it.

Note further, irrational beasts increase after their own kind: so you should not strive to seek for, or to make this stone, except only from its own seed, from which it has its beginning and being. Neither ought you to look for any animal soul for the making of it: flesh and blood, which the great Creator has bestowed upon animals, belong properly to animals. God composed them of flesh and blood, by which an animal is made. But our stone, <unclear>hi</unclear>ch came to me as an inheritance from the ancient philosophers, is made of one and of two things, which contain a third; this is the naked truth, and it is rightly spoken. For the ancients understood by "man and wife" one body—not in respect of the outward appearance, but by reason of their innate love, which they received at the first working of their natures; in that respect, it is acknowledged that they are one. And as both propagate and increase their seed, even so the seed of that matter from which our stone is made can be propagated and augmented.

If you are a true lover of our <term>Art</term>, you will take this expression into consideration to keep yourself out of the pit into which erroneous <term>Sophisters</term> <note>In this context, deceptive or false alchemists</note> usually fall, which their enemy dug for them. My friend, so that you may know further from where <unclear>t'is</unclear> seed comes, then first ask yourself for what purpose you intend to seek after this stone. Reason will then dictate to you that it must necessarily spring from a metallic root, which the Creator has ordained for metals to generate by. # Of the great stone of the

darkness, then the omnipotent and eternal God, who is without a beginning and end, whose wisdom was from eternity; by his unsearchable decree, he created heaven and earth and the things contained therein, whether they be visible or invisible, by whatever name they may be called. But of the manner of this glorious Creation, my intent is not now to philosophize much upon: let Scripture and Faith be impartial judges here!

The great Creator has given in the Creation a seed to every creature, by which it should generate and increase, so that animals, vegetables, and minerals might be continually preserved. Man has no power bestowed on him to make or bring forth a new kind of seed according to his fancy against God's ordinance; there is granted unto him only a propagation and increase. God reserved for his sole power the ability to make seed, otherwise man could play the Creator also, which does not become him and belongs merely to the highest Creator.

Understand the seed which works metals in this way: there is a heavenly influence, according to God's good pleasure and ordinance, that falls from above and mingles with sidereal qualities. When such conjunctions happen, then these two beget an earthly substance as a third thing, which is the beginning of our seed, its first original, by which its first descent is proved. From these three, the elements have their offspring—as water, air, and earth—which work further by an Ætnick <note>Volcanic or subterranean fire, named after Mount Etna</note> fire to the bringing forth of a perfect thing, which Hermes and all those before me (for I could find no more) have called the three principles; these were found to be an internal soul, an incomprehensible spirit, and a visible body. These three, being together in one dwelling, in process of time—yet by <term>Vulcan's</term> <note>The use of fire or the god of fire, representing the alchemical process</note> help—become a comprehensible being, as a <term>Mercury</term>, <term>Sulphur</term>, and <term>Salt</term>. These three, by a uniting being brought into a coagulation according to Nature's miraculous operation, bring forth a perfect body as Nature would have it, and as the Creator had ordained the seed for it. ### Ancient Philosophers.

...after the fountain of our work, and hopes to get the victory in this warfare, to him I tell this for a truth: that where there is a *Metallic Soul*, a *Metallic spirit*, and a *Metallic form of body*, there must also necessarily be a *Metallic Mercury*, a *Metallic Sulfur*, and a *Metallic Salt*. These must necessarily produce a perfect *Metallic body*. If you do not understand it now, then surely you are not suited for *Philosophy*; and in brief it is this: it will not be possible for you to reap the benefit of any *Metallic body* unless you have completely joined the previously named three principles. Note further: *Animals* are composed of flesh and blood; there is in them also a living *spirit* and breath which dwells in them, but they are destitute of a rational *Soul*, with which man is endowed above them. This is the reason that when *Animals* lose their lives, they are gone, with no more hope for them forever. But man—who offers his body to his Creator at the time of death—has a *Soul* which, at the day of resurrection, is to receive a glorified body for his *Soul*; they are to dwell together, and so *Soul*, *Body*, and *Spirit* come together again in a heavenly clarification, which in all eternity will never be separated again, etc.

Therefore man, by reason of his *Soul*, is acknowledged to be a fixed *Creature*, because he is to live forever after this life (though in his body he is subject to a temporal death). For death is to man only a clarification; according to God's ordinance, by certain degrees he is delivered from <unclear>sinfulnesse</unclear> and transplanted into a better condition. This does not befall other *Animals*; therefore they are esteemed to be unfixed *Creatures*, for once these are gone by death, they cannot expect nor look for any resuscitation, because they lack a rational *Soul*, for which the sole *Mediator Jesus Christ* has suffered and shed his innocent blood. A *spirit* may have a dwelling in a body, but it does not follow that it must abide there constantly, even though the body and that *spirit* be at rest, and that body with that *spirit* does not contest over any controversy: because both

# Of the great stone of the

...lack the strongest part, which upholds and binds together soul and body, and protects and keeps them from dangers—namely, the tender, noble, and fixed soul. For where the soul is quite gone and lost, no redemption is ever hoped for; for a thing which has no soul is not perfect, which is one of the highest mysteries that seekers ought to know. Upon my conscience, I am commanded not to conceal this mystery, but to make it known to those who seriously love the fundamentals of truth. And take careful notice of what I say: spirits hidden in metals are not alike; some are more volatile, and others more fixed. Their souls and bodies are not alike either: that metal which contains the three types of fixedness is blessed with a power to endure the fire and so overcome all its enemies, which is only found in *Sol*. *Lune* contains a fixed Mercury, and that is the reason why she does not fly so soon in the fire as other imperfect metals do, but stands out her examinations in the fire and shows the same victoriously, because devouring *Saturn* can rob nothing from her.

That arch-wench *Venus* is clothed and possessed with an abundant tincture; the most part of her body is a mere tincture, like the tincture which also dwells in the best metal, and by reason of its superfluity, she is tinted red. Because her body is leprous, that fixed tincture cannot have any abiding place in her unfixed body, but must vanish together with her body. For when the body is consumed by destruction or death, that body cannot subsist either, but must give way and fly, because the habitation is destroyed and consumed with fire, so that her place is unknown, nor may any other dwell there from this time forward. But in a fixed body, she willingly dwells constantly.

Fixed salt has bestowed on valorous *Mars* a hard, sturdy, and gross body, whereby the gallantry of his mind is proved, and it is not so easily taken from this warlike prince, because his body is hard and is not easily mastered or con-

Through this, certain Keys may be advanced, and the needy laborer may obtain a portion of a living *Particulariter* <note>An alchemical term referring to a process that transforms a specific or "particular" amount of metal, rather than a universal transformation.</note>, if he has reached the highest step of that ladder. For the phlegmatic quality or moist nature of *Lune* must be dried out by the hot blood of *Venus*, and her great pains must be eased by the external Salt.

There is no need to seek for seed in the Elements, because our seed is not placed so far back. Rather, there is a closer place where our seed has its sure and certain home. If you only rectify and regulate the Philosophical Mercury, Sulphur, and Salt, so that an inseparable union is made of their soul, spirit, and body—one that can never be divided again—then the Band of Love is perfectly formed, and the dwelling is well prepared for the Crown.

And note that this is only a liquid Key, like a heavenly property, and a dry water attached to an earthly substance. All of this is but one thing, proceeding and growing from three, two, and one. If you can achieve this, then you have mastered the work. Join the Bride and Bridegroom together; let them feed and cherish one another with their own flesh and blood, and let them increase and multiply infinitely by their own seed. I would willingly communicate and disclose more to you, but the Creator has forbidden it. It is not proper for me to speak any further of it, for fear that the gifts of the Most High might be misused, and I would be the cause of great sins, bringing God's wrath upon myself and falling with the rest into eternal punishment.

My friend, if these expressions do not satisfy your understanding of the matter, and I lead you to the practical part of my own experience—how I attained the Philosopher's Stone by God's assistance—I pray you consider it well. Diligently study my XII Keys, read them repeatedly, and then proceed according to my instructions, which I have laid out fundamentally in the form of a parable.

<meta>Continuing from the previous page's discussion on the union of metals, this page describes the "anatomization" of gold into its constituent spiritual parts and introduces an alchemical allegory involving the pride and subsequent imprisonment of Mercury by the other planetary deities.</meta>

# Of the great stone of the

Take a piece of the finest Gold and anatomize it by such means as Nature has afforded to Artists, just as a physician anatomizes a man's body to investigate the condition of his internal parts. Reduce your Gold to what it was at first; then you will find the seed—the beginning, middle, and end—out of which our Gold and its wife are made: namely, a penetrating, subtle spirit; a pure, tender, and undefiled Soul; and an Astral Salt and Balsam. After their uniting, this is nothing else but a Mercurial liquor. This same water was brought to school to its own god, Mercury, who examined the water. Having found it without deceit or falsehood, he formed a friendship with it and took the water into a matrimonial state, and both became an incombustible Oil. For Mercury grew so proud because of it that he no longer knew himself; he flung away his Eagle's wings, swallowed the smooth tail of the Dragon himself, and offered battle to *Mars*. *Mars* gathered his champions together and gave the command that *Mercury* should be taken prisoner and kept closely imprisoned. *Vulcan* was commanded to be the jailer until a female kind should come for his deliverance. 

This rumor being spread abroad, the other Planets held a meeting where they consulted on what further was to be done in that business and how the proceedings might wisely be prosecuted. Then Saturn made a speech in this manner: "I, *Saturn*, the highest Planet in the firmament, declare before this honorable assembly of my lords that I am the meanest and most contemptible among you all. I have a weak, corruptible body of a black color, subject to many infirmities in this miserable world, and yet I am an examiner of you all. For I have no abiding place, and I take along with me those who are like me. I can lay the fault for this misery of mine on no one but Mercury, who by his carelessness has brought this evil upon me. Therefore, my lords, I beseech you to be pleased to revenge my quarrel on him; and since he is already cast into prison, take his life there and let him rot, so that not one drop of his blood may remain."

pleasant speech, and amiable gestures, bearing fragrant
flowers in her hand, which were a most refreshing pleasure
to the eyes to behold by reason of the variety of colors. She

shrill voice: "My descent is of women; my seed is scattered
far and near by them; thereby they replenished the Earth.
Their soul is kind to mine; therefore my desire is to feed
and to drink of their blood." The beast having spoken thus

All these things thus happening, there was a meeting of several learned men who endeavored to interpret and explain what happened and what had been spoken, so that they might better understand these mysteries. None of them was able to perform that business, for all of them had different thoughts concerning these things. At last there stood up an ancient man, with hair as white as snow, dressed in purple from head to toe; on his head he had a crown, in the middle of which was set a precious carbuncle. He was bound with the girdle of life and went barefooted. He spoke with a special spirit which was hidden within him; his speech and sayings went through his body and life, and his soul felt it to the innermost part. This man stepped up high and asked the assembly to be silent and to give diligent attention to him, because he was sent from above to explain these writings to them and to make them known in a philosophical expression. The assembly being silent, he began to speak thus:

> Awaken, O man, and behold the light, so that darkness may not mislead you. The Gods of Fortune and the great Gods have revealed this to me in a deep sleep. <page-num>11</page-num>
<header>Ancient Philosophers.</header>

Hungary has first begotten me; Heaven and the stars preserve me; Earth gives suck to me. Though I must die and be buried, however the god <term>Vulcan</term> begets me a second time; therefore, Hungary is my native country, and my mother encompasses the whole earth.

When the assembly heard this, he spoke further as follows: "Make that which is highest to be lowest, that which is visible to be invisible, and that which is comprehensible to be incomprehensible; and provide that the lowermost becomes the uppermost. Make the invisible become visible; make the incomprehensible a palpable thing. This is the whole Art, and very perfect, without any defect. But within it dwells death and life, dying and rising. It is a round globe on which the goddess Fortune lets her chariot roll about, and brings the salvation of wisdom to men of God. Its true sense is, **ALL in ALL**; but the highest is Judge, who judges things eternal."

He who desires to know what **ALL in ALL** is, let him make great wings for the earth, put her into a great anguish, let her soar upward, make her fly through the air, and be exalted to the highest place of the uppermost heaven. Then burn her wings with a powerful fire, so that the earth may fall into the Red Sea and be drowned within it. Then command the sea to stand still, dry up the water by fire and air, and reduce it to earth again; then I say, you have **ALL in ALL**. If you cannot find this, then feel into your own bosom, and around you into all things that are found in the world; then you will find **ALL in ALL**, which is of an attractive quality of mineral and metallic bodies, descending from <term>Sal</term> and <term>Sulphur</term>, twice begotten of <term>Mercury</term>. It is not appropriate for me to speak more of **ALL in ALL**, because **ALL** comprehends **ALL**."

This speech being made, he said further: "Beloved men,"

<page-num>82</page-num>
<header>The first Key.</header>

I hope you have, by listening to my voice, learned wisdom: how and in what you are to seek for the great stone of the ancient Philosophers, which heals leprous, imperfect metals, reveals to them a new birth, preserves men in health, prolongs their lives, and by its heavenly power and operation has kept me alive so long that I am weary of life and wish for nothing but death.

Thanks be to God for his grace and wisdom, which he has granted so graciously to me for such a long time; blessed be his holy name forever, Amen. Thus, he vanished away before their eyes.

After the finishing of this, everyone returned home, meditating seriously on these things day and night, and everyone worked according to the various gifts they received from God's bounty and goodness, etc.

## Hereupon follow the XII Keys of
# BASILIUS VALENTINUS.

->*Whereby the doors are opened to the ancient*<-
->*stone of Philosophers, where is found that*<-
->*everlasting fountain of health and*<-
->*of wealth.*<-

### The first Key.

**MY** friend, you must know that impure and defiled things are not fit for our work. For their leprosy is no help for the advancement of our work: good things are hindered in ways that are unclean. Goods from mines are worth their money: but if sophisticated, they are made unfit, being adulterated in their former and original operation.

<summary>A wise figure concludes his speech on the life-prolonging power of the Philosophers' Stone before vanishing, serving as an introduction to the Twelve Keys of Basilius Valentinus. The first key begins by warning that only pure, unadulterated materials are suitable for alchemical work.</summary>
<keywords>Basilius Valentinus, Twelve Keys, Philosophers' Stone, alchemy, leprosy of metals, purification, longevity, divine wisdom</keywords>

--- Page 233 ---
<meta>Continuing from the introduction to the Twelve Keys of Basilius Valentinus, this page describes the first key, focusing on the purification of metals using the symbolic "gray wolf" to cleanse the "King" (gold).</meta>

<page-num>39</page-num>
<header>The first Key.</header>
<sig>C 2</sig>

As physicians cleanse and purge the inward parts of the body by means of medicine, expelling all impurities from there, so also must these bodies be purified of their impurities, so that perfection may be active in our birth. Our masters require a pure, undefiled body, which must not be mixed with any spot or foreign matter. For foreign additions are like leprosy to our metals. The King's crown must be of pure gold, and a chaste bride must be married to him. Therefore, if you will work through or upon our bodies, then take the greedy gray wolf, which by reason of its name stands in subjection to valorous *Mars*; but regarding his descent, he is a child of old *Saturn*, found in the valleys and hills of the world, and is very hungry. Cast the King's body before him and let him feed upon it; and when he has devoured the King, then make a great fire and cast the wolf into it, letting him be quite burned. Then the King will be set at liberty again. This being done three times, the wolf is conquered by the *Lion*, finding nothing more on him to feed upon; then is our body perfect for the beginning of our work.

Note that this is the only true and suitable way to purge our bodies, for the *Lion* is cleansed by the wolf's blood, and the tincture of that blood rejoices mightily in the Lion's tincture, because they are near kin to one another. When the *Lion* is satisfied, his spirit is as well, and his eyes cast proud rays like the lustrous sun; his internal essence is of great ability and good for all such things you intend to apply him to. Being brought into its proper preparation, the sons of men are then indebted to him—those who were burdened with the falling sickness <note>epilepsy</note> and other diseases. The ten lepers run after him and desire to drink of the blood of his soul, and all those who are diseased rejoice greatly in his spirit. For he who drinks of this golden fountain feels himself thoroughly renewed in his nature; all evil things are taken away, the blood is strengthened, the heart receives strength, and all the limbs are in full vigor; it opens all pores and nerves, expelling

<meta>catchword: ling</meta>

<summary>The first key of alchemy describes a purification process where a "gray wolf" (antimony) is used to devour and refine the "King" (gold) through fire. This process creates a "Lion" with medicinal properties capable of curing diseases like leprosy and the falling sickness.</summary>
<keywords>The First Key, alchemy, purification, gray wolf, antimony, Mars, Saturn, Lion, gold, medicinal tincture, falling sickness</keywords>

--- Page 234 ---
<meta>Continuing from the instructions on the first key, this page concludes the discussion on purifying the "King" (gold) and introduces the second key.</meta>

...expelling their malignities, so that goodness may come into their places.

My friend, you must take great care that the Fountain of Life be kept from muddiness; no foreign water must be mingled with our Fountain, or else a monster will be produced. A wholesome fish will be turned into a serpent if, by an intermediary, a corrosiveness is joined by which our body is broken; then let that corrosiveness be washed away. This is because corrosives are not to be used for internal diseases; since acidities are rather destructive, causing diseases, our Fountain must be without poison—even though poison expels poison.

A tree that brings forth no good fruit is cut off at the trunk; better twigs are grafted into it. When these grafts are united with the tree, then its root, trunk, and twigs bring forth better fruits, which are more wholesome.

The King in the heavenly firmament walks through six places, but in the seventh he keeps his seat, for there the kingly throne is hung with golden pieces.

If you understand correctly what I say, then with that Key you have opened the first lock, and you have driven back the bolt. But if you cannot find any light in these words, then no glass eyes <note>spectacles</note> will help you, nor will any natural eyes enable you to find out the last thing, which you lacked at first. I will speak no further of this Key, as *Lucius Papirius* taught and commanded me.

### The Second Key.

In the courts of great potentates, several sorts of drinks are found, and none are like the others in smell, taste, or color, because they are of several preparations. However, all of them are drinkable because they are suited for several places and are necessary for the maintenance of the court. When the Sun emits its rays, spreading them

under the clouds, then the common saying is, the Sun draws water, and it will rain: which happening often, that year proves fertile.

To raise a magnificent palace to its height, several artisans and workmen must be employed before that structure and its rooms can be finished. For where stones must be used, wood is of no use.

The daily ebbing and flowing of the sea, out of an infused love which it receives from above out of the starry heaven, is for the purpose that countries are enriched by it: at every return, it brings great good to mankind.

A virgin who is to be married is set out gloriously with several garments, dressed in the best manner so that she may please her bridegroom; and the bond of love may take deeper root by their looking heartily at one another. When the bride joins with the bridegroom in the usual manner, these garments are taken off, and the bride keeps only that which she received from the Creator at her birth.

Even so, when our bridegroom *Apollo* with his bride *Diana* is to be married, several garments must first be made for them; their heads and bodies must be well washed with water, and you must learn to make these waters by several distillations. For these waters differ very much: some are high-quality, some are poor, according to the various uses for which they are employed; this I suggested when I spoke of the various sorts of drinks used in princes' courts. And know that when the humidity ascends from the earth and the vapor is drawn up, they gather together on high; their weight makes them fall down, and thereby the earth’s lost humidity is restored. This refreshes the earth and gives her nourishment, by which the plants spring up. Therefore, some waters in their preparation must be distilled often; the extracts must be often restored to the earth and must be drawn off again:
