## The second Key.
<meta>catchword: The</meta>

Just as *Euripus* <note>A strait in Greece famous for its strong, oscillating currents.</note> does often disgorge itself at a certain period.
The *Kingly Palace* having been raised and adorned by several *artisans* and *workmen*, and the *glassy Sea* having finished its course, and the *Palace* being furnished with goods, then the *King* may safely enter into it and keep his residence there.
My friend, note this very well: the *Bridegroom* with his *Bride* must be married naked, and therefore the *ornaments* prepared for their clothing and the necessary attire for their heads and faces must be taken from them again. They must possess the grave in nakedness, just as they were born naked, so that their seed might not be destroyed by any strange mixture.
At the closing of this, I tell you in good truth that the most precious water, from which the *Bridegroom’s Bath* must be made, must be prepared very carefully and wisely from two contrary *fencers* or contrary materials. For one *fencer* must drive the other; they must be fitted for the fight, and one must conquer the other. For what does it avail the *Eagle* that she keeps her nest alone in the *Alps*, where her chicks are destroyed by the frost because of the snow on the tops of these *mountains*?
But if you add the cold *Dragon* to the *Eagle*—who has lived for a long time in stone cliffs and *subterranean caves* where he crept in and out—and both are placed on that *hellish stool*, then *Pluto* will breathe so strongly upon them, expelling a fiery volatile spirit out of the cold *Dragon*. This spirit's great heat will burn the *Eagle’s* feathers, preparing a sweating bath so that the snow on the highest tops of the *mountains* dissolves and turns into water. Thus, the mineral bath is richly prepared, and riches and health are bestowed on the *King*.

<summary>The author explains that the alchemical "Bride" and "Bridegroom" must be stripped of all impurities before their union. He describes the reaction between the "Eagle" and "Dragon" under heat as a "fencing" match that results in a mineral bath essential for the King's health.</summary>

<meta>This page transitions from the Second Key to the Third Key, continuing the alchemical allegory of transformation through the interaction of opposing elements—specifically water and fire.</meta>

->The third Key.<-

Water destroys fire, quenching it completely; if an abundance of water is poured onto a small fire, the fire must yield to the water, giving way to its victory. Thus our fiery Sulphur must be prepared with water and conquered by art, so that after the water is separated, the fiery life of our sulphurous vapor shall achieve the triumphing victory. But here no victory can be obtained unless the King has bestowed strength and virtue upon his water, and has delivered to it the Key of his Court color, so that he may be destroyed by it and made invisible; however, at this time his visible form must appear again, but with great reduction of his simple essence and great improvement of his condition.

Limmers <note>Illuminators or artists.</note> apply yellow on white, red upon yellow, or a purple color; though all these colors are present, the last is predominant, being the highest in its degree. The same order must also be observed in our *Magisterium*, and when this is done, you will have before you the light of wisdom, which shines in darkness and yet does not burn.

For our Sulphur does not burn, yet it gives off light from afar; neither does it tinge unless it is prepared and tinged freely with its own tincture, in order to give a further tincture to the weak, imperfect bodies of metals. This Sulphur has no tinging quality unless the tincture is given to it in a fixation; for a weak thing cannot be victorious—the stronger keeps down the weaker, and weak things must yield to strong ones. The conclusion here is this: a weak and lowly thing cannot help another that suffers from the same frailty, nor can it provide any advancement to its operation; can one combustible thing protect another of the same condition? A Protector must have a greater

It argues that a reagent must possess greater strength and a higher "tincture" than the substance it intends to protect or improve.</summary>

<keywords>The Third Key, Sulphur, alchemy, tincture, Magisterium, fixation, purification, elements, Limmers</keywords>

He who would prepare our incombustible Sulphur of the Philosophers must be careful to seek our Sulphur in a subject where it lies incombustible; this cannot be unless the Salt-Sea has first swallowed the body and cast it up again freely. Then exalt it to its degree, so that it excels all other Stars in Heaven with its luster, and is as rich in blood in its substance as the Pelican is at the opening of her breast, nourishing many of her chicks without weakening her own body. This is the Rose of our Masters, of a scarlet color, and the red blood of the Dragon, of which so many have written; it is that purple mantle of the highest Commander in our Art, with which the Queen of salvation is clothed and covered, and by which all needy metals may be warmed.

Keep this honorable mantle with the Astral Salt very carefully, which follows after this heavenly Sulphur; let no accident befall it. Impart to it the bird's volatile quality as much as is necessary; then the Cock will devour the Fox, which is drowned in water or revived by fire, and is devoured again by the Fox, where like is requited with like (or like is reconciled to unlike).

## The fourth Key.

All flesh begotten of earth must be destroyed and return to earth again, as it was at first; then that terrestrial Salt provides a new birth by heavenly resuscitation. For if there is not first an earth, there cannot follow any resurrection in our work. For earth contains that natural Balsam and is the Salt of those who sought for it by a knowledge of all things (or universal knowledge). The final judgment of the world will be by fire; that which the great Creator at first made from nothing must by fire be turned

[T]urned to ashes again; out of these ashes the Phoenix brings forth her chicks again. For these ashes really contain the true <term>Tartar</term>, which must be dissolved; after its dissolution, the firm and strong lock of the royal room is opened.

A new heaven and a new earth are made after that great combustion, or burning, and the new man will appear more gloriously than he was in the first world, because in the other he is clarified.

If ashes and sand are well-ripened and digested by fire, then the Artist turns them into glass, which afterward holds in the fire; in its color, it is like a transparent stone, and looks no more like any ashes. This is a huge mystery to ignorant men, but not so to knowing men, for they found it to be so by their daily experience and manual practices.

Men burn lime from stones to make use of them for a cement in buildings. Before the fire prepares it for that purpose, it is a stone, and cannot be used for a cement as long as it is a hard stone. Fire brings stones to a maturity, and they receive from the fire a very hot degree, whereby it is strengthened and grows so potent that there is almost nothing comparable to it: the fiery spirit of lime.

Everything being reduced to ashes provides by Art a Salt; if you, at the deconstructing of it, are able to keep its <term>Sulphur</term> and <term>Mercury</term> apart and make restitution thereby to the Salt, according to Art, then fire will bring it to that again which it was before its deconstruction and destruction. Worldly-wise men call this a folly, counting it mere lies; they call it a new Creature, which man has no grant from God to make. They themselves do not understand that this Creature has been formerly so, and the Artist shows its increase only by the seed of Nature.

That Artist who lacks ashes cannot make any Salt for our Art, because our work cannot be made lively without our Salt; for the <term>coagulation</term> of things is worked merely by the Salt. As Salt preserves things from <term>putrefaction</term>, even so the

Note further, you who intend to learn this Art: the <term>Salt out of ashes</term> is of great efficacy, and many virtues are hidden within it. Yet the salt is worth nothing unless its innermost part is turned to the outside. For the spirit alone is what provides power and virtue; the naked body is able to do nothing here. If you know how to obtain that, then you have the <term>Philosophers' Salt</term> and their <term>incombustible Oil</term>, about which many before me have written great volumes.

## The fifth Key.

The life of the earth makes vegetables spring up, and he who says that the earth is dead tells an untruth; for a dead thing cannot impart any liveliness to another, and growth stops in dead things because the spirit of life has fled. The spirit is the life and soul of the earth, which dwells in her and receives its efficacy upon earthly things from <term>heavenly Astrals</term>. For all vegetables, metals, and minerals receive their power, growth, and nourishment from the spirit of the earth. For the spirit is the life, which is fed by <term>Astrals</term>, and which further imparts nourishment to growing things. As the child lies hidden in the mother’s womb and is fed there by the mother, so the earth feeds

<meta>This page continues the Fifth Key, explaining how the spirit of the earth—received from the heavens—is the true source of growth for minerals and vegetables. It defines the "Mercury of Philosophers" as a vital spirit extracted from matter, which serves as the root of life.</meta>

# The fifth Key. 21

...nourishes minerals also, which lie hidden in her belly by a spirit that she receives from above. The earth affords no power *per se* [of itself], but the living spirit that dwells in her does it; and if she should lack her spirit, then she would be dead and could afford no nourishment, because the spirit—which preserves living powers and drives forth vegetables and other growing things by a nutrient—is taken away from her sulfur, or fatness.

Two contrary spirits may dwell together in one subject, but they are still at variance, as in gunpowder. When ignited, these two spirits fly asunder, making a great noise and flying into the air, and are no longer discerned; nobody can tell where they have gone or what they had been, if it were not known through experience what kind of spirits they were and in what subject they dwelled.

From this you may learn that life is a mere spirit, and all those things that the ignorant world considers dead must be brought into an incomprehensible, visible, spiritual life and must be preserved within it, if life is to work with life. The spirits, which are fed and nourished by a heavenly dew, are born of one elemental, heavenly, and earthly substance, which is called *materia informis*.

And just as a magnet belongs to iron—which by reason of its own wonderful, invisible love is of an attractive quality—so our gold has a magnet also; this magnet is the *prima materia* of our great stone. If you understand this expression correctly, then you may be blessed with riches in this world.

I must present one more declaration to you in this chapter: a man who looks into a mirror sees a reflection of his image, but it is not palpable, except for the glass and the person looking into it. Likewise, from this matter must be expelled a visible spirit that is incomprehensible. That same spirit, I say, is the root of the life of our body and the *Mercury of Philosophers*, from which the liquid water in our Art is prepared

For our beginning is a closed-up, comprehensible body; its middle is a volatile spirit, and in the golden water there is no corrosiveness at all, by which our philosophers prolonged their lives. But the end of it is a super-fixed medicine for human and metallic bodies; this knowledge indeed fits angels better than man. Truly, men attain that knowledge also, obtaining it from God by their earnest prayers—those who are thankful to him for it and beneficial to the needy.

At the closing, I tell you as a certain truth that one work must beget the other: for our matter, at the beginning of our work, must be purified in the best manner, then opened, broken, and destroyed, and reduced to dust and ashes. All this being done, then make of it a volatile spirit as white as snow, and another volatile spirit as red as blood; these two spirits contain a third, and yet are but one spirit. These are the three spirits which preserve and increase life; join these together, provide them with their natural, necessary food and drink, and keep them warm in the bed of wedlock until their perfect birth; then you will see and find what the Creator and Nature have allowed you to know. And know that I never made so plain a revelation: God has incorporated more operations and wonders into Nature than thousands may believe. There is a seal and lock set before me, preventing me from saying more, so that others also may write of marvelous things which are naturally permitted by the Creator, but which ignorant men consider to be supernatural. For natural things have their first beginning from supernatural ones, yet both together are found to be merely natural.

## The sixth Key.

Man without a woman is but half a body, and so the woman without the man is but half a body either, for each apart can produce no fruit; but living together in a matrimonial way, then is their body perfect, and by their seed they may expect an increase.

If too much seed be cast on a ground, so that that acre is overburdened, no firm fruit can be expected, and if there be too little of the seed, then is the fruit thin also; the weeds grow then abundantly, and from that also no great goodness can be expected.

He that will not burden his conscience with any sins in the selling of wares, let him be just in his dealing, having just measures and just weights; then he avoids men’s curses and gets the prayers of the poor. In deep waters men are easily drowned, and shallow waters are soon dried up by the heat of the Sun and are good for nothing.

For the obtaining of a wished-for aim and scope, care must be had that a certain measure or quantity be taken in the conjunction of the Philosophic liquid substance, so that the greater quantity does not overlay the lesser part and be suppressed thereby, and the increase and growing of it be obstructed. Let the lesser be not too weak for the bigger; let there be an equal domination. Too much rain spoils the fruit, and too great a drought hinders true maturity. Therefore, if *Neptune* has prepared a perfect water-bath, then take a just quantity of your *aqua permanens* <note>Permanent water; a fundamental alchemical solvent</note>; take great care that you do neither too much nor too little. A double fiery man must be fed with a white Swan; these must kill each other, and both must revive again, and the air of the four corners of the world must possess three parts of the closed-up dwelling of the fiery man, so that the Swan's

They say two men can master a third, especially if they have elbow room to vent their malice. From this, you are to know from a true basis that a double wind must come, called *Vulturnus* <note>The East-South-East wind</note>, then a single wind called *Notus* <note>The South wind</note>; these come rushing from the East and South and will create a stir, being robbed and their blowing or motion calmed, and the air is turned into water. Then you may trust that a spiritual thing will become a physical one, and that the number by the four seasons of the year in the fourth heaven will predominate, after the seven Planets have finished their ruling. It will finish its course in the lowest dwelling of the Palace and will hold in the highest fiery examination; then the two which went forth suppressed the third and consumed him.

Here in our mastery, an exact knowledge is required: for the division and conjunction must be rightly achieved if you intend to gain riches by your Art, and the Scales must not be falsified by unequal weights. This is the Rock spoken of in this Chapter, which you must finish without any defect, by the artificial heaven, with air and earth, with the true water and sensible fire, setting them in equal weights, about which I inform you truly.

## The seventh Key.

Natural heat preserves a man's life: for when natural heat is gone, then life is at an end. Natural fire, being moderately used, is a defense against cold: but an immoderate heat is destructive. There is no necessity that the Sun should touch the earth physically with its whole sub-

<summary>The Sixth Key concludes with the union of alchemical principles through specific winds and precise measurements, followed by the start of the Seventh Key regarding the vital role of moderate heat.</summary>

--- Page 245 ---
<meta>This page continues the Seventh Key, which discusses the necessity of natural heat and the interaction of the four elements in the process of maturation and growth.</meta>

substance, it is sufficient that the earth be strengthened by the rays which she emits onto the earth, and in that way performs her duty; for in that manner she has sufficient efficacy to carry out her office, bringing things to maturity by her <term>digestion</term>: for the distance of the air brings the solar rays into a balanced temperature, so by means of the air the fire works, and the air works by the help of fire.

Earth produces nothing without water, and water without earth can raise nothing either; now, just as these two cannot be without each other in the generating of fruits, neither can fire exist without air, nor air without fire: fire is lifeless without air, and without fire the air cannot show its due <term>calidity</term> and dryness.

The vine at its final ripening has need of a greater solar heat than it has at the beginning of the spring; and if the Sun operates well during the harvest, then the vine yields a better and stronger sap, which it does not do if the Sun's heat is lacking. The common people count all things dead in winter because frost has locked up the earth so that nothing can spring up; but when the spring season approaches, and the Sun in its ascent breaks the frost, all things turn to life again. Trees and herbs appear in their liveliness, and the animals which hid themselves from the frost creep forth again out of their caves and holes. Vegetables afford their new fragrance; their operation is apparent in their pleasant blossoms of several colors. Then the summer works further, bringing these blossoms to further ripeness into fruits, which is followed by a rich harvest; for which thanks are due to the Creator, who set these periods for Nature.

Thus one year works after the other, for so long until the Architect thereof pulls them down and the inhabitants of the earth be exalted by the glory of God; then all earthly Nature will be at an end in her working, and in its place there will be an infinite, eternal one. When the Sun in winter goes further off from us, she does not dissolve so

<summary>The author explains the interdependent relationship between the four elements and the sun's heat in fostering life and maturation. This natural cycle of growth and harvest is presented as a divine system that will eventually be replaced by an eternal state.</summary>

### The seventh Key.

...well the great snow; but approaching nearer to us, then the air grows warmer, and the snow is easily melted, and being turned to water, it is gone: for the weaker must give way to the stronger. The same order must be observed in the management of the fire, so that the moist liquor may not be dried up (<note>exiccated</note>) too suddenly, and the <term>Philosophical earth</term> be not too soon melted and dissolved; otherwise, your wholesome fishes would turn into scorpions. And if you intend to be a proper administrator in your office, then first take your spiritual water, over which the spirit moved at the beginning; shut the door of the stronghold upon it, because this heavenly place will be besieged by earthly enemies. Your heaven must be guarded with three bulwarks, and only one entrance must be strongly guarded by a watchman. All this being finished, then kindle the light of wisdom and look for the penny you lost; let the light be of such a size as you see there is need for. For you must know that creeping beasts and worms have their dwelling in a cold and moist earth; their condition and nature leads them to it. But man's habitation is upon the earth, according as his tempered and mixed condition requires; but angelic spirits, having not an earthly but an angelic body—not being in subjection to a sinful flesh as man is—are placed in a higher station and are able to endure both heat and cold in the upper and lower regions without any trouble. And when man is purified (<note>clarified</note>), then he will be able to do like these heavenly spirits: God rules heaven and earth and works all in all.

If we prove to be good governors of our souls, then we shall be God's children and heirs, to accomplish that which is impossible for us to do now—which cannot be done unless all the water is dried up, and heaven and earth, together with humanity, are judged by fire.

<header>The eighth Key.</header>

## The eighth Key.

No flesh, whether it is man’s or beast’s, can bring any further increase or propagation unless it first enters into putrefaction (<note>decomposition</note>). Similarly, all *Vegetables* (<note>plants</note>), unless their seeds are brought into putrefaction, cannot be increased. Many beasts and worms are generated by putrefaction; this mystery in *Nature* deserves admiration! *Nature* permits this because this living increase is for the most part found in the earth, which, along with other *Elements*, is so raised by spiritual seeds. To prove this with examples, women in villages know how to give instances in that particular: for they cannot hatch any *Chickens* unless they put the eggs into putrefaction (<note>the warmth-induced change of incubation</note>). If bread is put into honey, then the *Ants* are bred, which is one of *Nature's* mysteries! It is commonly seen that *Maggots* breed in flesh—in men, horses, and similar carcasses—and in *Apples*, *Pears*, etc.; and who is able to list all the kinds of worms which are generated by putrefaction?

Some *Vegetables* also grow in certain places where none ever grew formerly, nor were they sown in those places; they were produced solely by putrefaction. The reason for it is that the earth in such places is inclined toward it and is impregnated thereby, which the sidereal qualities have infused and wrought a seed into; specifically, these seeds putrefy in the earth and, by the elemental operation, generate a physical matter according to that matter's quality. Thus the *Astrals* (<note>astral influences</note>), together with the *Elements*, may raise a new seed where none existed before, which seed may be increased by a further putrefaction. But to man, it is not granted to stir up a new kind of seed, because he does not have the operation of the *Elements* and the astral substance at his command to fashion what he pleases. Thus, several sorts of *Herbs* are generated mere-

solely by putrefaction: And whereas the country people, holding it a mere custom, do not take it into further consideration, nor imagine any cause for it; therefore among the common people it has become merely a customary business. But you, who ought to know more than ordinary people, must consider it further and learn to know its cause and ground: how, and from what, these living creatures are generated by putrefaction; do not know it just because it is usual, but rather know it is a mystery in Nature, because every life comes from putrefaction.

Every Element *per se* (<note>by itself</note>) has its corruption and generation. Let the Artist be informed, and learn the sufficient ground why in every Element the other three are hidden: for air contains fire, water, and earth, which although it seems incredible, yet it is a truth; and fire contains air, water, and earth; and earth contains water, air, and fire; otherwise they would not generate; water also contains air, earth, and fire. Though every Element is *per se*, yet they are mixed: all of which is found true at distillings, where these Elements are thus separated.

To make this appear to the ignorant, who may cry out that I speak merely lies: if you intend to learn the anatomy of natural things and to separate the Elements, I tell you for a truth that at the distilling of earth there comes first the Element air, being the highest; then at a certain progress, there comes the Element water; the fire lies hidden in the air, because both are of a spiritual substance and love and embrace one another entirely; the earth remains at the bottom, in which lies hidden the glorious Salt. When you distill any water, air and fire come over at first, then the water; the body of the earth abides at the bottom.

The Element fire, if it be driven into a visible substance, may be received apart by extraction. In like manner the other three Elements do dwell in the air. For none of these can be without air; earth can produce nothing without air, fire does not burn, nor has it any life with-

Neither can air consume anything, nor dry up any moisture, unless it is done by a natural heat which is in the air: since heat and warmth are found in the air, therefore the element of fire must necessarily be in the air. For all hot and dry things are suitable for the fiery substance of things: he who denies this truth understands nothing of Nature's mysteries, nor does he know any basis for their properties.

You must understand that if anything is to be generated by putrefaction, it must be in this manner: Earth is brought by a secret moisture into a state of corruption, which is the beginning of putrefaction; for without moisture, which is the element of water, no true putrefaction can occur. Now, if any offspring shall come from there, it must come from a warm quality, as the element of fire must kindle and spread itself; for without a natural heat, nothing can be generated. And if that offspring is to have a living breath and motion, it cannot be without air; for if air were not cooperating, then the first composition from which the offspring should come would be choked and perish for want of air. Thus you see plainly that perfect creatures cannot exist without any of the four elements, each showing its operation in the other, which they produce in and through putrefaction; for from this point on, nothing can be brought to life without them. To show this to be true—that for a perfect birth and generation, all four elements are required—

Then note that as *Adam*, the first man, was created by the Creator from *Limus terrae* (<note>the mud of the earth</note>), there did not yet appear any sensible life before God had breathed upon him. Then a life appeared in that clod of earth; in that earth was the Salt, which is the body; the breathed-in air was *Mercury*, the spirit; by this inbreathing, the air immediately provided a proper and suitable <term>calidity</term> (<note>heat</note>), which was *Sulphur*, that is, fire; then he stirred. *Adam* showed by this stirring that a living soul had been infused into him. Thus *Adam* was first built and begotten out of earth, water, air, and fire, out of a soul, body, and spirit, raised from Mercury, Sulphur, and Salt. So *Eve*, the first woman, the Mother of us all, was composed of the same, being taken from *Adam*; thus *Adam* and *Eve* were built, which you must note very well.

To return now again to putrefaction, the seeker in Philosophy should know that in the same way no metallic seed can work, nor be increased, unless that metallic seed—first in and of itself, without any strange addition or mixture—is brought into a full putrefaction, any more than the seeds of animals and vegetables can bring their increase without putrefaction. These same metals also must reach their perfect operation by the help of the elements; not that the elements are the seed, but the metallic seed, which descended from a heavenly, astral, elemental substance and has come to a corporality, and must be further brought by the elements into such corruption and putrefaction.

Note this also: wine contains a volatile spirit. In its distillation, its spirit comes first and its phlegm last; but when wine is turned into vinegar by continued heat, then its spirit is no longer as volatile as before. In the distillation of vinegar, its phlegm and watery part come first, and its spirit last. Although the same matter is in the vessel, yet its condition is altered; it is no longer wine but has been transmuted by putrefaction into vinegar. That which is extracted from wine is of another nature and operation than that which is drawn from vinegar. For if <term>Vitrum Antimony</term> (<note>glass of antimony</note>) be extracted with wine, or spirit of wine, it causes many stools by purging, and also vomits, because its venom is not yet quite broken or destroyed. But if antimonial glass is extracted with distilled vinegar, that extraction is of a deep color; this vinegar being drawn off in a <term>Balneo Mariæ</term> (<note>water bath</note>), and the yellow remaining

The powder being well purified with distilled water to remove all its acidity, you then have a sweet powder which no longer causes any bowel movements, but is a rare medicine of admirable efficacy; it may well be regarded as a *miraculum Medicinae* <note>miracle of medicine</note>.

This wonderful powder dissolves in a humid place into a liquid which is of great use and efficacy in surgery, curing symptoms without causing any pain at all to the patients; enough has been said of this for now.

At the conclusion of this note, observe this principally: that there are heavenly creatures begotten whose lives are preserved by astral influences and are fed by the four elements. Afterward they die and putrefy; when this is done, the astral influences—by means of the elements—infuse life into these putrefied bodies once more. This life may return again to a heavenly state, which establishes its habitation in the highest place of the firmament. When this is done, you shall see that the life and body of the earthly is consumed by the heavenly, and that the earthly body has entered into a heavenly one.

### The ninth Key.

The highest planet of heaven, named *Saturn*, has the least authority in our *Magisterium*, and yet he is the chief key of the whole Art and is set on the lowest step. Although he soared by a nimble flight to the highest altitude beyond all lights, yet, at the clipping of his feathers, he must be brought to the lowest luster, and his corruption must be the way for his improvement. Black must be turned into white, and white must be brought into red, and must pass and run through all the colors of the other planets to attain, in the end, the courtly color of the triumphing King.

And I say this much: although *Saturn* appears to be of a despicable condition and is held in great contempt, yet he does

Some may hold this to be difficult to understand, and it is a hard saying; but seeing that the matter is common, men must stretch their wits on this point. Just as an unequal state must exist in the world—for there must be a difference between master and servant, and they must be distinct in their service.

*Saturn* contains several sorts of colors brought forth by Art, such as black, gray, white, yellow, red, and others besides. Likewise, the Philosophers' matter must overcome and pass through many colors before the Great Stone can be exalted to its perfection. For as often as a new gate for entrance is opened to the fire, it provides a new fashion for a garment, as if for a prize, so that at last the poor laborer attains riches and no longer needs to go borrowing from his neighbors for a livelihood.

When Lady *Venus* possesses her Kingdom and, according to the custom observed in Royal Courts, distributes the offices as is fitting, then they make an appearance in their glory: *Music* carries a flag of red color, on which is painted *Charity*, beautiful in a green garb. In her Court, *Saturn* is in the position of the *Steward*; when he is in his office, then *Astronomy* carries a black flag before him, on which is painted *Faith* in a yellow and red garb. *Jupiter* with his scepter is *Marshal*; *Rhetoric* carries before him a flag of gray color, on which is painted *Hope*, curiously decorated in colors. *Mars* is expert in warlike affairs and bears sway in a fiery thirstiness; *Geometry* carries before him a bloody flag, on which is painted *Fortitude* in a red garb. *Mercury* is *Chancellor*; *Arithmetic* carries a flag before him of all manner of colors; on it is painted *Temperance*, dressed in colors. *Sol* is *Viceroy* of the Kingdom; *Grammar* carries a yellow flag before him, on it is

The meaning is that one planet must dispossess and displace another from its glory, office, place, and power, so that the best may finally rise to the highest power. In their best fixed color, granted to them by their first mother out of innate constancy, love, and friendship, they may achieve victory. Then the old world has passed, and a new world has come in its place; one planet has spiritually consumed the others. Only the strongest remains, sustained by the nourishment the others provided, and so two and three are conquered by One.

In closing, you must understand that you are to raise the heavenly scales. In one, place *Aries*, *Taurus*, *Cancer*, *Scorpio*, and *Capricorn*; in the other, you must lay *Gemini*, *Sagittarius*, *Aquarius*, *Pisces*, and *Virgo*. Then, ensure that the gold-rich *Leo* leaps into the lap of *Virgo*; then that scale will outweigh the other. Then, let the twelve signs of heaven, along with the *Septentriones*, come into an opposite aspect. Then, after the appearance of all kinds of colors, a conjunction will occur; the greatest will join with the lowliest, and the lowliest with the greatest.

## The tenth Key.

In our stone which I have made, and others also long before me, are locked up Elements, all mineral and metallic forms; indeed, all the properties and qualities of the world: for in it must be found the greatest and most powerful heat; for by its great internal fire the cold body of *Saturn* is warmed, and by that kindling and heating is transmuted into the best Gold. There must be found in it also the greatest coldness, because when it is added to *Venus*, it allays the degree of her heat, and does coagulate quick Mercury, and in that coagulation he is also transmuted into the best Gold. The reason for it is because nature has infused all these qualities into the matter of our great stone, which qualities must be digested and brought to maturity *per gradus ignis* <note>by degrees of fire</note>, and attain to the highest perfection. This does not come to pass unless Mount *Ætna* in *Sicily* is consumed by its fire, and no more cold is felt on the supposedly high Mountains, *Hyperboreis*, which place may also be called *Filictus*.

Fruits being plucked off before they are fully ripe are tough and shrink, and are unfit for use: if a Potter does not sufficiently burn his potter's wares, what use can they be put to? Our Elixir is in the same condition: it must be allowed a due time; it must not be shortened in its welfare; no false thing must be imposed upon it, else an aspersion of unworthiness will be cast upon it. For if blossoms are plucked off, we are sure that no fruits will grow on such Trees. Therefore, making haste in our *Magisterium* is not good; a hasty man seldom does any good work in our Art, because by making haste good things are spoiled. Let no seeker be deceived by greediness, either to take out

<meta>catchword: or</meta>

<summary>This page describes the Philosopher's Stone as containing all elemental qualities, specifically the extreme heat required to transmute Saturn and the extreme cold needed to coagulate Mercury. It warns the practitioner that the alchemical process requires patience and maturity, comparing haste to plucking unripe fruit or failing to fire pottery correctly.</summary>

<keywords>Tenth Key, Philosopher's Stone, transmutation, Saturn, Gold, Venus, Mercury, gradus ignis, Magisterium, alchemy, Mount Etna</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the Tenth Key, concluding the warning against rushing the alchemical process and describing the final perfection of the Great Stone as a universal medicine and transformative agent.</meta>

or to pluck off things before their time, so that the apple does not slip out of his hand and the stem <note>Original text "steel," an archaic term for a stalk or stele.</note> stays in his hand; for in good truth, if our stone is not sufficiently ripened, then what matter can it produce to any ripeness?

In water the matter is dissolved and is united by putrefaction; in the ashes it gets blossoms; in sand its superfluous humidity is dried out; a constant fire produces a fixed ripeness. It does not follow from this that the *Balneum Mariae*, horse-dung, ashes, and sands must necessarily be used, but only that the degrees and regulation of fire must be observed in such a manner. For the stone is made in an empty furnace of a threefold guard, firmly closed and locked up, and digested by a continuous fire, so that all vapors and fumes vanish, and the Garment of Honor appears in a rare splendor, remains in a place in the lowest part of heaven, and its movement comes to a halt. And when the King can no longer lift up his arms, then the glory of the world is conquered. The King has now come to an everlasting fixedness; nothing can endanger him anymore, because he has become invincible. To this I say: your earth, being dissolved in its own water, must be dried out in a suitable heat; then the air will breathe a new life into it. Once it is made alive, then you have a matter which must go by no other name than the Great Stone of the World, which penetrates human and metallic bodies like a spirit and is a universal medicine without any defect. It expels evil things, keeping and preserving the good ones. It is also a transmutation of bad things into goodness; its color ranges from a transparent redness to a dark brown, from a ruby-red to a garnet; and it is of an exceeding ponderousness and is exceptionally heavy.

He who obtains this stone, let him return thanks to the Creator of all creatures for such a heavenly balsam. Let him make good use of it for both himself and others, so that his needs being served with it here, he may fare well also in the other world. God be praised for his inexpressible benefits forevermore: Amen.

# The eleventh Key.

**T**his Key indicates the <term>augmentation</term> of our great stone, which I present to you by way of comparison. There dwelt in the *Orient* an excellent champion named *Orpheus*, who was very rich and had great dominions. He took his own sister, named *Eurydice*, in marriage and lived with her as his wife. Because he could not have any offspring by her—the cause of which he believed to be the sin he committed by choosing his own sister to be his wife—he constantly begged the Highest with great earnestness, wrestling with Him for a blessing in that regard.

Being once overtaken by a deep sleep, in his dream there came a flying man to him named *Phoebus*. He approached his feet, which were very warm, and said to him: "You noble champion, you have traveled over many kingdoms and countries, many cities and potent dominions; you have undergone great hardships at sea and risked many battles in war, which allowed you to reach that noble state. You were chosen before others to be dignified with honor and received much praise because of the valor you showed in those warlike actions. Therefore, the Father in heaven has commanded me to show you that your prayers were heard, and you have obtained this grant: you are to take the blood out of your right side, and the blood of your wife's left side, and the blood which remained in the hearts of your father and mother. These are but two by nature's right, and are but one sort of blood; unite these together, and let it enter again into the <term>globe of the seven wise masters</term>, closed nakedly. Then is the mighty one fed with his own flesh and drenched with his own blood of honor. If you proceed well in this, then you shall have a great inheritance and father an infinite multitude descending from your own body."

<summary>The Eleventh Key uses an allegory involving Orpheus and Eurydice to describe the augmentation or multiplication of the philosopher's stone. It details a divine vision where Orpheus is instructed to combine the "blood" of the husband, wife, and parents within a sealed vessel to achieve infinite growth.</summary>

<keywords>augmentation, Orpheus, Eurydice, Phoebus, alchemical marriage, blood of the stone, seven wise masters, multiplication, alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 257 ---
<meta>This page continues the conclusion of the eleventh key, detailing the outcome of the champion’s vision and the author’s final advice to the reader before beginning the twelfth and final key.</meta>

Yet know that the last seed, in the eighth revolution of time—the first seed out of which you were made in the beginning—will bring its course to an end. If you do this more often, and always begin <term>de novo</term>, then you shall see your children's children. A Macrocosm generated by a Microcosm is plentifully filled, and the Kingdom of the great Creator is fully possessed.

This being ended, *Phoebus* fled away. The champion awakened and arose from his bed; having done all as he was commanded, the champion had not only good success and prosperity in all his undertakings, but God also blessed him with many children by his wife. By their father’s testament, these also grew great and famous; that nobility remained in that generation, and they were blessed with great riches forever.

Seeker of this Art, if you have understanding and wit, you do not need any further interpretation of it; if you lack that wit, do not blame me, but your own ignorance. For I am forbidden to open the lock any further; I must, and will, obey. It is set down plainly enough for those whom God intends to bless with it; and it is so plain that men will hardly believe it. I have set down the whole process figuratively in the manner which my predecessors observed before me, and I have done it more plainly than they did because I concealed nothing. If you have pulled away the veil from your eyes, you will find that which many sought for and was found by very few. For the matter is named entirely, and the beginning, middle, and end of the process are shown also.

## The twelfth Key.

**A** fencer who does not know well how to use his weapons must necessarily be beaten by one who knows better how to use them; he who learned in the fencing school

<meta>catchword: school</meta>

<summary>The author concludes the parable of the eleventh key, asserting that the alchemical process has been revealed plainly for those with the wit to understand it. He then begins the twelfth key by using the analogy of a fencer to illustrate the necessity of skill and knowledge.</summary>

school perfectly how to use all manner of weapons, he will get the garland in that school.

In the same way, he who has obtained a tincture by God's blessing, but does not know how to use it, is in the same condition as the fencer who is unskillful in the use of his weapons.

This being the twelfth and last Key, aiming to finish this book of mine, I will not lead you any longer by philosophical allegorical expressions, but will reveal to you the Key of the Tincture in a full, real process. Therefore, follow this, my ensuing doctrine, which is this:

The Medicine and well-prepared Philosopher's stone having been made of the true virgin's milk, which was fully prepared, then take one part of the best and finest gold, purified through antimony, and laminate it as thinly as it can possibly be beaten. Put these together in a vessel or melting pot. At first, let your fire be gentle for twelve hours; then let it continually stay in the melting for three days and nights. Then the purged gold and Stone are turned into a pure medicine of a subtle, spiritual, and penetrating quality; for without the ferment of gold, the Medicine or Stone cannot easily create the tincture, being too subtle and too penetrative. But being fermented with its like, the finished tincture has gained an entry to work into the other. Then take one part of the prepared ferment to a thousand parts of melted metal which you intend to tinge: I tell you for a certain truth, all will be transmuted into perfect fixed gold; for the one body willingly embraces the other, even if it is not of its kind, yet it joins with it by force and must become like it—and like must be produced from like.

To him who makes use of this method, all fixations are revealed: the porches at the ends have their exits; no creature is comparable to this subtlety: it is ALL in ALL, and according to its natural descent, it contains and possesses all that may be found under the sun.

O! Beginning of the first beginning! Consider the end: ☉! End of the last ends, consider the beginning: forget not

# The Eleventh Key.

...to remember the middle in all fidelity: God the Father, Son, and Holy Ghost grant unto you things needful for your Spirit, Soul, and Body.

---

### Of the first matter of the Philosopher's Stone.

There is found a stone, which is not costly,
*Out of it is drawn a flying fire,*
*Of which the stone itself is made*
*Of white and red together joined.*
*It is a stone, and not a stone,*
*In it Nature works alone,*
*Out of it springs a fountain clearly,*
*Which drowns its fixed father fully.*
*His life and body are both devoured,*
*At last his soul to him is restored.*
*To whom his flying mother has become*
*Like, in his own kingdom.*
*Himself also in quality and might*
*Has gained a greater strength,*
*The son in old age does excel*
*The mother, who is made volatile,*
*By Vulcan's art, but first however*
*By the spirit must the father be born.*
*Body, soul, spirit, consist in two;*
*The whole business goes to and fro.*
*Comes only from one, which is merely*
*A thing that fixes flying matters fully.*
*They are two and three, and yet but one—*
*Conceive of it rightly, or else you hit none!*
Set Adam into a water bath,
In which *Venus* has her fellow,
Which the old *dragon* has prepared,
Of which his strength could not be spared.

### A short way and
# REVIEW
### Of former Writings of
## BASILIUS VALENTINUS.

<image-desc>An ornamental woodcut initial 'I' set within a square frame decorated with foliage and floral patterns.</image-desc> I, *Basilius Valentinus*, have formerly written a treatise regarding what discoveries the ancients made in their philosophical expressions about obtaining that mighty treasure, whereby true philosophers prolonged their lives in health, while also enjoying an abundance of riches.

The Highest in Heaven, before whom all secrets are open, I call to witness that I wrote no untruths but revealed the naked truth, so much so that people of understanding need no further light than what I have set down in my theory, which was supported and confirmed by the practice of my Twelve Keys. These might be sufficient for them; however, the restlessness of my mind still urged me to reveal the said treatise further through this short review, thereby to purify and increase the kindled light, so that seekers after wisdom may have a brighter light for finding the secrets they delve into. Some may look on it with an evil eye, thinking that these mysteries were made too plain already, and blame me for my honest dealing. Let such people know that to hardened individuals these writings will be difficult enough yet, and they will not reach the secrets they aim for; but to the adopted sons they are clear enough. Therefore, listen to me and follow my doctrines, then you will

be sure to find the true way to the sheepfold. I have written no more than I shall testify to, and own at the day of Resurrection.

This short way is faithfully shown in the following instruction, in a plain-dealing expression, avoiding an eloquent style.

I have told you formerly, that all things are composed of three, *namely* of Mercury, Sulphur, and Salt, and it is so, as I told you.

But note also, that the Stone is made of one, two, three, four, and five: by the word five, I mean the Quintessence; by the word four, are understood the Elements; by three, are meant the *principles*; by two is meant the double *mercurial* substance; by one is meant the first principle of all things, which proceeds from the word at the first Creation, *Fiat*, "Let there be."

Some may hold these expressions to be very intricate, as if there were no sense nor ground for what has been said; for the clearing of these doubts, I will speak briefly of *Mercury*, secondly of Sulphur, and thirdly of Salt; for these are the principles of the matter of our Stone.

In the first place you must note, that common Mercury does not avail here; but our *Mercury* is made of the best of Metals, by the spagyrick Art, as pure, subtle, and clear as any well water of a crystalline transparency, without any impurity. Make of it a water, or incombustible oil; for Mercury's first beginning was a water, as all philosophers testify to my saying. In this Mercurial oil must be dissolved its own Mercury, out of which that water was made. This Mercury must be precipitated with its own oil; then you have a double Mercurial essence.

Note, I hold in my second Key that Gold, after it is purified according to the tenor of the second Key, must be reduced into a special water, and then reduced into a subtle Calx <note>A powder produced by roasting or chemically treating a metal</note>, of which the fourth Key speaks. This Calx must be driven over through the Helmet <note>The head or cap of a distilling vessel or alembic</note> by a spirit of Salt <note>Hydrochloric acid</note>, and precipitated again, and by reverberating <note>Heating in a furnace where the flame is reflected onto the material</note> must be brought to a

## *Now follows the discussion of Sulphur.*

For this <term>sulphur</term>, you must look in the same <term>metal</term>. That <term>metal</term> must be purified and destroyed in a <term>reverberating fire</term>, then extracted from its body, leaving no corrosiveness in it, of which I gave a hint in the *third Key*. This <term>sulphur</term> must afterwards be dissolved in its own blood, from which it gained its fixedness, as indicated in the *sixth Key*, in the proper quantity. When this is done, you have dissolved and fed the true <term>lion</term> with the blood of the <term>green lion</term>. For the fixed blood of the <term>red lion</term> is made from the unfixed blood of the <term>green lion</term>. These are of one nature; the unfixed blood makes the fixed one volatile, and the fixed one makes the volatile fixed, just as it was before its dissolution. Let it stand together in a gentle heat so that all the <term>sulphur</term> is dissolved. Then you have the second ferment, feeding fixed <term>sulphur</term> with an unfixed one. All <term>philosophers</term> agree with my saying. The same is distilled with <term>spirit of wine</term> and is as red as blood, being called *<term>aurum potabile</term>*, from which no reduction to any physical body can be expected anymore.

---

## *Now I also declare what the Philosophers' Salt means.*

Salt causes fixation and volatility, according to how it is ordered and prepared. For the spirit from <term>salt</term> and tar-

The <term>Salt of Tartar</term> *by itself* fixes mightily, especially if the heat of *<term>Calx vive</term>* <note>quicklime</note> is incorporated with it, for both are of a high degree for fixation. The vegetable salt of wine has this fixing quality, and according to a special preparation, brings fixed things to a state of volatility: which is a mystery in Nature, and a miracle in the *philosophical art*.

If there is a salt made of the urine of a man who drinks nothing but wine, this salt, being volatile, also brings fixed things to a state of volatility and brings them over <note>distills them</note>, but does not fix them. Although the person from whose urine the salt is made had drunk nothing but wine, yet that salt differs much from that which is made of <term>Tartar</term>; for man performed one transmutation in his body—namely, he turned the vegetable salt into an animal spirit of salt, making flesh, fatness, etc., just as horses and other beasts do when feeding on grass, hay, etc.

Likewise, bees make honey from the best flowers and herbs; it is the same with the rest. The reason for this Key lies in putrefaction, from which this separation and transmutation take their origin.

Ordinary spirit of salt, being distilled in a special manner, makes gold and silver volatile if a small quantity of *<term>Dragon's spirit</term>* is added to it. It dissolves them and brings them over through the helmet <note>The cap of a distilling apparatus</note>; the *<term>Eagle</term>* does the same with the *<term>Dragon spirit</term>*, which creeps in and out of the stone cliffs. But if anything is melted with salt before its spirit is separated from the body, then it fixes the substance rather than making it volatile.

I also say this: if the spirit of common salt is united with spirit of wine and both are distilled a third time, then it loses its tartness and grows sweet. This prepared spirit does not corrode the *<term>Solar body</term>* <note>Gold</note>, but being poured on a subtly prepared <term>Gold Calx</term> <note>Gold powder</note>, it extracts its highest red tincture, which, being made correctly, can bring

Note further that the spirit of salt also destroys *Lune*, bringing her into a spiritual substance according to my instruction after it is prepared, out of which potable *Lune* is afterward made. This spirit is assigned to the spirit of *Lune* and *Sol* <note>gold</note>, like man and wife, by means of the conjunction of the Mercurial spirit, or its oil.

The spirit of the Philosophers' Salt grants success in coagulation. It is true that the spirit of Tartar, the spirit of wine, and the true *acetum* <note>vinegar</note> are able to achieve much, for the spirit of *acetum* is of a cold quality, and the spirit of *Calx vive* <note>quicklime</note> is very hot; these are of contrary dispositions. Now I have spoken according to the custom of philosophers; it is not fitting for me to be more plain and to show the world how the doors of wisdom are bolted.

As a farewell, take this in sincerity: seek for your matter in metallic substances; make of them a *Mercury*; ferment one Sulphur with Mercury; ferment that with its own Sulphur; bring that into an order with Salt; distill them over jointly; join all proportionally. All will become one, which at first came from one. Coagulate and fix it in continued heat; increase and ferment it a third time, according to the meaning of my two last Keys. Then you will find the end you look for. Regarding how this tincture is to be used, let the twelfth Key satisfy you with its certain process.

### Thanks be to God.

**F**or a final conclusion, be assured that out of black *Saturn* <note>lead</note> and bountiful *Jupiter* <note>tin</note>, a spirit can be distilled which is afterward turned into a sweet oil as its noblest part. This specifically takes away the fluid quality of *Mercury*, making it fixed and improving it; I have told you of this in my other writings.

---

### Additions.

**H**aving thus obtained your matter, then watch the fire closely and manage it skillfully: for that is of the greatest importance at the end of the work. Our fire is not a common fire, and our furnace is not a common furnace. Though philosophers before me have written that our fire is no common fire, I tell you in good earnest that, according to their custom, they kept all mysteries secret because the matter is lowly and the work is easy—facilitated and accomplished by the management of fire; therefore, they refrained from telling the plain truth.

Lamp-fire made from spirit of wine <note>alcohol</note> is useless; the expenses for it would be excessive. Horse dung is only a waste, which cannot complete the work through any perfect or certain stages.

Furnaces of various kinds are useless: for in our threefold furnace certain stages are observed. Do not let idlers convince you to use such furnaces: for our furnace is a simple one, our fire is a simple fire, and our matter is a simple matter. The glass is compared to the circumference of the earthly globe. You do not need to look any further for more information concerning the fire, its management, and the furnace.

There is no need to write more books about it; only look well to the management of the fire, and learn to distinguish between cold and warm. If you are expert and exact in this, then your work will be well finished, and the Art brought to its conclusion. May the Creator of nature be praised forever. Amen.

---

### Of Mercury.

There are several sorts of *Mercury*. Mercury of animals and vegetables is merely a vapor of an incomprehensible being, unless it is caught and reduced to an oil; then it is ready for use. But mercury of metals is of another condition, as is also that of minerals: though it also may be compared to a vapor, yet it is comprehensible and fluid. One mercury is better and nobler than another: for *Solar Mercury* <note>mercury associated with or derived from gold</note> is the best of them all; next to that is *Lunar Mercury* <note>mercury associated with or derived from silver</note>, and so forth. There is also a difference among salts and sulphurs: among the mineral salts, the one that takes the prize is made of antimony; and that sulphur which is drawn from vitriol is preferred above all others. Mercury of metals is hot and dry, cold and moist; it contains the four qualities.

There are medicines prepared from it of a wonderful efficacy, in several sorts and forms, which is the reason why there is such a variety of virtues in it. In mercury lies hidden the highest *arcanum* <note>secret remedy or mystery</note> for human health, but it is not to be used crude; rather, it must first be prepared into its essence. It is sublimed with copper-water <note>likely a solution of vitriol or copper salts</note> and is further reduced into an oil. There is an oil made of it *per se* <note>by itself</note>, without any corrosiveness, which is pleasant and fragrant; several sorts of oils with additives can be made from it, good for many things. It is also prepared with gold, being first made

The mixed mercury serves for inward use.

Mercury being purged, is precipitated with the blood of *Venus* <note>likely a copper-based substance</note>, is well digested with distilled vinegar, and thus its corroding quality is taken off: Have a care what quantity you administer; if it is given in a true dose, then it does its part very well: but for its operation, it is not equally sublimed to the fixed; its coagulation is found in *Saturn* <note>lead</note>, its malleability is apparent when it is robbed of its life: it contains its own tincture upon white and red, being brought in its fixed coagulation to a white body, is tinged again by vitriol-water, and being reduced to gold, is graduated by antimony. Although that bloodthirsty Iron Captain <note>Mars/Iron</note> with his spear assaults *Mercury* very much, yet he alone cannot conquer him, unless *Saturn* comes in to hide him, and *Jupiter* <note>tin</note> commands peace with his scepter. Such process being finished, then the Angel *Gabriel*, the strength of the Lord, and *Uriel* the light of God has showed mercy to humble *Michael*; then *Raphael* can make right use of the highest medicine; nothing can prevail against the medicine. Thus much be spoken of *Mercury*: now I wing myself from here, and fly to a place where frost and heat can better be tolerated and endured.

### Of Antimony.

It falls very difficult to mechanics <note>practitioners or artisans</note> to have finished learning with their compasses: because that great Architect JEHOVAH has reserved many things for his own

power. In the same condition we find *Antimony*; it is very difficult to find out all the mysteries that are hidden within it; its virtue is miraculous, its power is great, its color hidden within it is various, and its crude body is poisonous, yet its essence is an antidote against poison and is like *Quick-silver*, which ignorant physicians can neither comprehend nor find; but the knowing physician believes it to be true, having made many experiments with it.

This mineral contains much *Mercury*, much *Sulphur*, and little *Salt*, which is the reason why it is so brittle and applicable: for there is no malleability in it, by reason of the small quantity of *Salt*. The greatest affinity it bears toward *Saturn* is by reason of *Mercury*: for Philosophers' Lead is made out of it, and it is attracted to *Gold* by reason of its *Sulphur*: for it purges *Gold*, leaving no impurity in it; there is an operation in it equal to *Gold* if it is well prepared and administered to man medicinally: it flies out of the fire, and stays firmly in the fire, if it is prepared accordingly. Its volatile spirit is poisonous and purges grievously, not without damage to the body; its remaining fixed part purges also, but not in the same manner as the former did; it does not provoke bowel movements, but merely seeks the disease wherever it is, penetrates the whole body and its members, suffers no evil to abide there, does not cause evacuation <note>The original "expels not" likely refers to emetic or outward purging</note>, and brings the body to a better condition.

In brief, *Antimony* is the Lord of medicines; from it is made a *Regulus* out of *Tartar* and *Salt*; if, during the melting of *Antimony*, some iron filings are added by a technique used, there comes forth a wonderful star, which philosophers before me called the *Signat* star: this star, being several times melted with cold *Earth-salt*, then grows yellowish, is of a fiery quality, and of a wonderful efficacy: its *Salt* afterward yields a liquor, which further is brought to a fixed incombustible oil, which serves for several uses.

Curious flowers, either red, yellow, or white, according to how the fire has been governed. These flowers being extracted, and the extract, without any addition *per se* being driven into an oil, have an admirable efficiency. This extraction may be made also with vinegar of crude antimony, or of its *regulus*; but it requires a longer time, neither is it so good as the former preparation.

And being reduced into a <term>philistea</term>, there is a glass made of it *per se*, of which I made mention in my eighth Key, which is extracted also; then abstracted, there remains a powder of incredible operation, which may safely be used after it has been edulcorated. This powder, being dissolved, heals wounds, sores, etc., causing no pains: this powder being extracted once more with spirit of wine, or driven through the helmet with some other matter, affords a sweet oil; to speak further of it is needless.

Antimony is melted also with cold <term>earth-salt</term>, dissolved and digested for a time in spirit of wine; it affords a white fixed powder that is effectual against <term>morbus Gallicus</term>, breaking inward abscesses; it has several virtues besides. You must learn to prepare antimony yourself; lay hands on it, dive into its inward qualities, and you will meet with wonderful matters: for my conscience will not suffer me to discover all its qualities: I do not desire to bring the physicians' curses upon me, who were at great expense and toiled much in burning of coals for its preparation, if I should rob them of their livelihood. Therefore, learn you also as your predecessors did; seek as I have done; then you will find also what others have told of.

There is an oil also made of antimony, with the <term>flying dragon</term> being added to it, which, being rectified thrice, is then prepared: though a cancer were ever so bad, and the *wolf* <note>A term for a corrosive or "eating" ulcer</note> ever so biting, yet these with all their fellows, be they fistulas or old ulcers, must fly and be gone. The little powder of the <term>flying dragon</term> prepared with the <term>lions' blood</term> must be administered also, three or four grains for a dose, according to the party's age and complexion.

A further process may be made with this oil with the addition of a water made of <term>stone serpents</term> and other necessary spices—not those which are transported from the Indies. This powder is of such efficacy that it radically cures many chronic diseases.

There is made a red oil of antimony, <term>calx vive</term> <note>quicklime</note>, <term>sal-ammoniac</term>, and common sulfur, which has performed great cures in old ulcers. With stone salt, or with common salt, a red oil is forced from antimony, which is admirably good for outward symptoms.

There is made a sublimate of antimony, with <term>spirit of tartar</term> and <term>salmiac</term>, after being digested for a time, which, by means of *<term>Mars</term>* <note>iron</note>, is turned into quick mercury. This antimonial mercury has been sought by many, but few have obtained it; this is the reason why its praise is not publicized, much less is its operative quality known. If you know how to precipitate it well, then your arrow will hit the bullseye to perform strange matters; its qualities ought not to be made common.

It is needless to describe its combustible sulfur—how that is made of antimony is easy and known—but that which is fixed is a secret and hidden from many. If an oil be made of it in which its own sulfur is dissolved, and these are fixed together, then you have a medicine of rare qualities in virtue, operation, and ability, far beyond vegetables.

Quicksilver being imbibed with quick sulfur, melted with antimony for some hours in a wind-oven, and the salt of the remainder being extracted with distilled vinegar, you then have the *<term>Philosophers' Salt</term>*, which cures all manner of agues.

There is an *<term>acetum</term>* <note>vinegar</note> made of antimony, of an acidity like other *acetums*; if its own salt be dissolved in this *acetum* and distilled over, then this *acetum* is sharpened, which is an excellent cooler in hot swellings and other inflamed symptoms around wounds, especially if an ointment is made of it together with *<term>anima of Saturn</term>* <note>the essence or "soul" of lead</note>.

<header>A short way and Repetition, etc.</header>

The Quintessence of Antimony is the highest medicine, the noblest and most subtle found in it, and is the fourth part of an *universal* medicine. Let the preparation of it remain a mystery; its quantity, or dose, is three grains. There belong four instruments to the making of it; the furnace is the fifth, in which *Vulcan* dwells. The manuals and the government of fire provide for the ordering of it.

You physicians, if you are wise, seek out this medicine in that subject where it lies and may be found best and most effectively. I refrain from speaking further of antimony; let *Justinian* judge of the rest.

### Of Copper-water.

If I could prevail with *Apollo* to be merciful, and to give liberty to his *Muse* to assist me in describing art and wisdom, then I would bring an offering to *Minerva*, whereby the gods of wisdom might take notice of a grateful mind for the gifts they had bestowed; and I would write of a mineral whose salt is set forth in the highest manner, whose great and good qualities are of such transcendency that reason is not able to comprehend or conceive of them. It went generally by the name of copper-water; to make the meaning and sense of it plain, let it be known and be thus informed, that *Vitriol* contains two spirits, a white and a red one. The white spirit is the white sulfur upon white; the red spirit is the red sulfur upon red. He who has ears, let him hear!

Observe it diligently, and remember every word, for they are of a large extent; every word is as ponderous as a <term>centner stone</term> <note>a stone weighing a hundredweight, or approximately 100 pounds</note>. The white spirit is sour and causes an appetite and a good digestion in a man's stomach. The red spirit is even sourer and is more ponderous than the white; in its distilling, a longer fire must be continued, because it is more fixed in its degree. From the white, by distilling of sulfur...

From Silver <note>Lune</note> is made *argentum potabile* <note>drinkable silver</note>. In the same way, gold—being destroyed in the spirit of common salt, made spiritual by distilling, and having its sulfur taken from it and joined with a red spirit in a proper dose so that it may be dissolved, then for a time putrefied in spirit of wine to be further digested and often abstracted until nothing remains at the bottom—then you have made an *aurum potabile* <note>drinkable gold</note>, about which great volumes have been written, though very few of their processes were correct. Note that the red spirit must be rectified to remove its acidity and be brought into a sweetness, subtly penetrating, with a pleasant taste and sweet fragrance.

I have told you great matters now, which slipped from me against my intention; the sweet spirit is made of sulfur of vitriol, which is combustible like other sulfur before it is destroyed. For the sulfur of philosophers (note this well) is not combustible; its preparation does not need to be written down, as it is easy and requires no great pains or expenses to get a combustible sulfur out of vitriol.

This sweet oil is the essence of vitriol, and it is a medicine worthy of being called the third pillar of the universal medicine. The salt is drawn from Colchotar <note>the residue remaining after the distillation of vitriol</note> and is dissolved in the red or white oil, or in both, and is distilled again. If it is fermented with *Venus* <note>copper</note>, it performs its office very well, for it produces a medicine which, during melting, tints pure iron into pure copper. Colchotar of sulfur provides true foundations for the healing of perished wounds, which otherwise are hardly brought to any healing. For such sores which, by reason of a long-continued "white redness," will admit no healing, Colchotar provides an entry, setting a new foundation. That quality and virtue is not in the Colchotar itself, but the spirit together with the salt are the masters that dwell within it.

A vitriol of a high degree is made of copper and verdigris, and its tincture is far-reaching. There is a

Vitriol is also made of iron, which is of a strange quality: iron and copper are very closely related, belonging together like man and wife. This mystery should have been concealed, but because it is of great importance, I could not help but speak of it.

When vitriol is corroded with *Salmiac* <note>ammonium chloride</note>, a combustible sulfur arises in its sublimation along with its mercury, of which there is but little because it consists mostly of sulfur. If that same sulfur is set free again by the *Eagle* <note>a chemical solvent or process, often associated with mercury</note> with spirit of wine, a medicine can be made from it, as I told you previously. Although there is a new way to make a combustible sulfur out of vitriol—such as by precipitation following a prior dissolution by the salt or liquor of tartar, as well as by a common *lixivium* <note>alkaline solution or lye</note> made of beech-ashes—yet this is the better method because the body of vitriol is better and more opened with the key of the *Eagle*.

There are other mysteries hidden in vitriol which are excellent in your practical work and are apparently known as *Venus* <note>copper</note> and *Mars* <note>iron</note> bear real record in their spirits; the same acknowledges *Sol* <note>gold</note> and *Lune* <note>silver</note>. But I do not intend at this time to write a complete book of surgery <note>Chirurgerie</note> or to make reports of details in praise of vitriol. I have already written too much about it; you must learn and also experience it. You will find that vitriol needs no advocate to speak for it, and it will sufficiently inform you of an absolute surgical <note>Chirurgick</note> book contained in its nature as a third part of the universal medicine against all manner of diseases.

In closing this, I tell you this much: there is neither a cold nor a moist quality found in its nature, but it is of a hot and dry substantial quality. This is the reason why, by its super-abounding heat <note>calidity</note>, it heats other things, digests them, and at last brings them to a full rarity, provided the fire is continued for a certain time.

The things I write about vitriol I have not begged nor borrowed from other men's writings, but I found them to be so through long-continued practice <note>practick</note>, whereby nature enabled me to become a truth-teller <note>Sooth-sayer</note> by permission of the highest Creator.

### Of Common Sulfur.

Usual common sulfur is not so perfectly exalted in its degree, nor brought to maturity, as it is found in antimony and vitriol. There is made of it *per se* <note>by itself</note>, an oil against putrid, stinking wounds, destroying and killing such worms that grow in them, especially if that little salt in it is dissolved from its sulfur.

A balsam is made of it with salad oil or oil of juniper, and in like manner with the white spirit of turpentine, and it is of a red color; it is made thus: take flowers of sulfur, made with the *colcothar* <note>the metallic residue after distillation</note> of *vitriol*, digest them for a time in horse dung or by any other method. This balsam may safely be used for those who are in a consumption of the lungs <note>tuberculosis</note>, especially if rectified several times with spirit of wine, distilled over, and separated so that it is blood red.

This balsam is a preservative against corruption and rottenness.

The quintessence of sulfur is in a mineral where a sulfurous flint is generated: these crushed pebbles being put in a glass, and a strong aqua fortis <note>nitric acid</note> poured over them,

Thus I conclude speaking any further of combustible sulfur. An oil may be made from it which is found very useful; the sulfur must be sublimed in a tall vessel with strong heat. This sublimation, over a long time, changes into a liquor or oil when standing in a damp place, but since I do not intend to be long-winded, let it rest there. A liver <note>liver of sulfur</note> may be cooked out of common sulfur, which is turned into milk <note>milk of sulfur</note>; and it may also be changed into a red oil with linseed oil. Many other medicines may be made out of sulfur: its flowers, essence, and oil are preferred above the rest, along with the white and red fixed cinnabar which are made from it, because a mighty virtue is found in them.

How this spirit is obtained, I have told you; observe it, keep it, and take it as a farewell.

### Of Arsenic.

Arsenic is in the kinship of mercury and antimony, just as a bastard in a family may be. Its whole substance is poisonous and volatile, even as the former two; in its external color to the eye, it is white, yellow, and red, but inwardly it is adorned with all manner of colors, like the metals it was obliged to forsake, being forced there-

## Of Saltpeter.

Two elements are predominant in me, fire and air; the lesser quantity is water and earth. I am fiery, burning, and volatile. There is in me a subtle spirit; I am altogether like Mercury, hot on the inside and cold on the outside, and am slippery and very nimble at expelling my enemies. My greatest enemy is common sulfur, and yet he is also my best friend; for being purged by him and clarified in the fire, I am then able to allay all heats of the body, within and without, and am one of the best medicines to expel and keep off the poisonous plague. I am a greater cooler outwardly than *Saturn* <note>lead</note>, but my spirit is hotter than any; I cool and burn according to how men will make use of me and according to how I am prepared.

When metals are to be broken, I must be a help, otherwise no victory can be obtained, whether the undertakings are great or small. Before I am destroyed, I am mere ice, but when I am

### Of Salarmoniac.

Salarmoniac is none of the least important keys to open metals with; therefore the ancients have compared it with a volatile bird. It must be prepared, otherwise you can do no feats with it; for if it is not prepared, it does more hurt than good to metals, and carries them away out of the chimney-hole. It can elevate and sublime with its swift wings the tincture of minerals, and of some metals, to the very mountains where a store of snow is found, usually even at the greatest heat of the summer. If it is sublimed with common salt, then it purges and clears, and may be used safely.

He who supposes he can transmute metals with this salt, which is so volatile, surely he does not hit the nail on the head, for it has no such power. But to destroy metals, and make them fit for transmutation, in that respect it has sufficient power: for no metal can be transmuted, unless it

## Of Tartar.

This salt is not listed in the book of minerals but is generated from a vegetable seed; yet its Creator has put such virtue into it that it bears a wonderful love and friendship toward metals, making them malleable. It purges *Luna* to a whiteness and incorporates into her such additions as are convenient for her; when digested for a time with minerals or metals, and then sublimed and vilified, they all become a quick *Mercury*. To do this, there is no other vegetable salt besides it—is this not a wonderful thing! The orator is yet to be born who shall be of such ability and eloquence as to sufficiently express all the mysteries hidden in it. But to make the philosopher's stone out of it is no such matter, since it is a vegetable, and that power is not given to any vegetable. It is a very good remedy in *medicine*, to be used inwardly and outwardly; its salt being made spiritual and sweet, it dissolves and breaks the stone in the bladder and dissolves the coagulated tartar of the gout settled in the joints or anywhere else. Its ordinary spirit, which is used for the opening of metals, being used and applied outwardly, also lays a foundation for the healing of such ulcers that hardly admit any healing, such as *fistulas*, *cancers*, *wolves* <note>A term for corrosive or "eating" ulcers, such as lupus.</note>, and the like. I know nothing more to

<summary>This page concludes the personified instructions on using Salarmoniac to refine metals and introduces Tartar, a vegetable salt used both to make metals malleable and as a potent medical remedy for stones, gout, and stubborn ulcers.</summary>
<keywords>Salarmoniac, Hydra, Tartar, Luna, Mercury, philosopher's stone, medicine, gout, fistulas, metallurgy</keywords>
