### Of Vinegar.

In Alchemy and *Medicine*, almost nothing can be prepared unless Vinegar lends a helping hand to it. Therefore, I thought it convenient to give it its due praise and commendation, especially to insert it here in this treatise. In *Alchemy*, it is used to set *Metals and Minerals* into *putrefaction*. It is also used to extract their essences and tinctures, once they have been prepared for it, just as the spirit of wine is commonly used to extract tinctures from vegetables.

In *Medicine*, it deserves praise as well, for it takes the pure from the impure and acts as a separator. It removes the sharpness and corrosiveness from Mineral Medicines, fixes that which is volatile, and is a great defense against poison, as I told you when I spoke of the *Antimonial glass*.

Vinegar is also used internally, and both humans and beasts are benefited by it. Outwardly, it is applied to hot inflammations and swellings as a coolant. Spirit of wine and Vinegar are of great use, both in *Alchemy* and *Medicine*; both have their descent from *urine* <note>In alchemical theory, these substances were often compared or linked through the process of fermentation and putrefaction.</note> and are of one substance, but they differ in quality because of the putrefaction the Vinegar acquired there, which I told you about previously.

I must acquaint you with one thing, which is this: this is not the *Philosophers' Vinegar*. Our Vinegar, or *acetum*, is another liquor—namely, a matter in itself. For the stone of the Philosophers is made out of the <term>Azot</term> <note>Also spelled Azoth; the essential agent of transformation in alchemy.</note> of the Philosophers, which must be prepared with ordinary distilled <term>Azot</term>, with spirit of wine, and with other waters besides, and must be reduced to a certain order.

Note this as a *memorandum*: if distilled pure Vinegar is poured upon destroyed <term>Saturn</term> <note>The alchemical name for lead.</note> and is kept warm in

Then extract two or three parts of that Vinegar and leave it in a cellar; then you will find white transparent stones, like crystals. These are an excellent cooler and healer of all adust <note>A medical term referring to symptoms caused by "burnt" or overly dry humors.</note> and inflamed symptoms. If these crystals are reduced into a red oil and poured upon *Mercury*, precipitated by *Venus*, and proceeded with further as it ought to be—if that is done correctly—then neither *Sol* nor *Lune* will hinder you from getting riches.

### Of Wine.

The true vegetable stone is found in Wine, which is the noblest of all vegetables: it contains three sorts of Salt, three sorts of Mercury, and three sorts of Sulphur.

The first Salt sticks in the wood of the Vine. If that is burnt to ashes and a <term>lixivium</term> made of it to have its salt drawn forth, which must be coagulated, this is the first Salt.

The second Salt is found in <term>Tartar</term>. If that is incinerated, then draw its salt forth, dissolve and coagulate it several times, and let it be sufficiently clarified.

The third Salt is this: when the wine is distilled, it leaves dregs behind, which are made into powder. Its salt can be drawn out with warm water. Each of these salts has a special property; in their center they stand in harmony because they descend from one root.

It has three sorts of Mercury and three sorts of Sulphur. The first oil is made of the stem, the second oil is made out of crude <term>Tartar</term>, and the third is the oil of Wine. There is a strange property in the spirit of Wine: for without it there cannot be extracted any true tincture of *Sol*, nor can there be made without it any true *aurum potabile*. But few men know how a true spirit of Wine is made, much less can its property be found out wholly.

<header>A short way and Repetition, &c.</header>
<meta>catchword: I</meta>

Several ways have been tried to extract and obtain the spirit of wine without adulteration, such as by various instruments and distillations with metallic serpents <note>Coiled distillation tubes.</note>, and other strange inventions involving sponges, papers, and the like. Some caused a rectified *aquavitæ* to be frozen in the deepest frost, expecting its phlegm <note>The watery, non-spirituous part of a distillate.</note> to turn to ice while the spirit remained liquid, but nothing useful was achieved.

The true way of obtaining it I told you at the end of my Manuals; for it must be subtle, penetrating, without any phlegm, pure, aerial, and volatile, so that the air may attract it with a magnetic quality; therefore, it needs to be kept tightly sealed. It has a penetrating and effective operation, and its uses are many.

There are three things which are the noblest creatures in the world; these three bear a wonderful affection for one another. Among animals, it is man, from whose *Mume* <note>Mumia, a vital substance or extract derived from the human body in alchemical medicine.</note> an animal stone is made, in which the *microcosm* is contained. Among minerals, gold is the noblest; its stability is a sufficient testimony of its noble origin and character. Among vegetables, there lies hidden a vegetable stone. Man loves gold and wine above all other creatures that can be seen with the eyes. Gold loves man and wine because it releases its noblest part if the spirit of wine is applied to it, being made potable, which gives strength to man and prolongs his life in health.

Wine also bears affection for man and for gold because it easily unites with the tincture of *Sol* <note>Alchemical gold.</note>, expels melancholy and sadness, and refreshes and rejoices the human heart. He who possesses these three stones may boldly say that he has the stones of the *Universal* <note>The Universal Medicine or Philosopher's Stone.</note>; much of it is talked and written about, but what eye has seen it? Not one among many hundreds of millions.

These stones renew men and beasts, cure leprous <note>Base or corrupted.</note> metals, and cause barrenness to become fertile through a new birth; human reason is not able to comprehend or conceive of it.

<meta>This page concludes the author's discourse on the spirit of wine and the chemical separation of its elements, transitioning from the previous page's discussion on the nobility of gold, man, and wine to the practical and philosophical summary of his experimental findings.</meta>

<page-num>25</page-num>
<header>A short way and Repetition, &c.</header>

If a rectified *Aqua vitæ* is lit, then Mercury and the Vegetable Sulphur separate; that Sulphur burns brightly, being pure fire, while the tender Mercury takes to his wings and flies to his *Chaos*.

He who can confine and catch this fiery spirit may boast that he has won a great victory in the chemical battle: for this Vegetable fiery Sulphur is the only Key to draw the Sulphur from mineral and metallic bodies.

Thus I close my book; the things contained within it are not grounded on opinions, as most physicians rely on authors who claim that such and such herbs are cold and moist, dry, and warm, in the first, second, and third degree—simply because they heard their authors affirm it, while they themselves neither saw it nor tested it. They make mere collections from other men's writings, patching together volumes. The things I wrote about, I know to be true by long experimental knowledge; I hope this experience of mine will take its place and achieve victory, just as the Amazons did through their wisdom.

> May the eternal heavenly spirit ref<unclear>resh ou</unclear>r Souls, so that we may walk in heavenly streets, forsaking all false and erroneous byways. Amen.

### PREFACE.

<image-desc>Decorative initial I in a square woodcut frame with floral and foliage scrolls.</image-desc>
*I, Basilius Valentinus, write a short concluding statement upon my former writings, and this treatise serves as a declaration of them. But, my son and disciple, you must remember to lift up your eyes to the Mountain of God and of the Philosophers, from where you expect help: namely, the Sulphur, Vitriol, and Magnet of the Philosophers must be a great help to you. For the Philosophers' Sulphur, Vitriol, and Magnet are celestial, from which comes that Universal and Philosophical <term>Lapis</term> <note>Stone</note>; vulgar and ordinary sulfur, vitriol, and magnets afford merely particular remedies and medications.*

*In the Treatise of the Philosophers' Stone, I have expressly set down in a parable the Philosophers' Sulphur in the XII Keys; but as for the Philosophers' Mercury, or the true Philosophical Magnet, I gave a hint of it in only a few words:*

*words: however, I treated the same in the* <term>XII Keys</term>, *and I spoke of the* <term>prima materia</term> <note>first matter</note> *in my Rhythms or Verses.*

*I leave a light as a farewell to seeking disciples, by which they may see the clear day in a dark night; and I describe the virtue and operation of common* <term>Sulphur</term>, <term>Vitriol</term>, *and* <term>Magnet</term>. *The rest you may find in the* Treatise *which follows the* <term>XII Keys</term>: *in which, if you find the true way of working, you may obtain sufficient health and wealth in this world.*

*Make use of it in the name of* God, *and for His glory, and do good to the poor, and be helpful to them; otherwise, your earthly* Paradise *may be turned in the end into a damnable* Hell, *from which, O* Lord, *deliver all good people. Amen.*

### The First
# TREATISE,
## Of Philosophers' Sulphur, Vitriol, and Magnet.

---

->First Section.<-

->Of Sulphur, and ferment of Philosophers.<-

Loving seeker of chemical mysteries, I have written a treatise about the Philosophers' Stone, and have set down expressly the *materia* <note>matter</note> of the Philosophers' Sulphur in the first Key, <unclear>and</unclear> taught you in the second Key how you ought to distill our water of the Eagle and cold Dragon, who had his dwelling a long time in rocky cliffs, and crept in and out in subterranean concave and hollow places: pour this spirit, or *aqua* <note>water</note> upon purified and refined gold, seal it well, and set it into a dissolution, *in fimo* <note>in a dung bed for heat</note> for 14 nights to putrefy it, then draw it over the <term>helmet</term> <note>the top part of a still or alembic</note>, pour the water upon the gold <term>calx</term> <note>the powder remains of the gold</note> whole, and make all the gold come through the <term>helmet</term>: set this again under a <term>helmet</term>, abstract the water gently, leave a third part of it in the bottom, then set it into a cellar, let it coagulate and crystallize, wash these crystals with distilled

<summary>This section begins the practical instructions for creating the Philosophers' Stone, specifically describing how to prepare the Philosophers' Sulphur using gold and symbolic substances like the Eagle and Dragon.</summary>
<keywords>Philosophers' Stone, Sulphur, Gold Calx, distillation, Eagle and Dragon, in fimo, alchemy, crystallization</keywords>

--- Page 292 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for the preparation of the Philosophers' Sulphur and transitions into the second section regarding the Philosophers' Vitriol, the necessary "female" component for the alchemical conjunction.</meta>

distilled water, precipitate them with quicksilver <note>Mercury vive</note>, evaporate the mercury gently, then you have a subtle powder. Put it in a glass, seal <note>lute</note> it, and reverberate it <note>heat in a reverberatory furnace</note> for three days and nights; do it gently. Thus is the Philosophers' Sulphur well prepared for your work, and this is the purple mantle, or Philosophic Gold; keep it safely in a glass for your conjunction.

---

## The Second Section.

### Of the Philosophers' Vitriol.

I have told you plainly how Philosophers' Sulphur is made, which *loco masculi* <note>in the place of the male</note> is to make the King, or man; now you must have the female, or wife, which is the *Mercury* of Philosophers, or the <term>materia prima lapidis</term> <note>first matter of the stone</note>, which must be made artificially. For our <term>Azoth</term> is not common vinegar, but is extracted with common vinegar, and there is a salt made of <term>materia prima</term> <note>first matter</note> (this salt is called the Philosophers' *Mercury*, which is coagulated in the belly of the earth). When this matter is brought to light, it is not expensive and is found everywhere; children play with it. It is heavy and has a scent of dead men's bones; for two guilders <note>Gilders</note> you may buy this matter for the work. Therefore, take this matter, distill, calcine, sublime, and reduce it to ashes; for if an artist lacks ashes, how can he make a salt? And he that has not a metalline salt, how can he make the Philosophers' *Mercury*?

Therefore, if you have calcined the matter, then extract its salt and rectify it well; let it crystallize <note>shoot</note> into the vitriol, which must be sweet, without any corrosiveness or sharpness of salt. Thus you get the Philosophers' Vitriol, or Philosophic Oil. Further make a *mercurial* water from it; thus you have performed an artificial work. This is called the Philosophers' <term>Azoth</term>, which purges <term>Laton</term> <note>impure metal or brass</note>, but is not yet washed.

<meta>catchword: For</meta>

<summary>The author describes the final steps to produce "Philosophic Gold" and introduces the "female" counterpart, the Philosophers' Vitriol. This substance is described as a common, heavy material found everywhere, which must be processed into a metalline salt and then into a mercurial water known as Azoth.</summary>
<keywords>Philosophers' Sulphur, Philosophic Gold, Philosophers' Vitriol, Mercury of Philosophers, Azoth, materia prima, metalline salt, Laton, calcination, distillation</keywords>

--- Page 293 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific instructions for the chemical marriage of the Philosophic Sulphur and Mercury, detailing the preparation of the "Mercurius duplicatus" and the final stages of creating the Philosophers' Stone, before transitioning into a historical and theoretical overview in the third section.</meta>

For Azoth washes Laton, as the ancient Philosophers told two or three thousand years ago. For the Philosophic Salt, or Laton, must be purged, dissolved, and distilled with its own humidity, or its own Mercurial water, attract its magnet, and stay with it. And this is the Philosophers' Mercury, or *Mercurius duplicatus*, and they are two spirits, or a spirit and water of the salt of metals. Then this water bears the name of *succus Lunariæ, aqua cælestis, acetum Philosophorum, aqua Sulphuris, aqua permanens, aqua benedicta*. Take eight or ten parts of this water and one part of your ferment, or Sulphur of *Sol*, set it into the Philosophers' Egg, seal <note>lute</note> it well, put it in the Athanor <note>Athanar</note>, into that vaporous and yet dry fire; manage <note>govern</note> it until the appearance of a black, white, and red color, then you obtain the Philosophers' Stone, and you enjoy this noble, precious, and blessed medicine and tincture, and you may work miracles with it.

---

## The third Section.

### Of the Philosophers' Magnet.

Hermes, the father of Philosophers, possessed this art and was the first who wrote of it, and prepared the stone out of the *Mercury, Sol*, and *Luna* <note>Lune</note> of the Philosophers: whom many thousand laborers have imitated; I myself also did the same: and I speak truly, that the Philosophers' Stone may be composed of two bodies; its beginning and ending must be with Philosophic Mercury.

And this is now *prima materia* <note>first matter</note>, otherwise called *præda*: *materia prima* belongs only to God, and is coagulated in the entrails of the earth; first it turns to Mercury, then to lead, then to tin and copper, then to iron, etc. Thus the coagulated Mercury must be turned by art into its *prima materia*, or water—that is, Mercurial water. This is a stone and no stone, from which is made a volatile fire, in the form of a water,

<meta>catchword: which</meta>

<summary>This page explains the final preparation of the Philosophers' Stone by combining Mercurial water with the Sulphur of Sol in an Athanor furnace. It also introduces the third section, tracing the history of the art back to Hermes and defining the "prima materia" as the source from which all metals are naturally and artificially derived.</summary>
<keywords>Azoth, Laton, Mercurius duplicatus, Philosophers' Egg, Athanor, Philosophers' Stone, Hermes, Sol, Luna, prima materia, Mercurial water</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical description of the Philosophic Magnet, completing the sentence from the previous page regarding the "first matter" which dissolves its parental elements.</meta>

# Of the Philosophers' Magnet.

...which drowns and dissolves its fixed father and its volatile mother.

Metallic Salt is an imperfect body which turns into Philosophic Mercury—that is, into a permanent or blessed water—and is the Philosophers' *Magnet*, which loves its Philosophic *Mars*, sticks to him, and stays with him. Thus our *Sol* has a *Magnet* also, which *Magnet* is the first root and matter of our stone: if you conceive of and understand my saying, then you are the richest man in the world.

Hermes says: you must have three species for the work; first, a volatile or Mercurial water, *aqua coelestis*; second, *viridis Leo* <note>green Lion</note>, which is the Philosophic *Lune* <note>silver</note>; thirdly, *aes Hermetis* <note>the copper of Hermes</note>, or *Sol* <note>gold</note>, or *ferment*.

Lastly, note that Philosophers had two ways—a wet one, which I used, and a dry one. In this, you must proceed philosophically; you must thoroughly purge the Philosophers' Mercury and make Mercury with Mercury, adding the Philosophic Salt, ferment, or Sulphur of Philosophers, and proceed as you heard before; then you have the Philosophers' *Magnet*, which is the Philosophers' Mercury. Secondly, the Metallic Salt, or Philosophic Salt. Thirdly, *aes Hermetis*, or Philosophic Sulphur.

Thus I have described the whole Art; if you do not understand it, then you will get nothing, nor are you predestined for it.

---

### *Allegorical expressions between the Holy Trinity and the Philosophers' stone.*

Dear Christian lover and well-wisher to the blessed Art: how graciously and miraculously has the Holy Trinity created the Philosophers' Stone. For God the Father is a spirit, and yet makes himself known in the form of a man, as he speaks in *Genesis*, chapter 1: let us

<header>Allegorical expression between the Holy, etc.</header>
<sig>S</sig>

...make man, an image like us. Also, this expression in his word speaks of his mouth, eyes, hands, and feet; so Philosophers' Mercury is held to be a spiritual body, as Philosophers call it. God the Father begets his only Son JESUS CHRIST, who is God and Man, and is without sin, nor did he need to die; but he laid down his life freely and rose again for his brothers' and sisters' sake, that they might live with him eternally without sin. So is *Sol*, or Gold, without defect, and is fixed, holding out gloriously through all fiery examinations; but by reason of its imperfect and sick brothers and sisters, it dies and rises gloriously, redeems and tinges them unto eternal life, making them perfect as good Gold.

The third person in the Trinity is God the Holy Ghost, a comforter sent by our Lord *Christ Jesus* to his believing Christians, who strengthens and comforts them in Faith, unto eternal life; even so is the spirit of material *Sol*, or of the body of Mercury; when they come together, then it is called the *double Mercury*. These are two spirits, God the Father and God the Holy Ghost; but God the Son, a glorified Man, is even as our glorified and fixed *Sol*, or Philosophers' stone. Since this *Lapis* <note>stone</note> is called *Trinus* <note>triple</note>, namely, out of two waters—or spirits of Mineral and of Vegetable—and of the Animal of Sulphur of *Sol*: these are the Two and Three, and yet but one. If you do not understand it, then you are not likely to succeed at all. Thus, by way of a similitude, I have sufficiently delineated the *universal* to you. Pray to God for a blessing, for without him you are not likely to prosper at all.

## The Second
# TREATISE
# On Vulgar Sulphur, Vitriol, and Magnet.

## The First Section.
## On Sulphurs.

### Chapter I.
### On the Sulphur of Saturn.

<margin>1.</margin>
<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'T' within an ornate woodcut frame featuring floral scrollwork and a small cross at the top center.</image-desc>A crystalline salt is extracted from calcined Saturn <note>lead</note> with distilled vinegar, which is distilled with spirit of wine into a red oil. This oil coagulates and fixes mercury, after it has first been precipitated with oil of vitriol; for all powders and medicaments intended to create *Sol* <note>gold</note> and *Lune* <note>silver</note> must be made fixed, holding firm through all trials by fire.

<margin>3.</margin>
A glorious tincture is made from this oil if you take three parts of the Mercury of Mars <note>iron</note> and one part of this red oil of Saturn, joining, coagulating, and fixing them: this work may be accomplished in a month's time, or somewhat longer. This tincture may be augmented with the Mercury of Mars *infinitely*; projecting one part of it upon

three parts of *Sol* <note>gold</note>, to make thereby an ingress <note>penetration</note> for the tincture: one part of this tincture transmutes thirty parts (if it be well prepared) of *Mercury* and of *Luna* <note>silver</note> into good *Sol*. Remember your Creator, and be mindful of the poor; then the Lord will be mindful of you also.

---

## Chap. II.

## Of Sulphur of Jupiter.

1. There is made from *Jupiter* <note>tin</note> a *minium* <note>red lead or pigment</note>, just as the like is made of *Saturn* <note>lead</note>; from there is extracted and distilled a red sweet oil. This oil tinges *Saturn*, after it is first calcined with *sal ammoniac*, into *Sol*.
2. The filings of *Jupiter* being calcined with quicklime for a day, and the *calx* <note>powdered oxide</note> being washed from it, then you have a fixed powder. If you can reduce it again into a fluid state and drive it <note>process it by heat</note> with *Saturn*, then you may get a reasonable part of good *Luna* and *Sol*, by which a needy laboratory worker may get a sufficient livelihood.
3. There is a calcination made of tin and lead with common salt, but it is better if made with the salt of the *caput mortuum* <note>the metallic residue after distillation</note> of vitriol and saltpeter. If the oil of vitriol is added to the *calx* of *Jupiter* and *Saturn* and made into one mass, seal it well with luting, let it stand for eight days and nights in warm sand, and then drive it: one hundredweight of lead affords in this manner six and a half marks of fine *Luna*; one mark of such *Luna* yields one ounce of *Sol*.

This has been my first work to make *Luna* and *Sol* with. Note, these six and a half marks of *Luna* afford six and a half ounces of *Sol*; this *Sol* and *Luna* amount to 208 Guilders, or 20 pounds and 16 shillings.

Of Sulphurs of Mars, and Venus.

## Chap. III.

### Of Sulphurs of Mars and Venus.

1. Take some pounds of Verdigris <note>copper acetate</note>, extract its tincture with distilled vinegar, and let it crystallize; then you have a glorious vitriol, out of which you may distill a red oil by retort. This oil dissolves Mars <note>iron</note>, turning it into a vitriol, which is reduced over a long time in a great fire into a red oil; then you have the Sulphur of Mars and Venus together. Add somewhat of the Sulphur of Sol <note>gold</note>, coagulate and fix it; then you have a medicine which improves men and metals.
2. Luna <note>silver</note> is graduated with the oil, and a good part of the King’s crown <note>gold</note> is obtained.
3. Two equal parts of laminated Sol and Luna melted together, putrefied in this oil for a day and a night, turns them into good gold. In this oil you will find many strange effects and virtues. *Laus Deo* <note>Praise be to God</note>.

---

2. This Sulphur of Sol tinges prepared calx of Luna <note>powdered silver oxide</note> into good gold, but you heard in my former expressions that the King has only an honorable garment, and must

# Chap. VI.
## Of Sulphur of Lune.

1. **T**his *Lune* <note>silver</note> is made spiritual by means of a water, expressed in our second Key, and may easily be made into potable silver, by which many diseases are cured.
2. If you take one part of this spiritual *Lune*, and you feed it with three parts of *Virgin's Milk*, and bring it to fixation, then you have an increase of *Lune*, which breeds monthly young ones; these are taken forth, and their places are supplied with *Mercury vive* <note>quicksilver</note>, etc. This powder is reduced with *Borax*, then you have a perpetual increase (*augmentum perpetuum*).

---

# Chap. VII.
## Of Antimonial Vitriol.

1. **T**here is made out of Vitriol of Antimony with distilled vinegar a sweet extraction; its *acetum* <note>vinegar</note> is separated from it. On the remainder is poured spirit of wine; it must be extracted, and the pure separated from the impure. This sweet extraction is distilled; the spirit of wine is often drawn from it by cohobation <note>repeated distillation</note>, and that powder is reduced to a glorious Oil of Antimony.
This oil cures all manner of diseases, being administered in a convenient vehicle; this medicinal oil is a great *arcanum* <note>secret remedy</note>.
2. Further, take one part of this oil, and two parts of the Mercurial water, in which is dissolved a fourth part of *Sol* <note>gold</note> (purple mantle); then join them, seal them hermetically, coagulate, and fix. This Tincture tinges *Lune* and *Mercury* into *Sol*. This is that pure Sulphur of Antimony:

## Chap. VIII.

### Of Sulphur of Vitriol.

1. From Vitriol is made a *lixivium* <note>alkaline solution or lye</note> with ashes of beechwood, and a sulphur is drawn from this vitriol and is precipitated with *salt of tartar*. Furthermore, the oil of sulphur is extracted with *juniper oil*; thus you have a red oil. Putrefy this with *spirit of wine*, then draw off the *spirit of wine* from it. This glorious oil of sulphur is good against many diseases; it is to be used against consumption, dropsy, plague, gravel <note>kidney stones</note>, and scabbiness.

2. *Vitriol* is also sublimed with *sal ammoniac*; but it is better if done with a *lixivium*, by which the body of vitriol is better opened and dissolved. This sublimate is dissolved into an oil, by which crude mercury can be coagulated and fixed, of which I shall write more soon when I treat of *vitriol*.

---

### Of common Sulphur.

Pour this water on tiles that have newly come out of the oven, soak them with it, and distill it *per retortam* <note>by retort</note>; you will have a yellow water from it like an *aqua fortis* <note>nitric acid</note>, which tinges <unclear>Lu e.</unclear> Take one part of this water and one part of *lunar calx* <note>calcined silver</note>; let it stand for three days and nights in warm sand; the fourth part of it turns into

# The second Section.
->of Vitriols.<-

## Chap. I.
### Of *Vitriol* of *Sol*, and of *Lune*.

In the first place, you must have our water of the cold earth salt and of the *Eagle* <note>often sal ammoniac or a specific mercurial sublimate</note>, whereby gold and silver are made spiritual. Let it shoot into crystals; this is that metallic vitriol out of which is distilled, together with spirit of wine and oil of sulphur, [a substance] to be used after the manner of metals.

<header>Of Vitriol of Saturn, and of Jupiter.</header>

## Chap. II.
### Of Vitriol of Saturn, and of Jupiter.

Calcine *Saturn* <note>lead</note> or *Jupiter* <note>tin</note>, and extract its *anima* <note>soul or essence</note> with *distilled vinegar*. Let it purify for 14 days and nights, and let the *vitriol* crystallize. This must be driven over with *spirit of wine*; it yields a sweet *oil*, and it is the *sulphur of Saturn and Jupiter*. This *oil* coagulates *mercury*, and having been first precipitated with *oil of vitriol*, it fixes it.

---

## Chap. III.
### Of Vitriol of Mars.

Take equal parts of the filings of *Mars* <note>iron</note> and of *sulphur*; calcine them in a *brick-kiln* until they reach a purple color. Pour *distilled water* or *vinegar* onto it; it extracts a green color. Evaporate a third part of that water and let it crystallize: in this way, you have an artificial *vitriol*. Distill from it a red spirit or *oil*. Take half an ounce of it and add it to *mercurial water* in which *Sol* <note>gold</note> is dissolved (take only a fourth part of an ounce of this *oil*). Fix this *tincture*; then you have an excellent *medicine* to project upon *lead*, *silver*, and *tin*, which are transmuted by it into pure *gold*.

> O! you Christian heart, return thanks to the Creator of minerals, metals, and other creatures!

---

### Chap. VI.
### Of common <term>Vitriol</term>.

1. Take good Hungarian <term>Vitriol</term>, dissolve it in distilled water, coagulate it again, and let it shoot into crystals: repeat this five times, then it is well purified, and the salts, <term>alums</term>, and <term>nitre</term> are separated from it. Distill this purified <term>Vitriol</term> with spirit of wine into a red oil, ferment it with spiritual <term>*Sol*</term> <note>gold</note>, add to it a proper dose of quick <term>Mercury of Antimony</term>, coagulate, and fix it; then you have a <term>tincture</term> for men, and it also tinges <term>*Luna*</term> <note>silver</note> into <term>*Sol*</term> <note>gold</note>.

### A Process for the Philosophic work of Vitriol.

Having obtained this process in the aforementioned year, and afterward—as you shall hear—having elaborated and wrought the same with my own hands, with no man overseeing me, I was heartily rejoiced, even as if I had been newborn, and returned hearty thanks to God. Its practice at first I have not plainly described because I had erred in its composition and was forced to begin the work anew. Having failed in my work, which I began in the year 1605 because the matter of the Earth and the spirit of <term>Mercury</term> were not sufficiently purged—and therefore the earth could not be perfectly united with the water during composition—I left that alone and began a new process at the end of the year 1605 in the City of *Strasburg*. I used more diligence and exactness; then my work (God be praised) prospered better, for which I am still thankful to God.

<summary>The author concludes his writings on the Philosophers' stone and the Twelve Keys, then begins a personal account of his laboratory work with vitriol in Strasburg in 1605. He notes that his initial attempts failed due to insufficiently purified materials but eventually succeeded through greater diligence.</summary>
<keywords>Philosophers' stone, Twelve Keys, prima materia, Philosophic Salt, Philosophic Sulphur, Vitriol, Strasburg, 1605, alchemy experiments, Mercury</keywords>

--- Page 308 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific laboratory record of the author's experiments with vitriol, following his relocation to Strasburg in late 1605 after previous failures.</meta>

# A Process upon the Philosophic

...God for it. In the name of the Holy Trinity, October 19, 1605. I took ten pounds of vitriol and dissolved it in warmed distilled rain-water. I let it stand for a day and a night; at that time, many impurities had settled. I filtered the matter and evaporated it gently, *ad cuticulam usque* <note>until a film forms on the surface</note>. I set it in a cool place to crystallize. This hot, dried vitriol I dissolved again in distilled rain-water and let it crystallize again. I repeated this work until the vitriol acquired a celestial green color, having no more impurities in it; it lost all its corrosiveness and had a very pleasant taste.

This highly purified vitriol—thus raw and not calcined—I put into a coated retort and distilled it over an open fire. I drove it over in the space of twelve hours by precise management of the fire in a white vapor. When no more of these vapors came and the red corrosive oil began to appear, I let the fire go out. The next morning, when all was cold, I took off the receiver and poured the distillate in the receiver into a vessel. Since some of the sealing clay had fallen in, I filtered it and obtained a clear solvent, which contained some watery residue because I used uncalcined vitriol. This I drew off in a <term>Balmy</term> <note>a vapor or water bath</note>, not leaving one drop of water in it.

I found my chaos at the bottom: a dark red, very heavy substance, which I poured into a vial and sealed it <term>Hermetice</term> <note>hermetically</note>. I placed it on a tripod inside a wooden globe in a vaporous water bath, where I left it until everything was dissolved. After some weeks, it separated into two parts: a bright transparent water and an earth, which settled to the bottom of the glass in the form of a thick, black, corrosive substance like pitch. I separated the white spirit from it, and I put the fluid black matter back in to be dissolved. The white spirit that was dissolved from it, I separated again. I repeated this work, leaving nothing in the bottom except a dry red earth. After that, I purified my white spirit *per distillationem* <note>by distillation</note> very precisely; it was as pure as a tear falling from the eye. The remaining earth I dried under a <term>Muffle</term> <note>a type of furnace</note>; it was as porous and as dry as dust. On this, I poured again

<summary>The author describes a detailed alchemical procedure from 1605 involving the purification of vitriol through repeated crystallization and distillation. The process results in a "white spirit" as clear as a tear and a dry, red, porous earth.</summary>

# Work of Vitriol.

Again, I took my white spirit and set it in digestion. This spirit extracted the Sulphur, or Philosophic Gold, and was tinged a reddish-yellow. I decanted it from the matter, and in a vessel, I distilled the spirit from the Sulphur. That Sulphur remained behind in the form of an oil, very fiery—nothing compares to its heat—and as red as a ruby. I poured this distilled white spirit onto the earth again, extracted more of its Sulphur, and added it to the previous amount. After this, that *Corpus terræ* <note>body of the earth</note> appeared paler in color; I calcined it for several hours in a muffle furnace, put it into a vessel, and poured my white spirit over it to extract its pure, white fixed Salt. The remaining earth was very porous and good for nothing, so I threw it away. Thus, these three principles were fully and perfectly separated.

After all this, I took my astral clarified Salt, which weighed half an ounce according to the weight at *Strasburg*, and of the white spirit, which weighed four ounces, and of Mercury, one ounce and a quarter of an ounce. I divided these into two parts, each weighing half an ounce and one dram. I added this Salt to one part of the water in a vial and sealed it, then set it in digestion. There I saw clearly how the Salt dissolved itself again into this spirit; therefore, I added the other part to it, which was half an ounce and one dram. No sooner was this added than the body, together with the spirit, immediately turned as black as coal. It ascended to the top of the glass, and having no room to go any further, it moved back and forth. Sometimes it <unclear>sailed</unclear> to the bottom, and soon after it rose to the middle, then it rose higher. Thus it moved from the fourth of *July* to the seventh of *August*, namely for 34 days, a wonderful work that I beheld with admiration.

At last, these were united and turned into a black powder, which remained dry at the bottom. Seeing that it was so, I increased my fire by one degree, took it out of the water bath, and set it in the ash bath. After ten days, the matter on the bottom began to look somewhat white, which made me rejoice heartily. I continued this degree of fire until the matter, both above and below, became as white as glittering snow. But it was

# A Process upon the Philosophic

...not yet fixed; making a trial of it, I set it in aga<unclear>in</unclear> and increased my fire one degree higher. Then the matter began to ascend and descend; it moved up high and stayed in the middle of the glass, not touching the bottom of it. This lasted 38 days and nights. At that time, as well as previously at 30 days, I saw a variety of colors which I am unable to describe. At last, this powder fell to the bottom and became fixed. I made a projection with it, adding one grain of it to one and a quarter ounces of Mercury, transmuting it into very good *Lune* <note>silver</note>. Now it was time to restore to this white tincture its true *anima* <note>soul/life-force</note> and imbibe it, to bring it from its whiteness to redness and to its perfect virtue.

Thereupon, I took the third principle, namely the *anima*, which I had reserved until now. In quantity, it was one ounce, a quarter of an ounce, and one dram. I poured my reserved spirit of Mercury into it (the quantity was one and a quarter ounces) and drew it over several times *per alembicum* <note>by means of an alembic or still</note> so that they finally united together. I divided these into seven equal parts. I poured one part onto my clarified earth, or tincture, which greedily embraced its *anima* along with its spirit and turned to a redness in twelve days and nights. However, it had no tinging quality as yet, except for Mercury vive <note>quicksilver/liquid mercury</note> and Saturn <note>lead</note>, which it transmuted into *Lune* <note>silver</note>; when separated, this *Lune* yielded three grains of gold. I proceeded further with my imbibition <note>the process of a solid absorbing a liquid</note> and added all seven parts of the *anima* to it. At the fourth imbibition, one part of my work tinged ten parts of copper into gold; at the fifth imbibition, one part tinged a hundred parts; at the sixth, it tinged a thousand parts; and at the seventh, it tinged ten thousand parts. Thus, God be praised, my work ended successfully, to the great joy of my heart. At this time, I obtained four ounces, a half ounce, and one dram of the true *Medicine*. The last two in weight were almost equal to the first. From the proceeds of this work, I paid 48,000 *Gilders* <note>currency</note> for land and property to that noble **gentleman**, *O. V. D.* *Actum* <note>Done/Enacted</note> 1607. I recorded these things as a memorandum, so that I should not forget any of the procedures or other matters.

### An exact work, how *Mercury vive* is coagulated and brought unto a *Lunar* fixation, which *Lune* holds *Sol* also in the trial.

Take of *Mercury vive* <note>quicksilver</note> two ounces, and of pulverized common *Sulphur* six ounces; grind these in a wooden dish with a wooden *Pestle*. Set it on a coal fire in a melting pot, stirring it about continually; let all the *Sulphur* evaporate. Then take out the *Mercury*, grind an equal quantity of *Sulphur* with it, and proceed with its heating as before; repeat this work five times. Then sublime this *Mercury* *per gradus ignis* <note>by degrees of fire</note>. Take out this sublimate, break it into pieces the size of a small *Nut* or bean, and soak them in the white of eggs. Then take a cementing pot and put ashes into it; in the middle of it, place an *Iron box*. Layer into it this sublimate with refined silver until the box is full. Then seal an *Iron lid* to it with lute, put ashes on the lid, and seal an earthen lid upon that. Set this pot into a sand capel <note>cupel or sand bath</note>. Let your first fire be gentle for twelve hours, then increase your fire for twelve hours more; at last, make a strong fire for twenty-four hours. Then break open the *Pot*; you will find a black-gray matter. Process it with *Lead*; from four ounces, you will get three ounces of *fixed silver*. Separate this fine silver in *aqua fortis* <note>nitric acid</note>; you will find a good deal of black *Gold Calx* <note>powder</note>. Reserve the *silver Calx* separately; you may layer with it another time. This is as far as I went in my experience.

Before I begin to speak of the Salts of Metals and Minerals, and declare their volumes and other precious and noble growth underground, in the first place I will provide the preparation of *aurum potabile* <note>potable or drinkable gold</note>, because therein lies the *Corona* <note>Crown</note> of *Medicinals* <note>medicines</note>, *universaliter* <note>universally</note>, and it merits the first place; for the Salts of other Metals and Minerals in their innate virtues are for *particulars* <note>specific ailments</note> only, and are ordained to preserve man in health. There is just cause to begin with the making of *aurum potabile* without sophistication <note>adulteration or deception</note>, and I will speak of the distinction of it, so that it may be judged infallibly to be the true one.

Since this is my last part, my intention is to make a perfect account of *aurum potabile* for the benefit of good and understanding men, whom God after my death will rejoice with this book of mine, which I wrote based upon tedious and laborious experience; herein I speak not by hearsay, but the things I do write of, I know experimentally to be true.

<meta>catchword: There-</meta>

Therefore, if God blesses you with a true knowledge of this, I ask that you keep this *secretum* <note>secret</note> in silence and privacy, lest you turn God’s blessing into a curse. This is because the preparation of this and of the stone is the same; both have their origin, first generation, and birth from the true seed and astrological *primum mobile* <note>first mover</note>, called the spirit of Mercury, about which I have previously written more extensively. For I speak the highest truth to you: that neither the *universal* nor *Particular Tincture*, nor *aurum potabile*, nor any other *universal Medicine*, can be obtained or prepared without this heavenly and spiritual essence, which has its origin in the starry heaven and takes and receives its nature from there. Therefore, be silent until death; at your departure, lay down your talent again as I have done. For if I had not informed you faithfully, you would know very little of that mystery and would still continue with the common people in folly, blindness, and madness; you would have sent a recipe into the greasy and ointment-filled shops of apothecaries. But where would your soul have gone after your departure? Into Galen's <note>Referring to traditional Galenic medicine, which the author opposes</note> lap, to the utmost depth of darkness where the devils have their dwelling places; even there, both your soul and body would have been sent, had you divulged any of these secrets.

To return to my intended business, I will first inform you what that true and highest *aurum potabile* and *universal Medicine* is. After this, there follows in order another *aurum potabile* made of the *fixed red Sulphur*, or soul of the corporeal gold, most highly purified, which is prepared in conjunction with the *Universal Spirit of Mercurie*. After this, there follows another *Particular Medicine*, which is half an *aurum potabile*, showing its efficacy and power in many trials. Then I will add a description of *aurum potabile* to it, because it follows the path of gold and wonderfully demonstrates its great energy and virtues.

The highest and chiefest *aurum potabile*, which the Lord God has placed in nature, is the boiled, prepared, and fixed

# Of the supernatural

At first, on the side around the glass where the matter lies, a green circle is seen, then a blue one, then a yellow one, and afterward all the colors of a rainbow join and appear, though they last only a short time. When the *anima* of *Sol* <note>the soul of gold</note> is wholly dissolved into the *Mercurial spirit*, and nothing is seen at the bottom, then pour into it twice as much of the best rectified spirit of wine, brought to its highest degree. The glass must be sealed <note>luted</note> exactly; digest and purify it gently for twelve or fifteen days together. Then abstract it *per alembicum* <note>by a distillation vessel</note>; that matter comes over in a blood-red transparent color. This extraction must be repeated, and nothing must be left in the bottom which is physical; then you have the true *aurum potabile* <note>drinkable gold</note>, which can never be reduced back into a solid body.

But note, the gold must be purified to the highest degree before its destruction and the extraction of its soul. There is another *aurum potabile* prepared artificially which, though it cannot be called or recorded in writing as the full and true potable gold, is yet considered more than half an *aurum potabile* because it is transcendently effective in many diseases where nature might have been in great doubt. This half *aurum potabile* is made in two ways, of which the latter is better and more effective than the former, and requires more labor and time than the former.

Take this extracted soul of gold, drawn out with the sweet spirit of common salt, purify <note>edulcorate</note> it most purely and exactly, and finally dry <note>exiccate</note> it. Put it in a spacious vial or glass vessel and pour red Oil of Vitriol on it, which has been concentrated <note>dephlegmed</note> and rectified *per retortam* <note>by a retort</note> so that it is transparent, clear, and white; you will see that it acts upon the gold and dissolves it, and is tinged a deep red.

Add enough of this oil so that the sulfur, or the soul of gold, may be dissolved in it. Let it putrefy in *Balneo Mariæ* <note>a water bath</note>; apply a reasonable fire to it so that you can see the soul of gold is quite dissolved in the Oil of Vitriol. The sediment <note>feces</note> which it has settled must be separated from it; then add twice as much of the best rectified spirit of wine to it, the rectification of which

Let it stay there for a month; then the sharpness of Vitriol is mitigated by the spirit of wine and loses its acidity and sharpness. Both together make an excellent medicine. Drive both over <note>distill</note>, and let nothing stay behind in the bottom; then you get more than half an *aurum potabile*, in the form and color of a deep yellow liquor. Note that some metals may be processed in this manner: first, a Vitriol may be obtained from the metal, then a spirit is further driven from it and joined in this manner with the Soul, dissolved, and further digested with spirit of wine. All must enter together into a medicine, as I mentioned formerly, which has its own special efficacy.

The second way to prepare this half *aurum potabile*—which, though it is but half an *aurum potabile*, is yet far preferred in virtue and efficacy over the other just spoken of—is done as follows:

Take the extracted *Solar Soul* spoken of above, put it into a vial, and pour onto it the extracted *Philosophical Sulphur*. This is the second principle, which is drawn with the spirit of Mercury from the philosophical earth and Mercury (or the spirit of Mercury) into an oiliness <note>oleity</note>, which now is Sulphur again and must be drawn off gently *per modum distillationis* <note>by way of distillation</note>.

Pour as much of this Philosophical Sulphur onto it so that the *Solar Soul* may be dissolved; let it stand in a gentle bath and let the dissolution be completed. Then pour more of the best spirit of wine into it, digest gently, and draw these over; let nothing stay behind in the bottom. Then you have a medicine which does not lack more than two grains of being the right and true *aurum potabile*.

These are the chief ways to make the corporeal *aurum potabile*. With this I conclude and proceed further with a short but true process for how silver, which is next to gold concerning perfection, is also made potable; this process must be done in the following manner.

<header>Of potable <term>Lune</term> <note>Silver</note>.</header>

Take the sky-colored sulfur, or spirit of <term>Lune</term>, which was extracted with distilled vinegar, as I informed you in the specific section on <term>Lune</term>. Wash it, rectify it with spirit of wine, and dry it. Put it in a vial and pour onto it three times as much spirit of mercury, which is prepared from the white spirit of vitriol, as I faithfully taught you in that place. Seal the glass firmly with lute, set it to putrefy in the vapor bath, and let it all be dissolved so that nothing more is seen at the bottom. Then add to it an equal quantity of the best spirit of wine, set it to digest for half a month, and distill everything over. Let nothing stay behind; then you have the true potable <term>Lune</term>, which is admirable in its efficacy and performs wonders when it is used.

## A description of the fiery Tartar.

Distill a spirit of wine from good wine and rectify it with white calcined tartar. Let all of it come over, and put that which is distilled into a vial. Add four ounces of well-sublimed sal ammoniac to one quart of spirit of wine. Place a helmet <note>an alembic head</note> upon it, set a large receiver into cold water, and drive the volatile spirits into it gently in a water bath <note>Balneo Mariae</note>, leaving only a small quantity behind. Note: the alembic must always be cooled with wet cloths; then the spirits will be dissolved and turn into a liquid. Thus is prepared this hot spirit of wine.

---

## Of the Salt of Tartar.

First you must note that the Philosophers' Tartar is not the common tartar with which the lock is opened, but it is a salt which comes from the root and is the only mystical key for all metals. It is prepared thus: make a strong lye <note>lixivium</note> of the ashes of <term>sarments</term> <note>vine-shoots</note>, or twigs of the vine, and boil...

# Of Calx vive <note>Quicklime</note>.

...boil away all its moisture; a reddish matter remains, which must be reverberated for three hours in a flaming fire. Stirring it constantly, let it come to a whiteness. This white matter must be dissolved in distilled rain water; let its sediment settle, filter it, and evaporate it in a glass vessel until the matter is dry. This dry matter is the Salt of Tartar, from which the true spirit is extracted.

Note: just as I spoke earlier of the virtue and qualities of precious stones, so too there are many common and lowly stones that possess great virtues. Through experience, they are known to have rare qualities, which ignorant and inexperienced men will hardly believe, nor can they comprehend them with their dull reasoning and understanding. I will demonstrate this using the example of *Calx vive*, which in human judgment is held in no great value and lies neglected in obscurity. However, it possesses a mighty virtue and efficacy, which becomes apparent when it is applied to the most severe diseases. Since its triumphant and transcendent power is almost entirely unknown to the general public, for the benefit of those who investigate natural and supernatural mysteries—and to whom I disclose these secrets in this my book—I will also reveal this mystery concerning *Calx vive* as a conclusion. I will show first how its spirit is extracted from it, a work which indeed requires an expert artist who is well-informed beforehand of its preparation.

Take as much unslaked lime as you wish; beat and grind it on a well-dried stone into an extremely fine powder. Pour onto it as much spirit of wine as the pulverized *Calx* is able to absorb; no liquid should sit on top of it. Attach a helmet <note>an alembic head</note> to it, seal it well with lute, and place a receiver before it. Distill the spirit gently from it in a water bath <note>Balneo</note>; this distillation must be repeated eight or ten times. This spirit of wine strengthens the spirit of *Calx* immensely and makes it more fiery hot. Take the remaining *Calx* out of the vessel, grind it very small, and add to it a tenth part of pure Salt of Tartar that contains no sediment.

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the chemical preparation of Calx vive, specifically detailing the distillation process when mixed with the residue of Salt of Tartar and the method for capturing its spirit.</meta>

# Of Calx vive.
<meta>catchword: iterated</meta>

As much as this matter weighs in total, add an equal amount of the addition of Salt of Tartar to it—namely, the stony matter remaining from which the Salt of Tartar was extracted. It must be well dried. All of this must be mixed together and put into a well-coated retort; three parts of the retort must remain empty. Attach a large receiver, or body, to it very securely. Note: the body into which the retort's nose is placed must have a pipe the width of a finger, to which another body may be attached containing a quantity of spirit of wine. Then, apply a gentle fire to it. At first, some of the phlegm <note>the watery, non-medicinal part of a distillate</note> comes over, which falls into the first attached body. When the phlegm has completely come over, increase the fire. A white spirit will come to the upper part of the body, similar to the white spirit of Vitriol. This does not fall into the phlegm but slides through the pipe into the other body, drawing itself into the spirit of wine and embracing it, just as one fire joins with another.

Note: if the spirit of *Calx* <note>quicklime</note> is not first prepared by the spirit of wine and drawn off and on as I described, then it does not behave this way; instead, it falls into the phlegm, where it is quenched and loses all its efficacy. It is such a difficult matter to search nature thoroughly, as she reserves many things for herself. Once this spirit has fully entered the spirit of wine, take off the body. Discard the phlegm, but carefully keep the spirit of wine and the spirit of *Calx*. Note that both these spirits are difficult to separate because they embrace each other closely; when distilled, they come over together.

Therefore, take these mixed and united spirits and put them into a glass jar. Light it; the spirit of wine burns away, while the spirit of *Calx* remains in the glass. Keep it carefully. This is a great *arcanum* <note>a deep secret or powerful remedy</note>; few other spirits exceed its efficacy if you know how to use it well. Its qualities can hardly be set down in any brief summary.

This spirit dissolves *Oculi Cancrorum* <note>Crab’s eyes; calcium carbonate stones found in the stomachs of crayfish, used in historical medicine</note> and the hardest crystals. When these three are driven over together and often iterated—

<summary>The author describes a complex distillation process to extract the "spirit of Calx" (quicklime) by mixing it with Salt of Tartar residue and capturing the resulting white spirit in spirit of wine. The final product is a highly potent chemical solvent and medicinal arcanum capable of dissolving hard minerals.</summary>

<keywords>Calx vive, quicklime, Salt of Tartar, distillation, spirit of wine, spirit of Vitriol, arcanum, Oculi Cancrorum, alchemy, solvent</keywords>

--- Page 323 ---
<meta>This page concludes the instructions for the distillation of the spirit of Calx, describing its medicinal uses for stones and gout, its alchemical properties in dissolving jewels and fixing volatile spirits, and ending with a prayer of thanksgiving.</meta>

# Of Calx vive.

Iterated in that distilling, three drops of that liquor being administered in warm wine, break and dissolve any gravel and stone in a man's body, expelling their very roots, without putting the patients to any pain.

This spirit of *Calx* <note>quicklime</note> at the beginning looks bluish; being gently rectified <note>purified through redistillation</note>, it looks white, transparent, and clear, leaving few feces <note>residue or dregs</note> behind. This spirit dissolves the most fixed jewels and precious stones. On the other hand, it fixes all volatile spirits with its transcendent heat.

This spirit conquers all manner of podagrical <note>relating to gout</note> symptoms, be they ever so nodose <note>knotty or swollen</note> and tartarous <note>characterized by hard deposits</note>, dissolving and expelling them radically.

To the omnipotent trine God <note>the Trinity</note>, Father, Son, and Holy Ghost, be returned hearty thanks for all his benefits, which he has bestowed on man, and for revealing those secrets I have worked on in his name. To him be eternal praises. Amen.

# BASILIUS VALENTINUS
### HIS
# TREATISE
### CONCERNING
# MICROCOSM.

Those who seek Art and have a desire to attain wisdom should note that the Highest, upon my continued prayers, has granted to me, a clergyman, the ability to make known the many and great mysteries of nature: among which man's body is one to be considered, and how it is governed in imitation of the *Microcosm* <note>The author here identifies the "Microcosm" as the "great world," which is an inversion of the standard terminology where the Macrocosm is the great world and the Microcosm is the "little world" of man.</note>. For it is proper that the lesser should imitate the greater, and the smallest and meanest ought to be governed by the greatest and most potent.

*Microcosm*, or the great world, contains three things as the most principal; the rest, which come from these, are merely accidentals. In the first place, one must consider the matter and form of this world; this matter is made formal out of a non-shape, or a nothingness, and the great Creator immediately prescribed an order for this matter—regarding what government it should keep—as soon as it came to life or motion. This matter and form are water and earth. For at the Creation, by a separation of the water from the earth, the matter and form were finished as two things belonging to one another. From these, all animals and vegetables have their beginning; and two other things, air and fire, which belong to one another, have worked life into them. The matter and

<summary>Basilius Valentinus introduces his treatise by explaining that man's body is governed in imitation of the "Microcosm" (the great world). He describes the creation of the world from water and earth, with life provided by air and fire.</summary>

The matter was not perfect without the form; these joining into one, by God’s ordinance, the form having become alive, then reached a perfection: the matter and form gained life by motion, air was the first cause of that motion, and perfect maturity was caused by a suitable heat, movably enclosed in the air; thus the earth was brought to fertility by the air, it was opened, and became porous by motion for generation. The earth being impregnated, made her seed apparent by her wateriness, then air and heat in the lower and upper regions of the Astrals caused a birth to be brought forth, the blossoms were produced, and the appointed fruit was ripened by the concoction of heat.

*Calidity* <note>Heat</note> is a sulfurous hot spirit, which like a medicine dries up the superfluous gross wateriness and phlegmatic matter, which in the generation at the beginning abounds too much in the earth, before the air could have an equal dominion when joining with it, carrying the same along in the superfluity of her birth.

The second principal part of the *Microcosm* is *mobility*, for the matter in itself was without life, which was stirred up by heat, then the vital spirit became perceptible; this is in man a sulfurous spirit, kindling the body by a heat, drying up the superfluity of the earth by the subtlety of its substance, and governing the body in a constant motion. For after the heat is gone, then coldness gains the dominion, the spirit of life being gone, no sensation is felt in the pulse and arteries, and a dead body is found instead of life; at the departure of the warm spirit of sulfur, rational men ought to take this mystery into consideration.

The first two Elements, the matter and form, being apparent, and having gained a mobility from the last two Elements by light, the *Microcosm* was not yet perfect; the Creator further allotted an increase to the seed of the earth, just as he did to vegetables and animals. God allowed to the earth an imagination for all sorts of seeds, and to bring

Matter and form of the *Microcosm* being extant, consisting of earth and water, the Creator then breathed life into them through a warm air, heating the cold earthly substance and providing a heat for life and motion. This was the Soul, which is the true Sulphur of Man—spiritual, incomprehensible, and sensibly felt by its own operation. All this being finished, God then allowed an imagination toward the good within the perfect understanding of Man, so that by his imagination he could judge all the beasts and impose a proper name on each; and by that imagination, he learned to know his wife also, recognizing that she had flesh and bones from his body. Then men appeared perfect, and that matter was made into the shape of a sensible body. This form, having been made alive by the Soul, was further granted a subtle spirit for imagination and knowledge. This is an invisible and incomprehensible form, like a master craftsman who frames all things in the mind; it has its habitation in the upper region of the *Microcosm*, according to its volatility, and deserves the name of *Mercury*, or the invisible spirit of man's body. Form and matter are earthly; life resides in motion, and the knowledge of every understanding of good and evil stands in the sharp speculation of the *Microcosm*. Any surplus found besides these three, nature casts off as a corpse <note>Cadaver</note>, and it is like a monster; through these three, it is found to be a separation and a *caput mortuum* <note>The useless remains or "dead head" left after an alchemical process</note>.

If glorified *Elias* were present, and the *Astrals* could speak, and if silent nature had a tongue to express this, then I would not need to bring in any further evidence to persuade the incredulous, who have not judiciously considered his saying. For a man possessed with blindness cannot pass judgment upon my writings; but understanding judges impatience, and wisdom separates herself from folly through her own experience.

<meta>This page continues the alchemical explanation of the Microcosm, specifically describing how the three primary principles—Sulphur, Mercury, and Salt—function together to sustain the human body and how this same system operates throughout the natural world.</meta>

This Vital spirit nourishes, feeds, and preserves itself by the oiliness <note>Oleity</note> of man’s Sulphur, which is predominant in the blood, and with or through it works in the whole body so that the substance may be perfect. This Vital spirit is Mercury, which is found in man and is preserved by an oiliness of its likeness; besides these two, Mercury and Sulphur, there is a third thing in man, namely Salt, which lies in the flesh, body, and bones.

The Salt administers its noblest spirit for nourishment to the blood—which saltiness is found there by the taste—and disperses itself throughout the body, preserves man’s body like a Balsam from putrefaction, and acts as the bond and union <note>copulation</note> whereby Mercury, or the Vital spirit, continues longer with the Balsam in the flesh and dwells together as one. For in the Salt there lies a spirit which must protect all other Balsams in their worth and dignity. The remainder found in the flesh, if these three are taken from it, is a dead thing, as I mentioned formerly, and is good for nothing, nor can it be used for anything.

As this Union, Dominion, and Government are in Man, the same are in Metals, Minerals, and Vegetables, which make up their perfect body, live, continue, and are preserved in the same manner as man is. As one follows the other in Man, according to order, other animals are in a similar condition according to their kind and property. As a cow is an animal, its food, such as grass, is vegetable; this vegetable matter is putrefied by the heat of the cow's body. In that putrefaction, a separation is made, which is the Key of all dissolutions and separations. Once separation is made, then the subtle spirit, the subtle Sulphur, and the subtle Salt of the vegetable substance of the grass go into all members of the whole body of the cow: the spirit rules the beast, the Sulphur nourishes it, and the Salt preserves it.

This being done, nature then distributes her gifts further, making a new separation—such as from the excess which the cow does not assume by way of assimilation and must part with—and distributes that same further, and that

For the subtlest spirit of the milk, together with the Sulphur, sublimes, is taken off, and turned into a coagulated fatness, which is butter. The rest is separated by other means and precipitated, and thereby another separation is made; this is a second coagulation, out of which men make their food. From the surplus, another separation is made by fire, though not as far as the former two. At last, there remains a wateriness <note>aquosity</note>, and it is of no great usefulness because the spirit and its nutriment are taken from it by the several separations.

After this, nature makes through further putrefaction another and gross excretion of a sulphurous and salt substance, which generates afresh a living spirit, which is the excrement. This serves for the earth to be manured with, making the earth fertile by its Sulphur and Salt, as it is a gross and fat substance, whereby new fruits are produced; here is another nutrition from an animal into a vegetable. This makes wheat and other fruits and grains grow, producing again a nutriment from the vegetables for animals. Thus, one nature follows after the other, though not so much comprehended or searched into by common <note>vulgar</note> people, who do not care to learn nature's qualities much, which makes such natural things seem incredible.

To return again to the structure of Man, the noblest spirit of life has its dominion and seat for the most part, and most strongly, in the heart of man's body, as in the noble part. And the Sulphur of man gives to that spirit a nutriment and spiritual access for its preservation by the air. For if air is taken away from man, then the spirit of life is choked up, departs invisibly, and death is at hand.

The noble salt spirit is a conserver of both; its noble spirit penetrates throughout. Its grossest matter, salt, is cast into the bladder, and that has a spirit of a special operation. That which goes from the salt through the bladder is worked upon by a heat, and administers a new <unclear>re-</unclear>

As for an example: take the *Salts* from *Minerals*, let them grow again, coagulate, and extract the *Salt* again by water; the same is seen in *nitrous earths* also and other common *Salts*, and there is no need to quote any further examples.

The spirit of life has its process into other members—from the heart into bones, arms, and the rest of the body that are strong. In diseases and symptoms, it is weak, and man, by reason of such symptoms, cannot perform his business with that full strength as at other times. When he is in health, feeding and cherishing his body with *Vegetable spirits* (which come from feeding on bread, meats, and drinking of wines), then his body grows stronger, and his *Vital spirit* grows potent by such nutriments; in its superfluity, it disperses itself into all members and shows its operation. If the heart grows faint, then it is a sign that the *Vital spirit* is not nourished, upon which deadly diseases speedily ensue, because that fire is not at liberty and falls into extinction or choking.

The fire in the heart and the natural heat are preserved and supported by the air; the *Lungs* stand most in need of that air. The *Liver* also must have air, or else it cannot laugh; the *Spleen* must have air, or else it will be oppressed with stitches and great pains. The true seat for the most part of the air is necessary for the *Lungs*; if these fall into any weakness, the cause thereof is because the *Salt* does not show its true and proper help, and must go into rottenness, casting up blood and matter. Then there is at hand a corruption of the air, from which the *Vital spirit* cannot find any true nourishment but must be starved, because the *Salt* does not effect its conserving quality. The substance of Salt, or the Salt spirit, which preserves the body, has its seat for the most part in the bladder, where all humors have their exit; the remaining coarse Salt is separated and excreted by urine, as you heard already. I repeat it here again for that purpose, because the most noble spirit, which preserves man, joins and makes friendship with the Vital spirit and its nutriment, which is Sulphur, and so they make the body perfect. And if any infirmity occurs, either from the operation or defective quality of the Stars, or from a disorderly life in eating and drinking, and many other inconveniences, and any corruption is present, then nature is not in her perfect condition.

Here the knowing Physician must inquire into the cause—from which of these three the symptom arises—and cure the same with suitable remedies, and not with any contrary Medicines. For instance, heat must be cured with heat, cold with cold, and pricking with pricking; for one heat draws the other, one cold draws another, even as iron is drawn by a magnet. And so pricking <term>simples</term> <note>medicinal herbs consisting of only one ingredient</note> may cure stitching diseases, and poisonous minerals can heal and set right poisonous symptoms, if they are duly and well prepared. And although sometimes a cooler may be supplied externally, nevertheless I speak as a Philosopher and one who is experienced in nature: that like must be cured and expelled fundamentally with the like. Otherwise, true medications are not applied, and the physicians do not deal truly in their profession. 

He who is not fundamentally learned in this, or does not observe these things, is not a true Physician; neither can he really say that he has learned any truth in <term>Physick</term> <note>the art or practice of medicine</note>, because he is not able to discern cold and heat, dry and wet. For knowledge and experience, and a fundamental inquiry into nature's mysteries, make a good physician—next to the knowledge of the Creator, from whom all and every wisdom descends, and who is the Author of the beginning, middle, and end of it.

<page-num>8</page-num>
# Of the Microcosm.

Next to the invocation of the Creator, there follow natural means and medicaments, as they are found in themselves in their highest degree. I make no further mention here of other animals; metals and minerals follow next, for in gold, silver, and other metals—even to the seventh and last planet—excellent things are hidden. Mercury is predominant in all, in some more than in others; and minerals also are not without their virtuous medicines, and the former seven planets were in their beginning only minerals.

The tincture of *Sol* <note>the Sun, representing gold</note> together with potable gold and silver are of great efficacy. Mercury rules the microcosm: that which is found in the best metals and most precious stones may be drawn also, if need be, from minerals. For perfect metals are grown, or have their descent, from minerals, such as vitriol, antimony, and the like. Vitriol is sulphur, antimony is mercury; the salt, which is the copula or binder, is found in both. If these are made fixed, they are like the best metals, for they are generated by them. Minerals come from the three principles as well as metals; the three principles come from their *prima materia* <note>first matter</note>, called *primum Ens* <note>first being or essence</note>, which is nothing other than a watery substance found dry; it is not likened to any matter which has grown, and it is preserved by the four elements, and these are cherished or nourished by astrals. The Creator has ordained all these out of nothing, so that man should not gaze only upon earthly matters but consider heavenly ones also, and ought to know supernatural things, so that faith may overtop the rest and have the prerogative in things seen and felt, and be preserved therein.

If physicians do not understand these things, they ought not to be considered physicians; for the knowledge of God and of nature makes a physician, as I have said before, and not great prattling without true knowledge. Good writings by expert men may contribute somewhat to this.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the microcosm and its medicinal applications, specifically focusing on how the human body contains cures for its own ailments, paralleling the alchemical principles found in metals and plants.</meta>

<page-num>9</page-num>
# Of the Microcosm.

In brief, human reason in physicians is not able to comprehend sufficiently, much less are they able to decide, fathom, and fully learn, what manner of medicaments there may be made of the *microcosm* <note>the human body as a reflection of the universe</note>, for he contains a perfect medicine for all diseases; like must be expelled and cured with like. Mercury of the *microcosm* is a living, incomprehensible, and volatile spirit, as I have told.

Man’s balsam dries up a dropsy <note>swelling caused by excess fluid</note>, and the clarified salt of it cures consumptions; in epileptic fits it performs excellently, and being prepared into a fragrant spirit, all corrosiveness being taken from it, is nothing inferior to *aurum potabile* <note>potable gold</note>, to preserve human health; it is very excellent for curing leprosy. Passing by such diseases which are of a lower nature and degree, it breaks the stone in the bladder, and cures all salt rheums, if the artist prepares it well, and knows how to make use of it afterward.

Thus I close with the *microcosm*, contained in few lines; much more could be spoken of this matter, or form, mobility and imagination, how they were brought to perfection. For if these stand together in a true middle, they will make up a sweet harmony: for without the matter, or form of the body, without the moving of the powers, and the defect of perfect thoughts, *Orpheus* will not please the *dolphin* with any harmonious melody: as it is with man, so it is with metals: Mercury is the *mobile* <note>the moving principle</note> in gold, if the body be anatomized: Sulphur is hot; being driven from a mineral and fixed, it dries the phlegmatic *Lune* <note>silver</note>, warms her, and makes her soul equal to himself. In the matter and form there lies a salt, which affords the coagulation of the body: the remainder in the gold put away, for separation will afford a further revelation.

Vegetables also show the form of their three principles: the visible matter contains the vegetable salt, which is its preservative; the fragrance of the vegetable is the balsam, which ministers a nourishment to its perfect growth; the odor, or smell of any herb is of a volatile quality, and

spiritual, and the spirit for the most part shows itself in the fragrance and penetrates the balsam; and its odor, whether it be pleasant or not, is the essence by which men through their senses learn the nature and properties of vegetables. For other things I have written, I praise the Lord, who dwells on high. Thus closing, I wish to everyone the grace and blessing of God, the Creator of all creatures, that they may be blessed, wise, and rich, both in this temporal and corruptible world, and in the other world attain to eternal bliss. *Amen.*

### Of the Mystery of the Microcosm, its Medicinal
### parts belonging to Man,
### written by
# BASILIUS VALENTINUS.

To make use of the heavenly revelation about the two luminaries <note>The Sun and Moon, often representing Gold/Sulphur and Silver/Mercury in alchemy</note>, and of the mysteries of the whole medicine which lies in that marvelous instrument of the *microcosm* <note>the human body</note>, both within and without—that is, in the body and outside it—ordinary wounds, sores, and ulcers that have their cause from within and descend from one root, must nonetheless be separately prepared and dressed. For that which is within is not like that which is without in respect to their operations; but in respect to their form and matter, they are subject to the same judgment. And so that I may rightly inform my fellow Christian, I must acknowledge and confess that there are two medicines which heal all diseases and symptoms, whatever they may be, and are made from one: the one is called <term>PHALAIA</term> and is for internal use; the other is called <term>ASA</term> and is for external cures. Both may be said to be only one; they differ only in their preparation and how both must be brought to their ope-
