...rative quality, the way to it is shown in my *Manuals*. For they must first be rightly known, and their nature must be searched into. Their matter is one, which by that expression I purposely set forth, lest it should be made too common; I, after the manner of *Ancient Philosophers* before me, hiding secrets under dark sentences, hoping by the prayers of others to have their souls saved and received into that Garden in which our first parents were created.

Note, both medicines are made of one matter, as I have already informed you. If used internally, it takes away all manner of infirmities: the matter is putrefied, separated, and in a spagyric <note>An alchemical process involving the separation and recombination of the constituents of a substance</note> way purged in the best manner, and brought to a medicine of the highest degree by fixing its own nature, which must be accomplished in the fire. Its former poisonous volatile quality must be rectified by being prepared to an everlasting fixedness, which expels, purges, and rectifies all malignant spirits, so that a good nature may live quietly in a pure habitation. For this prepared medicine keeps that course: wherever it meets with any malignity, it will be revenged on it and strives to expel it, and will solely keep possession there; for she cannot endure any contrary things about her which are defiled with the least impurity.

**PHALAIA** is the universal medicine to be used internally, and **ASA** is the universal remedy for external uses: it purifies man’s blood, takes away all impurity, strengthens the brain, heart, stomach, and all other parts, produces good blood, strengthens the memory, repairs the defects which have befallen the three principles <note>In alchemy: Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury</note>, and restores all lost things. It is the very key by which the body is opened: for it chases away leprosy, dropsy, consumptions, gout, and all other diseases generally; for no sinful creature has fallen so totally but that she may have a comfort toward salvation in a spiritual way, and a medicine toward health, appointed for that purpose by the Creator, which is obtained if nature be anatomized by an expert artist <note>A skilled alchemist or practitioner</note> to be prepared for that use.

Here I speak of such diseases which some call incurable. For ordinary diseases, there are ordinary means that are not mentioned here; their uses are described in a special treatise.

But regarding my **PHALAIA**, I say this much, according to my long experience: nothing can conceal or hide itself from her. Being a penetrating searcher of all infirmities, she penetrates the body spiritually like a vapor; she penetrates the arteries, muscles, and all parts of the body like a balsam, and restores strength that was lost through her salt spirit. Furthermore, I cannot say enough in praise of my **PHALAIA**, as she is a praise to those who make use of her. He who obtains this **PHALAIA** correctly finds that she is sufficient to cure all diseases. No tongue is able to fully express and set forth her virtues.

Just as diseases differ which affect the body, so there are means for their cure. But this medicine cures all diseases in general, being of a heavenly sidereal <note>Relating to the stars or celestial bodies</note> quality, descended from the elements and generated by the three principles <note>The alchemical principles of Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury</note>. Coming from the very heart of its center, from the whole circumference of the globe, it performs everything, affording to the *Microcosm* <note>The human body viewed as a miniature reflection of the universe</note> a perfect medicine found to be truly real, just as the name implies its virtue. If it is rightly made and prepared, its use will prove it sufficiently.

**ASA** is found in its operation to be for external symptoms, such as old ulcers, fistulas, and cancers, which made many surgeons doubt whether they could ever be cured. But this **ASA** has performed the cure. It consumes all bad blood which had fallen into corruption and may be used internally, because it will then desiccate <note>Dry up</note> and dry up the fountains from which spring all manner of sores, fistulas, cancers, wolves <note>A term for corrosive or eating ulcers</note>, *noli me tangere* <note>Latin for "touch me not"; a name for various corrosive skin diseases or cancers</note>, running legs, worms, and the like, on whatever part of the body they appear. Where plasters, poultices, and the like cannot help and are not strong enough, this alone will do it. For fresh wounds,

After God, be thankful to me, who has taught you inward and outward medicines. Such virtues are not found in foreign woods, drugs, or herbs. It remains effective until the end, preserving a person in health and strength for all the time the Creator has appointed for them. It has triumphantly shown its virtue in many people, obtaining victory against all its enemies, and it was apparent to the world that these two medicines, PHALAIA and ASA, are of one kind and made and prepared from one substance. And it is found daily that, in general, they can restore everything to a perfect state, as being de-

# Of the mystery of the Microcosm,

...descended from the center, can preserve the center as the root, and can bring things to rights, both within and without, tending toward that end for which it is prepared.

Thus I wish the reader, to whom I faithfully disclose these manuals, prosperity and success in its preparation, so that it may be for his health; the work will praise the master. Upon my oath, I further inform you that four things are required to make a perfect philosopher and true physician.

First, he must be persistent and fervent in his devotion to God, as the highest heavenly physician, to ask of Him grace, wisdom, understanding, and His blessing upon his undertakings. This is so it may appear to the world that God grants things for the good of men, and so He may be praised and magnified for such benefits; the physician is also to show himself godly and honest in his life and conduct <note>The original uses "conversation," which in this period referred to one's social conduct or way of life</note>.

Secondly, a physician ought to know the diseases and distinguish one from another, as well as what proper remedies he is to use against these diseases; for without the knowledge of diseases, a physician is not perfect. A person’s complexion <note>Refers to the patient's physical constitution or balance of humors</note> must be discerned, the cause of the disease searched into, and the means well considered, so that no contraries are applied, which would cause further trouble. Proper remedies fitting the disease must be applied, so that restoration may be made to former health.

Thirdly, it is requisite that he frequently read the writings of ancient philosophers, reading them over and over, and take notice of where they concur and agree, and where they all aim at the same mark. Then, he who has understanding will discern the good from the bad, and sophistry from truth. The ancients knew many good things; for my own part, I must confess that I borrowed the foundation of my knowledge from them, which led me to take it to heart. I am thereby moved to leave a cornerstone for others as well, so that truth may be further confirmed, and its grounds may be made easier, clearer, plainer, and more manifest by a further knowledge of my writing.

Fourthly, a philosopher must learn to anatomize things in nature, to know what they contain within and without, and to separate the poison from the medicinal quality. He who has learned all these things may discourse wisely about them and confirm their foundations in truth. But others, who are ashamed to work in this way, cannot boast of any truth, because they are led by the recipes found in other men's writings; they make a trade of other men's writings, without endeavoring to learn more through their own experience. I am not ashamed to learn daily, because nature is profound and endless and cannot be fully comprehended due to the shortness of man's life; no one can say that there is nothing left for him to learn. Not at all. 

Thus you see that God's blessing must be obtained by fervent and frequent prayer to God. The causes of diseases must be known, and their cure must be ordered according to the direction of philosophical writings, while adding experimental knowledge to them. He who does and knows these four things may take pride in his ways, confirming things through deeds rather than carrying out a trade based on other men's recipes. My medicines, if well prepared and duly used, will—by God's help—make it known that they received their strength from God, the marvelous Creator, to perform these things which ignorant people and men of little faith cannot comprehend. Through daily experience, faith gains strength, so that man may praise the Highest, who has put such virtues into natural things, for which mortals are unable to return sufficient thanks. 

As much as lies in my power, I will praise the Lord day and night, for it is not possible to repay Him in any other way. ## Of the Mystery of the Microcosm

<unclear>se</unclear>veral opinions there are about the original causes of metals, minerals, and vegetables; of their qualities and properties; of the origin of man, and of other animals—searching in their imaginations into their lives, virtues, etc. But my son, listen to me, and take notice of what I say: all their pretended sayings are a mere nothing; they speak ignorantly without any certainty because they have no experimental knowledge, having laid no foundation, nor have they learned any true decision in their demonstration. Thoughts pay no custom or toll; they fly into heaven and descend to the lowermost parts of the earth. If experience and their thoughts do not concur, then their thoughts are found to be a mere opinion; then they must confess, "I did not think it could be so!" Man's thoughts are aptly compared to a dream, because nothing follows from an imagination; Nature's secrets must be studied experimentally. If artists or mechanics were to imagine working such or such things—be it watches or other curious metallic works—but do not invent fit instruments whereby to make that work they have in their fancy, what can they produce by that imagination? An empty opinion, and no art. So in the knowledge of natural things, their secrets require a greater exactness to be searched into, which to lazy, inexperienced men seem strange and impossible. I tell you there is required an exact diligence to find that which lies hidden in them; it must be done by separation. Nature must be anatomized; the good and bad in it must be discerned, as well as what is contained in each at its center, for the general, and what comes from it in particular.

Therefore *Macrocosm* and *Microcosm*—yes, and the things which grow and are found therein—are compared to a round circle, in whose middle there is a center. Let the circle be turned which way it will, it remains round every way, and its center remains unmoved. A philosopher also must rightly know the center of each matter, which must stand unmoved in every substance, but the substance may be turned any way he pleases, and make of it seve-

several forms, according to how it received its power from above.

I speak now to be understood in this way: I take any natural thing in hand, dissolve it, or open it with a "Key"—which is the means of unfolding it—and search within it by the test of fire (the master of all tests) to see what may be made of it. Here I find many wonders and qualities that I never imagined, much less experienced.

Powders, oils, water, salt, volatile spirits, and fumes are made from natural things. In these preparations, wonders upon wonders are seen, as witnessed in distillations, digestions, and putrefactions. Many spiritual and physical colors are found and seen there, appearing as black, gray, white, blue, green, yellow, red, or azure, with reflections of all kinds of shimmering colors that cannot easily be described, and which inexperienced men would hardly believe.

From these preparations, various qualities are perceived: one is corrosive and sharp, another is pleasant and mild; one is sour, another is sweet. According to how they are prepared, they yield good or bad results, poison or medicine. For a good thing can be made into the worst poison, and the worst poison can be turned into the best medicine. This is not such a great marvel, because everything depends on the preparation of things. Though not everyone can conceive of it, it is nevertheless true and will remain a truth forever, because nature has manifested herself this way through experience.

A blind man cannot tell the condition of the inner parts of a human body, but the sighted physician who anatomizes the body can judge the location of the heart, brain, liver, lungs, kidneys <note>reins</note>, bladder, and intestines, as well as all the veins, knowing their form and condition. But before he performed this anatomy, all these were hidden from him. Similarly, a miner searching for ores does not know what riches he may expect from metals unless he opens the ore and refines it. By what he finds in it through fire, he can then truly calculate what riches he may expect from it. Other things must be approached in the same way, which true naturalists will endeavor to do, and

do not prate of things only without experimental knowledge, disputing of colors with the blind man; learn to know the ground with your own eyes and hands, which Nature hides within her; then you may speak wisely of them with good reason, and you may build upon an invincible Rock. If you do not do so, then you are but a fantastic prater, whose discourse is grounded on sand without experience, and is soon shaken by every wind, and ruined in the end.

The ground of this knowledge must be learned, as you heard, by anatomizing and separating things, which is made known by distillation: where every Element is separated apart, there it will be made known what is cold, or moist, warm, or dry. There you learn to know the three principles: how the spirit is separated from the body, and how the Oil is separated from the water, and how the Salt is drawn from the <term>Caput mort</term> <note>Caput mortuum, or the "dead head"; the useless remains left after distillation or sublimation</note> of each matter and is reduced again into a spirit, and how these three are afterward joined again, and by fire are brought into one body. Further, it is learned here how each, after its separation and afterward in a conjunction, may profitably and safely be used for the several uses they are prepared for; all of which must be done by a <term>medium</term>.

At the first Creation, man is earthy and gross, but his Soul, Spirit, and Body, being separated by death, putrefies under the ground; and when the Highest comes to judgment, he is raised again. His Body, Soul, and spirit come together, according to Faith and the Scriptures; that body is no more earthy as it was formerly, but is found heavenly and clarified, glittering as the Stars in the East, and is seen like the Sun when all the clouds are past. So it is here: when earthiness is broken, divided, and separated, then the three principles of the dead substance are made apparent. The dead one is forsaken, and the living power comes to its perfection because its obstruction is set aside, so that the virtue in the operation may be manifested. In this separation and manifestation, it is then known what these three principles are which are so much discussed: namely, <term>Mercury</term>, <term>Sulphur</term>, and <term>Salt</term>, according to the condition of

If anyone should intend to teach me otherwise with a long-windedness of words, he may fill me with words, but he must prove it in reality also; for without that, I am not bound to believe his words, but I desire some sign as *Thomas*, one of the twelve, who looked for an *ocular demonstration*. I might have left out *Thomas*, but since there is a rift between a *spiritual* and a *worldly* unbeliever, I gave liberty to my mind to speak it; for there is a great difference between *heavenly* and *worldly* matters regarding faith and things comprehensible, and that difference is also found in *sidereal* and *earthly* things. For *sidereal* things are comprehended by sharp imagination and arithmetic rules, but for the discovery of earthly things, there belong *speculation* and *separation*. With *speculation* must be joined an *intention*, and an *apprehension* is annexed to *speculation*; the former is done spiritually, because the spirit of man does not rest, but desires to apprehend more qualities of the spirit in natural things; every spirit always draws its like. The rest is *earthy*; for an *earthy* body separates by *manuals* <note>Physical tasks or laboratory operations</note> the *earthy* body from the *spiritual* part, and so the one may be discerned before and from the other. Since the *soul* in both shows herself in reality, she is therefore in all things truly, for she ties the *heavenly* and *earthy* together like a bond; but when the *heavenly* is separated from the *earthy*, so that the *soul* also must forsake her body, then you have separated and received the *three*, each apart, which after a true knowledge and conjunction can afford such a triumphant and clarified body, which is found to be many thousand times better, because the grossest part is set aside from the earthy.

For when heaven and earth come to be refined by the great *Creator*, then the greatest part will be consumed by *fire*, and by that purging it will be exalted to the same degree as the heavenly and set into the same alignment; for everything is created by one, everything is ordained by one, and though

Although through sin by one man all was corrupted unto death, yet all is brought to a better state of life by one. The only Creator intends to judge all by fire, and all must again become one, which will be that heavenly essence to which the earthly gave way by means of the fire. The eternal glory leaves a place for the devil and death, from where they shall look upon the elect, admiring the great majesty and glory of God, who, in a divine essence of three distinct persons, is all in all and has created all.

Thus the three persons in the <term>Deity</term> have displayed their invisible essence in us, giving us thereby to understand—by an unsearchable wisdom—what their creature and order is. We men are too weak to ascend higher; God is and will be God, and we men must be content with such gifts afforded to us. Hereafter shall be accomplished that which is prophesied by Prophets and Apostles, and which is now conceived of only by way of faith; therefore we ought now to be contented with what is suggested by <term>Nature</term> in a visible way. Other things incomprehensible to us, and matters of faith, will appear better understood at the end of the world. God grant to us all a true knowledge of temporal goods and of the eternal.

At the closing of this, I say that this is the whole Art and whole foundation of all <term>Philosophic</term> speech, in which that is sought which many desire—taking great pains and incurring great expenses—namely to obtain wisdom and judgment, a long life, health, and the riches of this world, all comprehended in few words. For example: first, you must know that I will show you such an example from the animals which, in appearance, is a humble and poor one, but of a mighty consequence if rightly considered. The hen lays an egg; that same egg is brought to a hardness or <term>coagulation</term> by heat. By further heat, it is brought to a <term>putrefaction</term>, where it is corrupted. In this <term>putrefaction</term>, the egg receives a new <term>Genus</term>, wherein a new life is raised and a chicken is hatched. This chicken being perfect, the shell opens, making way for the chicken to creep forth. This chicken, coming to a further ripeness and age, increases further in its kind. Thus <term>Nature</term>

<meta>This page continues the explanation of the generation of life, transitioning from the example of an egg to the reproduction of humans and plants, emphasizing the role of seeds, heat, and "putrefaction" in the creation of new forms.</meta>

<page-num>21</page-num>

<sig>X 3</sig>

...furthers her own kind, and augments *usque ad infinitum*. True, the egg is not the *prima materia* of the Cock or Hen, but the *prima materia* of their flesh is the first seed, out of which the egg has taken a form, which by the equal nature of the motion of both is driven together and united; from there, by a further heat, it went to a putrefaction, and from there into a new birth, which new birth still propagates and increases.

So it is with man, for one man alone cannot produce a new birth unless both seeds of male and female be united; for after this conjunction through the nutriment of the body and the continued natural heat of these two seeds—which in the center are known as one nature—they get a new life, and more men are begotten, who propagate further by their seed; by this means the whole world is filled with men. This seed of man is the noblest and subtlest blood of a white quality, in which dwells the vital spirit, which is driven together by motion. If these seeds of both kinds are united together by their desire of lust, and their natures are not corrupted or else contrary to one another, then a life is preserved by heat and brought to perfection in the mother's womb, and another man is brought forth. Thus much may be said of the seed of animals.

The vegetable seed is made palpable and visible, which from each kind of herb is separated and propagated in the earth for an increase; this seed must first putrefy in the earth, and then must be nourished by a temperate moisture; at last, this seed is brought to perfection by a convenient warm air. Thus vegetables are increased and preserved in their kind; but the first beginning of a vegetable seed is a spiritual essence or <term>astral influence</term>, by which an imagination was conceived in the earth, and it became impregnated with a matter out of which, by the help of the elements, it came to be something. Whatever form of seed the earth desired after the heavenly impression, that form it received first and brought it to a kind, which brings further increase by its palpable seed in the generation; by this, man may try his further skill, but he is not able to create a new seed, as Nature

<summary>This page describes the biological and spiritual processes of generation in animals, humans, and plants, highlighting the necessity of united seeds, heat, and "putrefaction." It posits that while seeds lead to physical growth, their ultimate origin is a spiritual essence or "astral influence" acting upon the earth.</summary>

<keywords>generation, seeds, prima materia, putrefaction, human reproduction, vegetable seed, astral influence, vital spirit, natural philosophy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the generation of life from the previous page, shifting from the growth of plants to the divine origins and composition of metals and minerals, using the Trinity as a model for the three principles of matter.</meta>

<page-num>22</page-num>
<header>Of the mystery of Microcosm.</header>

...does; only by an influence from above is it able to increase in a formed seed.

Of Metals and Minerals, I inform you of this: that there is only one Almighty Being, which is from eternity and abides unto eternity, who is the Creator of heaven and earth, namely the eternal Deity in three distinct persons; these three in the Deity are a perfect divine being. And though I confess and acknowledge these three persons, yet I confess only one God in one Being. I speak of this now as a *Type* <note>A symbolic representation or foreshadowing</note> of the first seed of the three principles: that the first beginning to beget metallic seeds is wrought in the earth by a sidereal impression, which quality presses from above into the nether parts, as in the belly of the earth, and continually works a heat therein with the help of the Elements; for both must be together. The earthy part provides an imagination, so that the earth is fitted for conception and is impregnated; the Elements nourish and feed this fruit, bringing it on by a continued hot quality unto perfection; and the earthy substance provides a form for it. Thus, at the beginning, the metallic and mineral seed is effected—namely, by an astral imagination, elemental operation, and terrestrial form. The astral is heavenly, the elemental is spiritual, and the earthy is corporeal; these three make from their first Center the first essence of the metallic seed. 

Philosophers have further searched into this, finding that out of this essence there has become a form of metallic matter, palpably joined together by three parts: a metallic Sulphur (heavenly), a metallic Mercury (spiritual), and a metallic salt (bodily). These three are found at the opening of metals, for metals and minerals must be broken and opened. Minerals are of the same kinship, of the same quality and nature as metals are, only they are not sufficiently ripened unto coagulation; they may be recognized as unripe metals, for the spirit in them is found to be as powerfully metallic as it is in the most perfect metals. For metals may be destroyed and easily reduced unto minerals, and from minerals are prepared medicaments which ripen and transmute metals, which must be noted; and it is done when Spirit,

<summary>The author explains that metals and minerals are formed in the earth through the combined influence of sidereal impressions, elemental nourishment, and terrestrial form. This process mirrors the Trinity and results in the three alchemical principles: metallic sulphur, mercury, and salt.</summary>

<keywords>metallurgy, alchemy, Trinity, sidereal impression, Sulphur, Mercury, Salt, transmutation, minerals, Microcosm</keywords>

--- Page 349 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion from the previous page regarding the three principles of matter. While page 22 introduced the metallic seeds as being formed by astral and elemental influences, this page concludes the work by explaining the reunion of these principles into a medicine and offering a final prayer to the Divine.</meta>

<page-num>23</page-num>
<header>its Medicinal parts, &c.</header>

Soul and Body are separated and purely reunited. The remaining earthliness being cast off, then follows a perfect birth, and the perfect ripening by heat performs its office, so that Spirit, Soul, and Body—which at the beginning in their first seed were a heavenly water—begat these three. Out of these three have become a <term>metallic Sulphur</term>, a <term>metallic Mercury</term>, and a <term>metallic Salt</term>; these in their conjunction made a fixed, visible, palpable body. First it began as a mineral one, then a metallic one by an <term>astral imagination</term>, digested and ripened by the elements, and by an earthy substance are made formal and material.

Now when these bodies of minerals and metals are reduced to their first beginning, then the heavenly seed appears and is spiritual; this spiritual part must become an earthy one by the union of the Soul, which is the *medium* and middle bond of their union, to make a medicine out of it. By this is obtained health, long life, wisdom, and riches in this mortal life: this is the true <term>seed of philosophers</term> <note>sperme of Philosophers</note>, long sought after but not known. Its light was desired by many to be seen, and it is even the <term>first matter</term>, which lies open before the eyes of all the world—though few men know it—it is found visibly in all places: namely Mercury, Sulphur, and Salt, and a mineral water or metallic liquor, as the Center, separated from its form and made by these <term>three principles</term>.

The <term>Heavenly Physician</term> <note>Phisician</note>, the eternal Creator and inexhaustible fountain of Grace, and the Father of all wisdom—Father, Son, and Holy Ghost in one Deity—teach us to truly know His wondrous works with proper gratitude, and make us co-heirs of His everlasting goods. Thus, after a temporal revelation, we may seek for heavenly treasures in a true light and may possess them eternally with all the elect, where there is unspeakable glory without end; which is attained by faith in our Savior, by bringing forth good fruit, by loving our neighbors, and helping the needy, which must be made evident with a blameless life and due obedience to God. *Amen.*
